# PREFACE

**Eliseus Pletenetsky,[^4] Archimandrite of the Caves of Kyiv,[^5] unto the Orthodox Russian Race,[^6] the most beloved sons of the Eastern Church: soul-saving health!**

Even as, therefore, according unto the grace given unto me, over those entrusted unto me within the monastery of the Holy Caves Lavra, both inwardly and outwardly, the Lord working together with me, I labor, and in zeal for the piety of the Eastern Church do abide: even so, with soul-profiting works, to gladden the hearts of her orthodox sons, and in orthodoxy to confirm them, and to adorn them, did it seem good [to provide] ecclesi- astical books. And at the beginning I deemed it meet to send forth a small book as a forerunner,[^7] that it might prepare the way for greater ones; for neither does the day dawn without its forerunner, the morning star. And nothing did I judge more fitting than this prayer book, named the *Horologion*, wherein for ordinary and fasting days God-inspired prayers are found. And for this reason also, that from the Holy Caves Monastery—which has blossomed forth with the beauty of prayer and fasting unto humility, to such a degree that wonderworking saints were made manifest therein, and unto this day many abide in the incorruption of their bodies—by those very virtues whereby she has blossomed into so great a flower, with these it was fitting to greet you, that ye likewise by these two virtues might blossom unto the glory of God. Truly great is the power of this book, that is, of prayer; for this is the morning star that enlightens souls and disposes the faithful to converse with God. And thereby it is possible to escape visible and invisible enemies, and to be delivered from present afflictions. For King Hezekiah, it is said, and the Prophet Isaiah prayed and cried aloud even unto heaven, and the Lord sent His angel, who smote all the mighty men of valor and the warriors and the leaders of the hosts in the camp of the King of Assyria.[^8] And again, King Manasseh prayed diligently in Babylon, and the Lord heard his prayer and his cry, and restored him unto Jerusalem unto his kingdom;[^9]

...his kingdom. And it is delivered down in remembrance by Africanus,[^10] that when Manasseh made prayer, his iron bonds were rent asunder, and he obtained for himself all health. And let no one say: "Seeing that already not once, nor from a single printing press has this book gone forth, why then this one?" Good merchandise is ever in the marketplace; necessary sustenance is continually in the shambles; and an instrument wherewith we frequently make use, the craftsman ever constructs.[^11] Ought it not all the more so to be with prayer, the spiritual instrument? Which is a continual work, and greater labor lies therein, even as Abba Agathon said: "Every way of life that a man undertakes has a guard therein, acquiring rest; but as for prayer, until his last breath it requires labor."[^12] In addition unto these things, I was also entreated by the orthodox, that we might fulfill the need which is in the school in the Orthodox City of Kyiv,[^13] and among the rest. Even so was it pleasing unto us, when by God's help we purposed to publish books unto the world, that it was not fitting to begin otherwise than from prayer, which surpasses all things. Therefore, as a single blessing from our venerable Caves Fathers, receive ye lovingly this book of greeting, hoping, if the Lord will, shortly to be deemed worthy of embracing others also; and pray to God One in Trinity, that He may prosper what we have devised through the typographical craft to please the Orthodox Churches present, and committing the undertaking that is to be unto the judgment and correction of the Eastern Church. Fare ye well in the Lord!

## FOREWORD TO THE HOROLOGION[^14]

*All flesh shall come to worship before Me in Jerusalem, says the Lord.* (Isa. 66:23)[^15]

Lo, orthodox Christian, and every devout reader: from the renowned Kyivan sites in Rus', that is, from the Caves Lavra of Kyiv, by the grace of God, and by the diligence and care of the Most Venerable among the Fathers, Kyr Eliseus Pletenetsky,[^16] Archimandrite of the Caves of Kyiv, there comes forth printed the prayer book *Horologion*, which is interpreted in Slavonic as the *Chasoslov*; containing within itself the day and night prayers, arranged according to the order of the Church of Jerusalem and of the Lavra of Saint Sabas the Sanctified, archimandrite thereof.[^17] For, lo, it is meet to follow the dogma, typikon, and order of the Eastern Church. And this we do as in acceptable comeliness. For when we pray, we turn our eyes unto the east, where our fatherland of Paradise is, concerning which it is written: *And God planted Paradise eastward in Eden;* (Gen. 2:8)[^18] unto which God, disposing us ever to yearn, commanded Moses to turn the Tabernacle toward the east; and the tribe of Judah pitched their tents toward the east; (Num. 2:3)[^19] for from thence also do we behold the sun enlightening the universe. Therefore, it is lawful for us to receive from the east piety with all ecclesiastical good order, and to embrace it. And all the more so, since the righteous throne of Jesus Christ our Savior upholds the Church of Jerusalem; from thence, as formerly from Mount Sinai, the world received the covenant of the new grace; from thence, when God was incarnate and dwelt with men, (Bar. 3:38)[^20] the human race attained unto its salvation; from thence the universe manifestly learned the mysteries of the Tri-hypostatic Godhead; and, to speak simply: from thence, as from an ever-flowing fount according unto the God-speaking Prophet: *For out of Zion shall go forth the law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem;* (Isa. 2:3)[^21] and into all the world the rivers of theology are poured forth, nor do they fail in flowing from thence. From that Church Christ commanded the word of grace to begin, as Luke says: *and that repentance and remission of sins should be preached in His name among all nations, beginning at Jerusalem.* (Lk. 24:47)[^22] In that Church the Apostles, having been filled with the Holy Spirit, went forth to confirm the universe; and unto her they turned again, and assembled together; and diverse discords and the upspringing of heresies, by divine counsel and judgment, as with a sword, they cut off from the fullness of the Church; sending forth from Jerusalem dogmas and injunctions unto the Churches of Christ they delivered, and transmitted them to be kept. And that Church of Jerusalem alone remained pure from heresy, for the Arians (as Hermias Sozomen testifies) did not enter into her.[^23] That Church is the Mother of Churches, according unto the Prophet: *A man will say, "Mother Zion," and a man was born in her, and the Highest Himself has established her;* (Ps. 86:5)[^24] and according unto Isaiah, who said: *And afterward thou shalt be called the righteous city, Mother of faithful cities, Zion;* (Isa. 1:26)[^25] and Damascene: *Rejoice, O holy Zion, Mother of Churches, dwelling-place of God; for thou first didst receive remission of sins on account of the Resurrection;*[^26] and the Second Ecumenical Council, in its synodical letter, calls the Church of Jerusalem the Mother of all Churches, speaking thus:[^27]

"Thus: And of the Mother of all Churches, which is in Jerusalem, we make known unto you that the most honorable and most God-beloved Cyril is bishop."[^28] From that Church of Jerusalem, the Mother of Churches, the foremost among the Apostles, Andrew, after the apportioning among the nations, having enlightened the Thracian lands with the light of the Gospel, and in the Thracian city of Byzantium, which is now Constantinople, having laid firm foundations of faith; and having been the first Patriarch there; traversing the ends of the universe, he reached the hills of Kyiv,[^29] and by the erection of the sign of the life-creating Cross, having put to shame the aerial adversaries,[^30] prophetically [proclaimed] that the readers of the true faith of Christ should dwell there, and that unfeigned worshipers of God in Trinity should abide and foreseeing this from afar by many ages, he prophesied it; which also in our Russian race, in the times of the Sovereign Vladimir—who was named a second Constantine Equal-to-the-Apostles—by the grace of Christ was radiantly fulfilled.[^31] When, filling the place of the fallen through ecclesiastical deprivation, and [coming into] light (the darkened stars), as a most radiant dawn with his peoples, he was abundantly deemed worthy of holy Baptism; and was numbered among those forechosen by God, in these latter times, with all his host, being illumined with the light of piety from the east and from Jerusalem; receiving all ecclesiastical traditions, order, and administration, as a patristic heritage from the Church of Constantinople Church and her Patriarchs, he inherited; and concerning this, the Rus'ians of that time and of future times, rendering submission and obedience unto him and to no other Patriarch, as unto their head and mother. This Church Emperor Justinian confesses to be the head of all Churches.[^32] And the August Emperors Leo and Anthemius confess her to be the mother of all Orthodox Christians, and sanction her tradition by law.[^33] Unto that same Patriarch, even unto this day, the wonderworking Lavra of the Monastery of the Caves, guarded by God, with all its dwellers, according to the order of the Patriarchal Stauropegion, has been subject from of old, and yields obedience.[^34]

And as for what pertains to the order and spiritual understanding of the prayers set forth in this book, I have judged good to write briefly, that there may be an answer unto heretics, and unto the Orthodox a warmer zeal for spiritual labors of prayer. According to the apostolic teaching, we are bound to pray without ceasing, as he says: *Rejoice evermore. Pray without ceasing. In every thing give thanks: for this is the will of God in Christ Jesus concerning you.* (1 Thess. 5:16–18)[^35] And to fulfill this is possible, even as Basil the Great teaches: Sometimes with the tongue, when this is possible, and especially necessary, and unto the edification of faith;[^36] but if not, then with the heart, praising God in psalms, and hymns, and spiritual songs, as it is written, singing and fulfilling prayer in the midst of work.[^37] Nevertheless, since it is commanded to pray without ceasing, it is not fitting to despise (as that same father said) the appointed times of prayer, which we have chosen by night, each having its own particular remembrance.[^38]

*At midnight* we begin our prayers, returning from sleep as from the darkness akin to death; and as having risen from the dead, we hymn God, preparing ourselves beforehand for the morning. And since the Midnight prayers are placed first, let no one marvel; for not without reason did the Church transmit that they should begin from thence, especially for two reasons.

The first, because the day has its beginning from midnight. Even if Chrysostom says that the day is counted from the evening according to the Hebrew tradition,[^39] yet Theophylact says that from the eighth hour of the night, and Saint Theodore Balsamon, Patriarch of Antioch, says that the day begins from the seventh hour of the night;[^40] which signifies nothing other than midnight. Outside philosophers also, such as Plutarch and Macrobius, testify that the day begins from midnight.[^41] And thus we do not begin the first prayers, called the Midnight Office, as from the night, but as from the light which God called day, as Moses says: *And God called the light Day, and the darkness He called Night.* (Gen. 1:5)[^42] For God also created day first, as Basil in the *Hexaemeron* testifies thus: *And God said, Let there be light:* The first voice of God (he says) created the nature of light, drove away the darkness, and enlightened the world.[^43]

And Athanasius [speaks] thus: In that God said before all things, *Let there be light*, it is manifest that the day precedes the night.[^44] And Damascene: Neither was night called first, but day; and on this account day was first, and night thereafter.[^45] And thus when we begin prayers from midnight, we consider that we have begun from the day, because midnight is the beginning of the day; and those first [prayers] we understand as proper. For it is nocturnal unto us at that time not to be weighed down with sleep, but as the day is beginning, it is meet also for our mind to be stirred toward its Creator, and equally with the sun for the mind to ascend: the one to enlighten the universe, and the eye to soar on high unto the Lord, that it may be deemed worthy of enlightenment, and for the adverse darkness to be driven away.

The second [reason]: Because toward midnight we are assured of the Second Coming of Christ and the general resurrection, even as the Gospel truth testifies: *At midnight,* it says, *there was a cry made, Behold, the bridegroom comes; go ye out to meet him.* (Mt. 25:6)[^46] *Behold, He comes* [signifies] the Coming; and *Go ye out* manifests our resurrection, even as the divine Chrysostom, interpreting these things, says: Either it remained in a parable, or again it shows that the resurrection takes place in the night.[^47] Furthermore, He Himself, the Firstborn from the dead, having risen at midnight, manifestly prefigured this; following whom the divine Fathers of the Sixth Ecumenical Council and Theophylact the church teacher clearly teach us that Christ arose from the dead toward midnight.[^48] And to pray at midnight we have many indications, even as the narrative of the Acts presents, saying: *And at midnight Paul and Silas prayed, and sang praises unto God.* (Acts 16:25)[^49] And the Psalmist also says: *At midnight I arose to give thanks unto Thee because of the judgments of Thy righteousness.* (Ps. 118:62)[^50] And in another [psalm]: *In the nights lift up your hands unto the holies, and bless the Lord.* (Ps. 133:2)[^51] And Christ commands: *Watch ye therefore: for ye know not when the master of the house comes, at even, or at midnight, or at the cockcrowing, or in the morning: lest coming suddenly He find you sleeping.* (Mk. 13:35–36)[^52] And keeping vigil and sending up midnight prayers unto God, let us instruct our mind: that Christ after the supper went unto the garden, according to Chrysostom: For at midnight (he says) the Lord journeys, passes over the brook of Cedron, and hastens unto the place known to the betrayer.[^53] At midnight, as He prayed there fervently and was in an agony, His sweat fell down to the ground as drops of blood. (Lk. 22:44)[^54] At midnight He was taken by the soldiers. (Jn. 18:12)[^55] At midnight He was led first unto Annas, and afterward unto Caiaphas; and whatsoever things He, the Sinless One, voluntarily endured there for the sake of our salvation. And meditating upon these things in contrition of heart, we perform the midnight supplications not idly nor unfruitfully.

*The Matins prayers* we perform anticipating the day, that we may not on our bed sleeping be overtaken by the day, according to him that said: *Mine eyes have anticipated the morning dawn, that I might meditate on Thy words.* (Ps. 118:148)[^56] And in another Psalm: *In the morning my prayer shall anticipate Thee.* (Ps. 87:14)[^57] And (according to Basil) to receive nothing else into our care before the remembrance rejoices [in God], according to what is written: *I remembered God, and was glad.* (Ps. 76:4)[^58] And at that time we remember that Christ stood before Caiaphas and his council, receiving mockings and unrighteous examinations; even as John Chrysostom testifies, that from midnight even unto morning, Christ was examined by Caiaphas, being in nothing overcome.[^59]

*At the First Hour* of the day we perform prayers, not giving our bodies unto work before we do that which was spoken: *For unto Thee will I pray, O Lord; in the morning Thou shalt hear my voice; in the morning will I stand before Thee, and Thou shalt look upon me;* (Ps. 5:4–5)[^60] praying that *the light of Thy countenance may be signed upon us, O Lord;* and that He may direct our steps unto work; saying with David: *And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us; and establish Thou the works of our hands upon us, yea, the work of our hands establish Thou.* (Ps. 89:17)[^61] And in all these things we remember that at that hour our Savior was led into the praetorium unto Pilate, (Mt. 27:2; Jn. 18:28)[^62] and before his judgment the Judge of all the world stood; and we meditate upon all that He suffered there.

*At the Third Hour* we perform prayers, at which Basil commands the brethren to be gathered, even if they happen to be divided unto different tasks.[^63]

At that same hour we remember the Descent of the Holy Spirit, and the bestowing of spiritual gifts upon the Apostles; (Acts 2:15)[^64] praying that we may be worthy of receiving Sanctification, and asking of Him guidance and instruction unto what is profitable, according to him that said: *Create in me a clean heart, O God; and renew a right spirit within me. Cast me not away from Thy presence; and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me. Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation, and establish me with Thy governing Spirit.* (Ps. 50:12–14)[^65] And: *Thy good Spirit shall lead me into the land of uprightness.* (Ps. 142:10)[^66] Furthermore, we remember that at the third hour Pilate judged Christ to be crucified upon the Cross; and the painful sufferings which He received there, with a contrite heart.[^67]

*At the Sixth Hour* we are bound to pray, as David says: *Evening, and morning, and at noon, will I declare and tell; and He shall hear my voice;* (Ps. 54:18)[^68] and that we may be delivered from the mishap and the demon of noonday. (Ps. 90:6)[^69] And Daniel three times [a day] was kneeling upon his knees, praying and confessing before his God. (Dan. 6:10)[^70] And we remember that at the sixth hour Christ hung upon the Cross for our sins, saying with the Prophet: *He bears our sins, and suffers for us;* and that same: *He was wounded for our sins, and was tormented for our iniquities; the chastisement of our peace was upon Him; and with His stripe we are healed.* (Isa. 53:4–5)[^71]

*At the Ninth Hour* to pray, from the very [Apostles] unto us from the very Apostles in the Acts it has been transmitted, narrating that Peter and John went up into the temple at the hour of prayer, being the ninth hour. (Acts 3:1)[^72] And we remember that at that time Christ gave up the ghost, and until evening remained crucified upon the Cross, being ever-living: He died in the flesh that we who were dead might live unto ages; calculating the great grace and love of Jesus; for in truth (as John says) *Hereby perceive we the love of God, because He laid down His life for us: and we ought to lay down our lives for the brethren.* (1 Jn. 3:16)[^73] And all the more in this present time of persecution, fearing nothing, but having ever in mind what was spoken by the Prophet: *For Thy sake are we killed all the day long;* (Ps. 43:23)[^74] *we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter.* (Ps. 43:23; Rom. 8:36)[^75] Again we remember with tears that beside the Cross stood the Mother of Jesus, Mary, and John His beloved bosom-disciple, and all His acquaintances, and many women from Galilee and Jerusalem; (Jn. 19:25; Lk. 23:49)[^76] and how weeping they looked upon our crucified Savior, sweetest Jesus.

*The Vespers prayers* we perform not as first before the others, even though from evening is the beginning for saints and feasts, nor is evening prior to morning and day, even though in Genesis it says: *And the evening and the morning were one day.* (Gen. 1:5)[^77] For evening (as Basil says in the *Hexaemeron*) is the designation of day and night, or a common boundary of both; and again, evening is the end of the day, according to Basil, and let (he says) the pre-eminence of the first creation be granted unto the day; the end of that day he remembers here first.[^78] Nor because David says: *The lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice,* (Ps. 140:2)[^79] art thou bound to deem the evening prayers prior or higher; for he says this in the Psalm (as Basil interpreted it): It was a law with the priests, that from their own and of themselves, even if no one else should bring [an offering], they should offer one lamb in the morning, and another in the evening; the one they called the morning lamb, and the other the evening lamb; God revealing that He wishes to be worshiped ever at the beginning and at the end of the day;[^80] (and others [say] thus:) that the morning sacrifice was not complete, whereas the evening had the completed service. Then the evening prayers are the completion of the prayers of night and day; while for saints and feasts occurring on the morrow, it becomes in part a beginning. And these prayers called evening prayers we perform when the day has ended, and having seen the evening light, we hymn God, one in Trinity: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit;[^81] and furthermore, rendering thanksgiving unto our Benefactor God, our Creator, for the gifts given in the day and our uncorrected shortcomings. And we remember and meditate that at the evening hour one of the soldiers with a spear pierced His side, and forthwith came there out blood and water; (Jn. 19:34)[^82] and that Joseph and Nicodemus, having taken Christ down from the Cross, wound Him in linen clothes, and laid Him in a sepulchre; (Jn. 19:38–40)[^83] and that Mary Magdalene and Mary the mother of Joses beheld where they laid Him. (Mk. 15:47)[^84] Again we reflect that Christ at late evening, that is, on the evening of the day of His Resurrection, when the doors were shut, stood in the midst of the disciples, and showed them His hands and His feet and His side, giving peace unto them. (Jn. 20:19–20)[^85]

And after these things, at last we perform the prayers called the Compline prayers, that is to say, after-evening prayers. And as the night begins, sending up supplication unto God that our sleep may be without stumbling and free from fantasies, and asking the Guardian Angel to preserve us from the snares of demons;[^86] and moreover, according to the tradition of the fathers, we make confession of all our sins committed either in word, or in deed, or in the very heart; which [fathers] teach concerning confession; as Basil the Great (at the end of the homily whose beginning is: *Man was created according to the image and likeness of God*) teaches us thus: Keep not hidden the audacious transgression in any wise, but confess it in the midst of all that hear, that through the common prayer his infirmity may be healed, and all evil fall away.[^87] And we reflect in ourselves that then at late evening, at the time of Compline, they sat over against the sepulchre of Jesus; as Matthew says: *And there was Mary Magdalene, and the other Mary, sitting over against the sepulchre.* (Mt. 27:61)[^88] And others, having returned, prepared spices and ointments, (Lk. 23:56)[^89] thinking quickly to see Christ and anoint Him.

Nor do I deem it unprofitable to set down here concerning the great prostrations, which we, sons of the Eastern Church, perform, falling upon our knees, striking the brow to the ground, and making worship unto God with all the members of our flesh.[^90] For such a prostration is as the whole burnt offering in the Old Law; for the whole calf was burnt unto the praise of God. After such a manner in ancient times also did they worship God: *Moses made haste,* it says, *and bowed his head toward the earth, and worshiped God.* (Ex. 34:8)[^91] And Joshua the son of Nun did the same at length: *And he fell,* it says, *to the earth upon his face before God, lying until eventide, he and the elders of Israel.* (Josh. 7:6)[^92] Inasmuch as if in such manner we bow also unto men, we sin not; for this is an ancient custom, and even now it exists among those that dwell in the East; even as we find in Scripture: Joseph bowed himself with his face to the earth before his father. (Gen. 48:12)[^93] Again, concerning the Shunammite woman it is written: *And the woman went in, and fell at Eliseus' feet, and bowed herself to the ground.* (4 Kgs. 4:37)[^94] And Jacob, not having made a bow once only, bowed unto his brother Esau: *Jacob himself,* it says, *passed over before them, and bowed himself to the ground seven times, until he came near to his brother.* (Gen. 33:3)[^95] How much more is it meet to make more abundant prostrations unto God: that is, falling prostrate and rising up again; even as we read concerning Daniel, who three times a day was kneeling upon his knees, praying unto God. (Dan. 6:10)[^96] And in the Gospels Luke writes: *A man full of leprosy, seeing Jesus, fell on his face and besought Him.* (Lk. 5:12)[^97] But as for those who make a bow with the foot, nor bow their head, only bending the knees a little, nowhere is such a custom found, not only unto God but not even unto men, except for the soldiers of Pilate, who, bending their knees, mocked Christ.[^98] But it is meet to worship God, as being the most sovereign of all, with the most sovereign member in the body, that is, with the head, in which are the higher powers of the soul and the mind. Moreover, Christ Himself, as Matthew testifies, *fell upon His face, praying.* (Mt. 26:39)[^99] And Luke: *When He was praying earnestly,* he says, *His sweat became as drops of blood falling down* (Lk. 22:44)[^100] *upon the ground.* (Lk. 22:44)[^101] For it could not be (preserving the divine dispensation whole) that the sweat should become as drops of blood falling down, had He not wearied His body by falling down and rising up frequently. It is written also concerning the Most Pure Birthgiver of God, that where she made prostrations upon a marble stone, even unto this day the marks exist, that is, depressions from her holy hands, head, and knees.[^102] Sozomen also testifies concerning James, the Brother of the Lord, the first bishop of Jerusalem,[^103] that he gave himself over unto so great labors of prayer that all his members were mortified, and his brow, from continual prostrations for help, became so hardened that it was in no wise better than the knees of a camel.[^104]

And Cassian the Roman, who visited all the monasteries and sketes in the eastern lands, testifies concerning the Egyptian and Thebaid monks, that they made prostrations falling down unto the ground and rising up again; in book 2, chapter 17, and in book 8, chapter 58.[^105] And that the Church maintains praying while standing, David also prophesied: *Behold now, bless the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord, who stand in the house of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God.* (Ps. 133:1-2)[^106] And Christ revealed that it is acceptable to pray standing, saying: *When ye stand praying, forgive.* (Mk. 11:25)[^107] Nor without reasoned word and mystery did the Eastern Church teach to pray standing, and falling prostrate; witness hereof is Basil the Great, writing to Amphilochius, bishop of Iconium, in the book *On the Holy Spirit.*[^108] He discloses that mystery in these words: *Necessarily on that day, that is to say on Sunday, the Church teaches her children to offer their prayers standing, that by continual remembrance of that never-ending life we may not neglect to prepare provision for our journey thither.*[^109] *Moreover, the entire period until Pentecost is a remembrance of the resurrection which we await in that age.* (And below thus:) *The ecclesiastical ordinances have taught us to pray in an upright posture of the body, as though transferring our mind from things present unto things to come.*[^110] And concerning prostrations, the same Father in the same place speaks thus: *Furthermore, as often as we bow the knees*[^111] *and rise up again, we show by our deed that through sin we fell down unto the earth, and that by the love of Him Who made us toward mankind we have been summoned unto heaven.* Thus far Basil.[^112] And so, O Christian, behold here that Basil the Great, the eye and pillar of the Church, showed not only these things in that tradition, but also the other manner with the concealment of mystery: twofold, that from antiquity to pray standing, and to make prostrations falling down and rising up, the Church maintains; and twofold, what mystery there is when we pray standing, and what is the mystery of the prostrations performed by falling down and rising up.[^113] And so, O orthodox Christian, thou hast the order of the figured prayers, concerning which, according to Basil, at no time of prayer, neither by day nor by night, should one flee away; even if they find themselves living far off anywhere by reason of business or local circumstance, they are bound of necessity to fulfill each thing there which is commanded in common, causing no division.[^114] In this it is evident that neither for the sake of a journey, nor urgent business, nor for hospitality is it permitted to leave off the rule;[^115] nor does any foreign law give release on account of necessity or befalling affliction, but constrains to fulfill all things lawfully instituted.[^116] Therefore, hearing and seeing such an orderly rule of prayer transmitted from the East, make haste to fulfill and keep it in a manner pleasing unto God; and unto the Church of Constantinople, her Mother, unto whom we of Rus' are subject, unalterably to submit; which in all things accords with the first-born Jerusalem Church,[^117] having only this difference between themselves, which is by local circumstance; not easily turning aside nor moving over unto others walking contrariwise; lest falling away from the piety handed down from the beginning by the fathers, we be deprived of the hope of salvation, and inherit the condemnation of transgressors of the law.[^118] And from this may Master Christ preserve us; but through the customary prayers according to the tradition of the fathers, propitiating our all-good God, by the intercession of His most blessed Mother, the Ever-Virgin Mary, and our Venerable Fathers, Founders of the Coenobitic Life, Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves (who from the holy Mount Athos having founded, adorned the Caves Lavra of Kyiv with godliness and an honorable order),[^119] and of all the saints, may we be vouchsafed to obtain forgiveness of sins and the meet mercy unto the final ages.[^120]

Fare thee well in the Lord, O prudent and orthodox Reader; and receiving small things with thanksgiving, pray that by the grace of Christ thou mayest be vouchsafed greater things.[^121]

Written: On the 20th day of the month of December, according to the ancient true Calendar, in the Year from the Nativity of Christ 1616.[^122]

Z. A. K. [Zacharias Archimandrite Kopystensky][^123]

*(Archival provenance inscription: "Belongs to the library of the Trinity-Sergius Lavra. Year 1859.")*[^124]

Mark: Chapter 11, [verses] 25, 26:[^125]
*And when ye stand praying, forgive, if ye have ought against any: that your Father also Who is in heaven may forgive you your trespasses. But if ye do not forgive, neither will your Father Who is in heaven forgive your trespasses.* (Mk. 11:25-26)[^126]

# THE MIDNIGHT OFFICE

## The Beginning of the Midnight Office[^127]

*After the sounding of the semantron (klepalo), when the brethren have gathered in the church narthex (pritvor) — if it be "God is the Lord": but if it be "Alleluia", then in the church proper — the Priest begins:*

> **Priest:** "Blessed is our God always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."

*And we say:*

> **Choir:** "Amen."

*And we begin to chant, quietly and with attentiveness:*

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.[^128] *Thrice.*

*O Heavenly King:*[^129] *Trisagion.* *Three prostrations, if it be "Alleluia."*[^130] *Glory... Both now...*

*Our Father:*

*And the exclamation from the Priest:*

> **Priest:** "For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."

*And we [say]:*

> **Choir:** "Amen."

*And:* Lord, have mercy. *12 times.* *Glory... Both now...*

O come, let us worship God our King.

O come, let us worship Christ, our King and our God.

O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ Himself, our King and our God.

*And 3 prostrations. Then immediately:*

*To the end: A Psalm of David, when Nathan the Prophet came unto him,*[^131] *after he had gone in unto Bathsheba, the wife of Uriah. 50.*[^132]

*Psalm 50*

Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy; and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression. Wash me thoroughly from mine iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin. For I know mine iniquity, and my sin is ever before me. Against Thee only have I sinned, and done this evil before Thee; that Thou mightest be justified in Thy words, and conquer when Thou art judged. For behold, I was conceived in iniquities, and in sins did my mother bear me. For behold, Thou hast loved truth; (Ps. 50:1–8a)[^133] the unknown and secret things of Thy wisdom hast Thou revealed unto me. Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow. Thou shalt make me hear joy and gladness; the bones which have been humbled shall rejoice. Turn away Thy face from my sins, and blot out all mine iniquities. Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me. Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me. Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation, and establish me with a governing Spirit. I will teach transgressors Thy ways, and the ungodly shall turn back unto Thee. Deliver me from (Ps. 50:8b–16a)[^134] bloodguiltiness, O God, the God of my salvation; and my tongue shall rejoice in Thy righteousness. O Lord, Thou shalt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise. For hadst Thou desired sacrifice, I would have given it; in whole-burnt-offerings Thou delightest not. A sacrifice unto God is a broken spirit; a broken and a humbled heart God will not despise. Do good, O Lord, in Thy good pleasure unto Zion, and let the walls of Jerusalem be built. Then shalt Thou be pleased with a sacrifice of righteousness, oblation and whole-burnt-offerings; then shall they offer bullocks upon Thine altar. (Ps. 50:16b–21)[^135]

*After this:*

*Kathisma 17.*

*First Stasis.*[^136]

*Psalm 118: First Stasis*

Blessed are the undefiled in the way, who walk in the law of the Lord. Blessed are they that search His testimonies; they shall seek Him with their whole heart. For they that work iniquity have not walked in His ways. Thou hast commanded Thy commandments to be kept diligently. O that my ways were directed to keep Thy statutes! Then shall I not be ashamed, when I look upon all Thy commandments. I will confess Thee with uprightness of heart, when I shall have learned the judgments of Thy righteousness. (Ps. 118:1–7)[^137] I will keep Thy statutes; O forsake me not utterly. Wherewithal shall a young man direct his way? By keeping Thy words. With my whole heart have I sought Thee; cast me not away from Thy commandments. In my heart have I hidden Thy sayings, that I might not sin against Thee. Blessed art Thou, O Lord; teach me Thy statutes. With my lips have I declared all the judgments of Thy mouth. In the way of Thy testimonies have I taken delight, as in all manner of riches. In Thy commandments will I meditate, and (Ps. 118:8–15)[^138] understand Thy paths. I will meditate on Thy statutes; I will not forget Thy words. Render unto Thy servant: give me life, and I will keep Thy words. Open Thou mine eyes, and I will understand the wonders of Thy law. I am a sojourner on the earth; hide not Thy commandments from me. My soul has longed to desire Thy judgments at all times. Thou hast rebuked the proud; accursed are they that turn aside from Thy commandments. Remove from me reproach and contempt, for I have sought Thy testimonies. (Ps. 118:15b–22)[^139]

# THE BOOK OF HOURS (HOROLOGION) [^140]

## The Book of Hours, with God the Holy One

Containing the night and day service, according to the Typikon of the Great Lavra in Jerusalem, of our Father among the saints Sabbas; such an order is maintained also in the other holy monasteries of Jerusalem.[^141]

## The Order upon Rising Swiftly from Sleep[^142]

*Without slumbering, without laziness, and having collected thyself upon rising from sleep, step away from the bed, and say this, thrice:*

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee for all things.[^143]

*After this, stand for a little while in silence, until all the senses are quieted; and then make three prostrations unto the earth, saying:*

Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, have mercy on me, a sinner.

*Then say this prayer of Saint Macarius the Great:*[^144]

O God, cleanse me, a sinner, for I have never done any good before Thee; but deliver me from the evil one, from the evil one, and let Thy will be in me, that uncondemned I may open mine unworthy mouth and praise Thy holy name: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.[^145]

*Then begin thus:*

Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, for the sake of the prayers of Thy most pure Mother and of all Thy saints, have mercy on us. Amen.[^146]

O Heavenly King, the Comforter, the Spirit of truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of blessings and Giver of life: (cont. on fol. 2v)[^147] come and abide in us, and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One.[^148]

Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us. *Thrice.*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

All-Holy Trinity, have mercy on us; Lord, cleanse our sins; Master, pardon our iniquities; Holy One, visit and heal our infirmities, for Thy name's sake. Lord, have mercy. *Thrice.*

Glory... Both now...

Our Father, Who art in the heavens, hallowed be Thy name.

Thy kingdom come.

Thy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven. (cont. on fol. 3r)[^149] Give us this day our daily bread.

And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors.

And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one.[^150]

For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, unto the ages. Amen.[^151]

*And immediately these three Troparia to the Trinity. Tone 1:*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
Having risen from sleep, we fall down before Thee, O Good One, and we cry aloud unto Thee the angelic hymn, O Mighty One:
Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; through the protection of Thine incorporeal hosts, have mercy on us.[^152]

*Glory... Tone 2:*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
From the couch and sleep hast Thou raised me up, O Lord, and (cont. on fol. 3v)[^153] enlighten my mind and heart, and open my lips to sing of Thee, O Holy Trinity:
Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; through the prayers of all Thy saints, have mercy on us.[^154]

*Both now... Tone 3:*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Suddenly the Judge shall come, and each man's deeds shall be laid bare; but with fear let us cry at midnight:
Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, have mercy on us.[^155]

*Lord, have mercy. 12 times. And this prayer:*[^156]

*Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*
Having risen from sleep, I give thanks to Thee, O Holy Trinity, that for the sake of Thy great goodness and longsuffering Thou hast not been angry with me, a sinner and slothful, neither hast Thou destroyed me with mine iniquities; but hast shown Thy customary love toward mankind, and (cont. on fol. 4r)[^157] hast raised me up as I lay in despair, that I might offer morning praise and glorify Thy dominion. And now enlighten the eyes of my mind, open my mouth to meditate on Thy words, to understand Thy commandments, to do Thy will, to sing unto Thee with confession of heart, and to hymn Thy most holy name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.[^158]

*Second Prayer:*[^159]

Glory to Thee, O King, God Almighty, Who by Thy divine and loving providence hast vouchsafed me, a sinner (cont. on fol. 4v)[^160] and unworthy, to rise from sleep and to attain entrance into Thy holy house. Receive, O Lord, the voice of my supplication also, as that of Thy holy and noetic Powers; and be well pleased that with a clean heart and a humble spirit I may offer praise unto Thee from my defiled lips, so that I also may become a partaker with the wise virgins, with the radiant lamp of my soul, and glorify Thee, God the Word Who art glorified in the Father and the Spirit. Amen.[^161]

*If thou desirest to say the Midnight Office in solitary solitude, then after the Prayer upon Rising from Sleep, begin immediately:*
*O come, let us worship, thrice. And three prostrations. Psalm 50. Kathisma 17. And the rest in order, as was written beforehand.*[^162]

*Psalm 118: First Stasis (cont.)*[^163]

For princes sat and spoke against me; but Thy servant was meditating on Thy statutes.

For Thy testimonies are my meditation, and Thy statutes are my counsels.

My soul has cleaved unto the earth; give me life according to Thy word.

I declared my ways, and Thou heardest me; teach me Thy statutes.

Make me to understand the way of Thy statutes, and I will meditate on Thy marvelous works.

My soul has slumbered through despondency; strengthen me with Thy words.

Remove from me the way of unrighteousness, and by Thy law have mercy on me. (Ps. 118:23–29)[^164] I have chosen the way of truth, and Thy judgments have I not forgotten.

I have cleaved unto Thy testimonies; O Lord, put me not to shame.

I ran the way of Thy commandments, when Thou didst enlarge my heart.

Set before me a law, O Lord, the way of Thy statutes, and I will seek it out continually.

Give me understanding, and I will search out Thy law, and I will keep it with my whole heart.

Guide me in the path of Thy commandments, for therein is my desire.

Incline my heart unto Thy testimonies, and not to covetousness.

Turn away mine eyes from seeing vanity; (cont. on fol. 10r)[^165] give me life in Thy way.

Confirm Thy word unto Thy servant, unto Thy fear.

Take away my reproach which I have feared, for Thy judgments are good.

Behold, I have longed for Thy commandments; give me life in Thy righteousness.

And let Thy mercy come upon me, O Lord, even Thy salvation, according to Thy word.

So shall I give an answer to them that reproach me, for I have trusted in Thy words.

And take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth, for I have trusted in Thy judgments.

And I will keep Thy law continually, for ever, and unto the age of the age. (Ps. 118:30–44)[^166] And I walked at large, for I sought Thy commandments.

And I spoke of Thy testimonies before kings, and was not ashamed.

And I meditated on Thy commandments, which I loved exceedingly.

And I lifted up my hands unto Thy commandments, which I loved, and I meditated on Thy statutes.

Remember Thy words unto Thy servant, wherein Thou hast given me hope.

This has comforted me in my humiliation, for Thy word has quickened me.

The proud transgressed exceedingly; yet from Thy law have I not declined. (cont. on fol. 11r)[^167] I remembered Thy judgments from of old, O Lord, and was comforted.

Despondency took hold of me because of the sinners that forsake Thy law.

Thy statutes were my songs in the place of my sojourning.

I remembered Thy name in the night, O Lord, and kept Thy law.

This came to pass for me, because I sought Thy statutes.

Thou art my portion, O Lord; I said that I would keep Thy law.

I made supplication before Thy face with my whole heart; have mercy on me according to Thy word.

I thought upon Thy ways, and turned my feet (cont. on fol. 11v)[^168] unto Thy testimonies.

I made ready and was not disquieted, to keep Thy commandments.

The cords of sinners have entangled me, but Thy law have I not forgotten.

At midnight I rose to give thanks unto Thee for the judgments of Thy righteousness.

I am a companion of all them that fear Thee, and keep Thy commandments.

The earth, O Lord, is full of Thy mercy; teach me Thy statutes.

Thou hast dealt kindly with Thy servant, O Lord, according to Thy word.

Teach me goodness and discipline and knowledge, for I have believed Thy commandments. (cont. on fol. 12r)[^169] Before I was humbled, I went astray; therefore have I kept Thy word.

Good art Thou, O Lord, and in Thy goodness teach me Thy statutes.

The unrighteousness of the proud has multiplied against me; but with my whole heart will I search out Thy commandments.

Their heart is curdled like milk, but as for me, I have meditated on Thy law.

It is good for me that Thou hast humbled me, that I might learn Thy statutes.

The law of Thy mouth is good to me, above thousands of gold and silver. (Ps. 118:45–72)[^170]

*Glory... Both now... Alleluia. Thrice. Three prostrations. Second Stasis.*[^171]

*Psalm 118: Second Stasis*[^172]

Thy hands have made me and fashioned me; give me understanding, and I will learn Thy commandments.

They that fear Thee shall see me and be glad, because I have trusted in Thy words.

I have known, O Lord, that Thy judgments are righteousness, and in very truth Thou hast humbled me.

Let Thy mercy be upon me to comfort me, according to Thy word unto Thy servant.

Let Thy compassions come unto me, and I shall live, for Thy law is my meditation.

Let the proud be put to shame, for they have transgressed against me unjustly; but I will meditate on Thy commandments.

Let them that fear Thee turn unto me, and those that (cont. on fol. 13r)[^173] know Thy testimonies.

Let my heart be blameless in Thy statutes, that I may not be put to shame.

My soul faints for Thy salvation; I have trusted in Thy words.

Mine eyes have failed for Thy word, saying: When wilt Thou comfort me?

For I am become like a wineskin in the frost; yet Thy statutes have I not forgotten.

How many are the days of Thy servant? When wilt Thou execute judgment on them that persecute me?

Transgressors have related fables unto me, but not like Thy law, O Lord.

All Thy commandments are truth; they have persecuted me unjustly: help Thou me. (Ps. 118:73–86)[^174] They almost made an end of me upon the earth, but I forsook not Thy commandments.

Quicken me according to Thy mercy, and I will keep the testimonies of Thy mouth.

For ever, O Lord, Thy word abides in heaven; Thy truth is unto generation and generation.

Thou hast established the earth, and it abides.

By Thine ordinance it continues this day, for all things are Thy servants.

Had not Thy law been my meditation, then should I have perished in my humiliation.

I will never forget Thy statutes, for in them Thou hast given me life. (Ps. 118:87–93)[^175]

*The Middle Stasis.*[^176]

*Psalm 118: Middle Stasis (cont.)*[^177]

I am Thine, save me; for I have sought Thy statutes.

Sinners waited for me to destroy me, but I understood Thy testimonies.

I have seen the end of all perfection; but Thy commandment is exceeding broad.

O how I have loved Thy law, O Lord! It is my meditation all the day.

Thou through Thy commandment hast made me wiser than mine enemies, for it is mine for ever.

I have understood more than all my teachers, for Thy testimonies are my meditation.

I have understood more than the ancients, because I have sought Thy commandments.

I have restrained my feet from every evil way, (cont. on fol. 14v)[^178] that I might keep Thy words.

I have not departed from Thy judgments, for Thou hast set a law for me.

How sweet are Thy words unto my palate, yea, sweeter than honey to my mouth!

Through Thy commandments have I gained understanding; therefore have I hated every way of unrighteousness.

Thy law is a lamp unto my feet, and a light unto my paths.

I have sworn, and determined that I would keep the judgments of Thy righteousness.

I was humbled exceedingly, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy word.

Be pleased with the freewill offerings of my mouth, O Lord, and teach me Thy judgments. (cont. on fol. 15r)[^179] My soul is continually in Thy hands, and Thy law have I not forgotten.

Sinners laid a snare for me, yet I strayed not from Thy commandments.

Thy testimonies have I inherited for ever, for they are the rejoicing of my heart.

I have inclined my heart to perform Thy statutes for ever, for a recompense.

Transgressors have I hated, but Thy law have I loved.

Thou art my helper and my protector; I have trusted in Thy words.

Depart from me, ye evildoers, and I will search out the commandments of my God.

Uphold me according to Thy word, and I shall live; (cont. on fol. 15v)[^180] and put me not to shame from mine expectation.

Help me, and I shall be saved, and I will meditate on Thy statutes continually.

Thou hast set at nought all them that depart from Thy statutes, for their inward thought is unrighteous.

I have accounted all the sinners of the earth as transgressors; therefore have I loved Thy testimonies.

Nail down my flesh with the fear of Thee, for I have been afraid of Thy judgments.

I have done judgment and righteousness; give me not over unto them that wrong me.

Receive Thy servant unto good, that the proud slander me not.

Mine eyes have failed for Thy salvation, and for the word of Thy righteousness. (cont. on fol. 16r)[^181] Deal with Thy servant according to Thy mercy, and teach me Thy judgments.

I am Thy servant; give me understanding, and I will know Thy testimonies.

It is time for the Lord to act, for they have made void Thy law.

Therefore have I loved Thy commandments above gold and topaz.

Therefore have I directed myself according to all Thy commandments; every way of unrighteousness have I hated.

Marvelous are Thy testimonies; therefore has my soul searched them out.

The manifestation of Thy words enlightens, and gives understanding unto infants. (Ps. 118:102–130)[^182] *Midnight Office, Daily*

I opened my mouth and drew in my breath, for I longed for Thy commandments.
*Glory... Both now... Alleluia. (Three times).*
*Bows, 3. The Third Stasis.*[^183]
Look Thou upon me and have mercy on me, according to the judgment of them that love Thy name.
Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me.
Deliver me from the false accusation of men, and I will keep Thy commandments.
Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thy statutes.
Mine eyes have poured down streams of water, because I kept not Thy law.

*Daily. [Fol. 17r]*
Righteous art Thou, O Lord, and upright are Thy judgments.
Thou hast commanded righteousness in Thy testimonies and truth exceedingly.[^184]
My zeal has consumed me, because mine enemies have forgotten Thy words.
Thy word is tried in the fire exceedingly, and Thy servant has loved it.
I am young and despised, yet have I not forgotten Thy statutes.
Thy righteousness is righteousness unto eternity, and Thy law is truth.
Afflictions and distress have found me; Thy commandments are my meditation.
Thy testimonies are righteousness unto eternity; give me understanding, and I shall live.

*Midnight Office, Daily*

I cried with my whole heart: Hear me, O Lord, I will search out Thy statutes.
I cried unto Thee: Save me, and I will keep Thy testimonies.
I prevented the dawn of morning, and cried; upon Thy words have I hoped.[^185]
Mine eyes prevented the morning dawn, that I might meditate on Thy words.
Hear my voice, O Lord, according to Thy mercy; quicken me according to Thy judgment.
They drew near that persecute me unlawfully; they are far removed from Thy law.
Near art Thou, O Lord, and all Thy ways are truth.
From the beginning have I known concerning Thy testimonies, that Thou hast founded them unto eternity.

*Daily. [Fol. 18r]*
Behold my humility, and deliver me, for I have not forgotten Thy law.
Judge my cause, and redeem me; quicken me according to Thy word.[^186]
Salvation is far from sinners, for they have not searched out Thy statutes.
Thy compassions are many, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy judgment.
Many are they that persecute me and afflict me; from Thy testimonies have I not declined.
I beheld the foolish, and I pined away, because they kept not Thy words.
Behold how I have loved Thy commandments, O Lord; quicken me in Thy mercy.
The beginning of Thy words is truth, and unto eternity *Midnight Office, Daily*

...are all the judgments of Thy righteousness.
Princes persecuted me without a cause, and my heart was afraid of Thy words.
I will rejoice at Thy words, as one that finds great spoil.
Unrighteousness have I hated and abhorred, but Thy law have I loved.
Seven times a day have I praised Thee for the judgments of Thy righteousness.[^187]
Great peace have they that love Thy law, and there is no stumbling-block unto them.
I have waited for Thy salvation, O Lord, and have loved Thy commandments.
My soul has kept Thy testimonies, and loved them exceedingly.

*Daily. [Fol. 19r]*
I have kept Thy commandments and Thy testimonies, for all my ways are before Thee, O Lord.
Let my prayer draw near before Thee, O Lord; give me understanding according to Thy word.
Let my supplication come before Thee, O Lord; deliver me according to Thy word.
My lips shall pour forth praise, when Thou hast taught me Thy statutes.
My tongue shall proclaim Thy words, for all Thy commandments are righteousness.
Let Thy hand be near to save me, for I have chosen Thy commandments.
I have longed for Thy salvation, O Lord, and Thy law is my meditation.
My soul shall live, and it shall praise Thee,[^188] *Midnight Office, Daily*

...and Thy judgments shall help me.
I have gone astray like a lost sheep; seek Thy servant, for I have not forgotten Thy commandments.
*Glory... Both now... Without Alleluia. Then:*
*The Confession of the Orthodox Faith.*[^189]
*(First Council)*
I believe in one God, the Father Almighty, Maker of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible. And in one Lord Jesus Christ, the Son of God, the Only-begotten, begotten of the Father before all ages; Light of Light, true God of true God; begotten, not made; of one essence with the Father, by Whom all things were made; Who for us men and for our salvation came down from Heaven, *Daily. [Fol. 20r]*
and was incarnate of the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary, and became man; and was crucified for us under Pontius Pilate, and suffered, and was buried; and rose again on the third day according to the Scriptures; and ascended into Heaven, and sits at the right hand of the Father; and shall come again with glory to judge the living and the dead; Whose kingdom shall have no end.
*(Second Council)*[^190]
And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord, the Giver of life, Who proceeds from the Father; Who with the Father and the Son together is worshipped and glorified; Who spoke by the prophets. In one, holy, catholic, and apostolic Church. I confess one baptism for the remission of sins. I look for the resurrection of the dead, and the life of the age to come. Amen.

*Midnight Office, Daily*

*Then, Trisagion. Metanoias, 3. O Most Holy Trinity... Our Father... And straightway the Priest exclaims:* **Priest:** For Thine is the kingdom... *Then, these Troparia in Tone 8:*[^191]
Behold, the Bridegroom comes at midnight, and blessed is the servant whom He shall find watching; but unworthy again is he whom He shall find heedless. Beware therefore, O my soul, lest thou be weighed down with sleep, lest thou be given over unto death, and be shut out from the kingdom; but arouse thyself, crying: Holy, holy, holy art Thou, O God; through the Birthgiver of God, have mercy on us.
*Glory...*
Considering that fearful day, O my soul, keep watch, lighting thy lamp and brightening it with oil; for thou knowest not when the voice shall come unto thee, saying: Behold, the Bridegroom! Beware therefore, O my soul, *Midnight Office, Daily*[^192]

...glorified, O good God, long-suffering and plenteous in mercy; Who lovest the righteous and hast mercy on the sinners; Who callest all to salvation through the promise of good things to come: do Thou Thyself, O Lord, receive our supplications at this hour, and direct our life unto Thy commandments. Sanctify our souls; purify our bodies; correct our thoughts; sober our minds; and deliver us from all affliction, evil, and distress. Encompass us about by Thy holy angels, that guarded and guided by their host, we may attain unto the unity of the faith and unto the knowledge of Thine unapproachable glory;

*Daily. [Fol. 21r]*

...lest thou slumber, and remain outside knocking like the five virgins; but watch vigilantly, that thou mayest meet Christ with rich oil, and He may grant thee the bridal chamber of His divine glory.
*Both now... Theotokion:*[^193]
Thee, the invincible rampart, the confirmation of salvation, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, do we beseech: destroy the counsels of the adversaries; turn the sorrow of thy people into joy; call forth thy peace; strengthen the Orthodox princes; and pray for the pacification of the whole world, for thou, O Birthgiver of God, art our hope.
*Then: Lord, have mercy (40). And the prayer of St. Basil the Great:*
*Thou Who at all times Daily. [Fol. 22r]*
...for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen.
> **Reader:** "Lord, have mercy. *(Three times)* Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, the very Birthgiver of God, thee do we magnify. In the name of the Lord, bless, Father."
> **Priest:** "God be gracious unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us."
*And if it be a fast, we make 15 great metanoias, saying secretly this prayer of St. Ephrem:*[^194]
O Lord and Master of my life, take from me the spirit of sloth, despair, lust of power, and idle talk. *(Bow, 1)*
But give rather the spirit of chastity, humility, *Midnight Office, Daily*

...thy coming in and thy going out, from henceforth and even unto eternity.[^195]
*Psalm 133.*
Behold now, bless the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord, who stand in the house of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God. In the nights lift up your hands unto the holies, and bless the Lord. The Lord bless thee out of Sion, He that made heaven and earth.
*Glory... Both now... Without Alleluia. After this, Trisagion. Metanoias, 3. After Our Father, Troparia in Tone 8:*
Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, Thy servants, and wherein they have sinned in life, forgive them; for no one is sinless save Thee alone, Who art able to give rest unto the departed, granting them life unending, *Daily. [Fol. 23r]*
O Thou only Creator. Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy servants; for they placed their hope on Thee, our Maker and Creator and God.
*Glory... Kontakion:*[^196]
With the saints give rest, O Christ, to the souls of Thy servants, where there is no sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life unending.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
All generations call thee blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; for in thee Christ our God, Whom nothing can contain, was pleased to be contained. Blessed are we also, having thee as our protectress; for day and night thou dost intercede for us, and by thy prayers the scepters of kingdoms are established. Therefore, giving thanks, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee.
*Lord, have mercy (12).*
*Then, Midnight Office, Daily*

*Then, this Prayer:*[^197]
Remember, O Lord, in the hope of resurrection and eternal life, our departed fathers and brethren, and all who have fallen asleep in the Orthodox faith; forgive them every transgression, voluntary and involuntary, committed by them in word, deed, or thought; and establish them in places of light and refreshment, in places of rest, whence all sickness, sorrow, and sighing have fled away; where the light of Thy countenance visits and makes glad all Thy saints from of old. Grant them Thy kingdom, and communion in Thine ineffable and eternal blessings, and the enjoyment of Thine unending and blessed life.

*Daily. [Fol. 25r]*
For Thou art the resurrection, the life, and the rest of Thy departed servants, O Christ our God; and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and with Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Then the Psalmist says:* **Reader:** Lord, have mercy. *(Twice)* Lord, bless.
*The Priest says:*
> **Priest:** "May Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His all-pure Mother, of our venerable and God-bearing fathers, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind."
*And we answer:* **Choir:** Amen.
*If a monk is alone in his cell, let him finish thus:*[^198]

*Midnight Office, Daily*

O Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, for the sake of the prayers of Thine all-pure Mother, of our venerable and God-bearing fathers, and of all the saints, have mercy on us. Amen.
*And the Priest makes a bow toward the brethren, saying:*[^199]
> **Priest:** "Bless, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner, wherein I have sinned this night and all the days of my life, in word, deed, or thought, and by all my senses."
*And all we answer:*
**Brethren:** God forgive and have mercy on thee, venerable father.
*Then the elder standing on the right side does likewise. And all the brethren together say softly:*
**Brethren:** Bless, holy father, and forgive me, a sinner.

*Daily. [Fol. 26r]*
*The same verse. And the Superior answers:*
**Superior:** In His grace, may God forgive and have mercy on you all.
*And after the forgiveness, the Priest says:*[^200]
> **Priest:** "Let us pray for our God-preserved Sovereign and for our pious Princes."
*The Psalmist answers:*
> **Reader:** "May God preserve the power of their kingdom and of their principality."
> **Priest:** "For the peace of their dominions."
*And all we answer:*
**Brethren:** May God grant them success.
> **Priest:** "For all the Orthodox episcopate."
*And all they answer:*

*Midnight Office, Daily*

**Brethren:** May God save and have mercy on them.[^201]
> **Priest:** "For our fathers, and for all our brethren in Christ."
*They answer:*
**Brethren:** May God save and have mercy on them.
> **Priest:** "For those who have commanded us, the unworthy, to pray for them."
*All we answer:*
**Brethren:** May God forgive and have mercy on them.
> **Priest:** "For them that show mercy and serve us."
**Brethren:** May God forgive and have mercy.
> **Priest:** "For them that hate us and love us."
**Brethren:** May God forgive and have mercy.
> **Priest:** "For the deliverance of captives."
**Brethren:** May God liberate and have mercy on them.
> **Priest:** "For them that sail and travel."
**Brethren:** May God preserve and have mercy on them.

*Daily. [Fol. 27r]*
> **Priest:** "For them that lie in sickness and are in distress."[^202]
**Brethren:** May God raise them up and have mercy.
> **Priest:** "For them that are afflicted and sit in prison."
**Brethren:** May God visit and have mercy on them.
> **Priest:** "For all Orthodox Christians."
**Brethren:** May God forgive and have mercy on them.
> **Priest:** "For the abundance of the fruits of the earth."
**Brethren:** May God multiply the fruits of the earth.
> **Priest:** "For the departed: let us remember the Orthodox kings and princes."
*And they answer:*
**Brethren:** May God have mercy on them, and give them rest.
> **Priest:** "The founders of this holy temple, and of this holy monastery."

*Daily Midnight Office*

Our parents and our kinsmen, and our fathers and brethren who have previously fallen asleep, here lying and Orthodox [Christians] everywhere.
*Then the Verse:*
> **Priest:** "Through the prayers of our holy fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."
*Likewise the priest makes commemoration also at Compline.*
*But if he is alone, he says this prayer at the Midnight Office and at Compline:*[^203]
Forgive, O Lord and Lover of mankind, them that hate us and wrong us. Do good unto them that do good. Unto all our brethren and kinsmen, and them that live in solitude: grant them all petitions that lead unto (cont. on fol. 28r) *Daily. [Fol. 28r]*
...salvation, and life eternal.
Visit them that lie in sickness, and heal them.
Deliver them that are in prisons.
Unto them that sail the sea be a pilot.
And direct and prosper them that journey by ways.
Remember, O Lord, our captive brethren of one faith in the Orthodox confession, and deliver them from every evil circumstance.
Have mercy, O Lord, and forgive them that have commanded us, the unworthy, to pray for them.
Have mercy, O Lord, on them that serve us and show mercy unto us, and grant them all petitions that lead unto salvation, and life eternal.
Remember, O Lord, our fathers and brethren who have previously departed, and settle them where the light (cont. on fol. 28v) *Daily Midnight Office*

...of Thy countenance visits. Remember, O Lord, our lowliness and wretchedness,[^204] and enlighten our mind with the light of the understanding of Thy Holy Gospel, and guide [us] upon the path of Thy commandments; through the prayers of Thine all-pure Mother, and of all Thy saints. Amen.

*(Engraved tailpiece: Renaissance cartouche with bearded grotesque mask) (Engraved headpiece: Floral scrollwork with foliage, blossoms, and birds)*

## ANOTHER MIDNIGHT OFFICE, SUNG ON SATURDAYS

*When the Beginning has been made, as was previously written for the Daily Midnight Office: after the Fiftieth Psalm [Ps. 50], we chant the Ninth Kathisma.*[^205]

*Psalm 64:*[^206]
Praise is meet for Thee, O God, in Zion, and unto Thee shall prayer be rendered in Jerusalem.
Hear my prayer: unto Thee shall all flesh come.
Words of the iniquitous (cont. on fol. 29v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...have overpowered us; and as for our ungodly deeds, Thou wilt purge them away. Blessed is he whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself; he shall dwell in Thy courts. We shall be filled with the good things of Thy house; holy is Thy temple, marvelous in righteousness. Hear us, O God our Savior, the hope of all the ends of the earth, and of them that are afar off upon the sea; Who preparest the mountains by Thy strength, being girded with power; Who troublest the depth of the sea. Who can withstand the roar of its waves? The nations shall be troubled, and they that dwell in the uttermost bounds shall be afraid of Thy signs. Thou shalt beautify the outgoings of the morning and the evening. Thou hast visited the earth and watered it; Thou hast greatly enriched it. The river of God is filled with (cont. on fol. 30r) *Saturday. [Fol. 30r]*

...waters. Thou hast prepared their food, for so is its preparation. Water Thou her furrows; multiply her fruits; in her drops she shall rejoice springing up. Thou wilt bless the crown of the year of Thy goodness, and Thy plains shall be filled with fatness. The pastures of the wilderness shall become fat, and the hills shall gird themselves with joy. The rams of the sheep are clothed, and the valleys shall abound with wheat; they shall cry aloud, yea, they shall sing.

*Psalm 65:*[^207]
Shout with joy unto the Lord, all the earth; chant ye unto His name, give glory unto His praise.
Say unto God: How awesome are Thy works; in the multitude of Thy power Thine enemies shall fawn upon Thee.
All the earth shall worship (cont. on fol. 30v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...Thee, and sing unto Thee; yea, let it sing unto Thy name, O Most High.
Come and see the works of God, how awesome He is in His counsels beyond the sons of men.
He turns the sea into dry land; they shall pass through the river on foot; there shall we rejoice in Him, Who rules by His power unto the ages.
His eyes look upon the nations; let not them that provoke Him be exalted in themselves.
Bless our God, ye nations, and cause the voice of His praise to be heard;
Who has appointed my soul unto life, and has not given my feet over unto slipping.
For Thou, O God, hast proved us; Thou hast tried us with fire, even as silver is tried. Thou hast brought us into the snare; Thou hast laid (cont. on fol. 31r) *Saturday. [Fol. 31r]*

...afflictions upon our back; Thou hast set men over our heads.
We went through fire and through water, and Thou hast brought us out into a place of refreshment.
I will go into Thy house with whole burnt-offerings; I will pay Thee my vows, which my lips have uttered, and my mouth has spoken in mine affliction.
I will offer unto Thee whole burnt-offerings full of marrow, with incense and rams; I will offer unto Thee bullocks with goats.
Come, give ear, and I will declare unto you, all ye that fear God, what things He has done for my soul.
I cried unto Him with my mouth, and I exalted Him with my tongue.
If I have regarded iniquity in my heart, let not the Lord hear me.
Therefore God has heard me; He has attended to the voice of my (cont. on fol. 31v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...supplication.
Blessed be God, Who has not turned away my prayer, nor His mercy from me.

*Psalm 66:*[^208]
God be gracious unto us, and bless us; cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us;
That we may know Thy way upon the earth, Thy salvation among all the nations.
Let the peoples praise Thee, O God; let all the peoples praise Thee.
Let the nations be glad and rejoice, for Thou shalt judge the peoples with uprightness, and shalt guide the nations upon earth.
Let the peoples praise Thee, O God; let all the peoples praise Thee.
The earth has yielded her fruit.
God, our God, bless us; (cont. on fol. 32r) *Saturday. [Fol. 32r]*

...God bless us, and let all the ends of the earth fear Him.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Psalm 67:*[^209]
Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered; and let them that hate Him flee from before His face.
As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish; as wax melts before the face of fire, so let the sinners perish from before the face of God.
And let the righteous be glad; let them rejoice before God, let them delight with gladness.
Sing unto God, sing praises unto His name; make way for Him Who rides upon the heavens; the Lord is His name, and rejoice ye before Him.
They shall be troubled before His face, the Father of orphans, and Judge of widows: (cont. on fol. 32v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...God in His holy place.
God settles the solitary in a house, leading forth the bound with strength, likewise them that provoke Him, who dwell in graves.
O God, when Thou wentest forth before Thy people, when Thou didst traverse in the wilderness, the earth shook;
Yea, the heavens dropped at the face of the God of Sinai, at the face of the God of Israel.
A rain of free will didst Thou set apart, O God, for Thine inheritance; and it was weakened, but Thou didst perfect it.
Thy creatures dwell therein; Thou hast prepared it in Thy goodness for the poor, O God.
The Lord gives the word unto them that preach good tidings with great power.
The King of the powers of the beloved, for the beauty of the house to divide the spoils.
Though ye sleep among the lots, (cont. on fol. 33r) *Saturday. [Fol. 33r]*

...[yet shall ye be as] the wings of a dove covered with silver, and her pinions with the glistening of gold.
When the Heavenly One scatters kings upon her, they shall become white as snow in Salmon.
The mountain of God is a fertile mountain, a curded mountain, a fertile mountain.
Why do ye look askance, ye curded mountains?
This is the mountain wherein God has been pleased to dwell; yea, the Lord will dwell therein unto the end.
The chariot of God is ten thousand times ten thousand, thousands of rejoicing ones; the Lord is among them in Sinai, in the holy place.
Thou hast ascended on high, Thou hast led captivity captive; Thou hast received gifts among men, yea, even for the disobedient, that Thou mightest dwell [there].
Blessed is the Lord God, blessed is the Lord day by day; the God of our salvation will prosper us; our God is the God (cont. on fol. 33v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...of salvation; and of the Lord, of the Lord are the issues of death.
But God shall crush the heads of His enemies, the hairy crown of them that go on in their trespasses.
The Lord said: I will turn back from Bashan, I will bring back from the depths of the sea;
That thy foot may be dipped in blood, the tongue of thy dogs from the enemies, from him.
Thy goings have been seen, O God, the goings of my God, my King, Who is in the sanctuary.
The princes went before, next to them that sing, in the midst of maidens playing on timbrels.
In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.
There is Benjamin the younger in ecstasy, the princes of Judah their leaders, the princes of Zebulun, the princes of Naphtali.
Command, O God, Thy power; strengthen, O God, this (cont. on fol. 34r) *Saturday. [Fol. 34r]*

...which Thou hast wrought in us.
From Thy temple at Jerusalem, kings shall bring gifts unto Thee.
Rebuke the wild beasts of the reed, the assembly of bulls among the heifers of the peoples, to exclude them that are tried with silver.
Scatter Thou the nations that desire wars.
Ambassadors shall come out of Egypt; Ethiopia shall stretch out her hands beforehand unto God.
Ye kingdoms of the earth, sing unto God; chant ye unto the Lord;
Unto Him that rides upon the heaven of heavens toward the east; lo, He will utter His voice, a voice of power.
Ascribe ye glory unto God; upon Israel is His magnificence, and His power is in the clouds.
Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel; He will give power and strength unto His people.
Blessed be God.
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Saturday Midnight Office*

*Psalm 68:*[^210]
Save me, O God, for the waters have entered in even unto my soul.
I am stuck fast in the mire of the deep, and there is no standing.
I am come into the depths of the sea, and a tempest has overwhelmed me.
I am wearied with crying, my throat is grown hoarse; mine eyes have failed, while I hope in my God.
They that hate me without a cause are multiplied more than the hairs of my head.
Mine enemies that persecute me unjustly are grown strong; what things I took not away, then did I restore.
O God, Thou knowest my foolishness, and my trespasses are not hidden from Thee.
Let not them that wait on Thee, O Lord, Lord of hosts, be ashamed for my sake; (cont. on fol. 35r) *Saturday. [Fol. 35r]*

...neither let them that seek Thee, O God of Israel, be put to confusion on my account.
For Thy sake have I borne reproach; shame has covered my face.
I am become a stranger unto my brethren, and an alien unto the sons of my mother.
For the zeal of Thy house has eaten me up, and the reproaches of them that reproached Thee have fallen upon me.
And I covered my soul with fasting, and it was made a reproach unto me.
And I made sackcloth my garment, and I became a proverb unto them.
They that sit in the gate talked against me, and they that drank wine sang of me.
But as for me, with my prayer [I come] unto Thee, O Lord, in an acceptable time.
O God, in the multitude of Thy mercy hear me, in the truth of Thy salvation.
Save me from the mire, that I sink not; deliver (cont. on fol. 35v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...me from them that hate me, and from the deep waters.
Let not the waterflood drown me, neither let the deep swallow me up, and let not the pit shut her mouth upon me.
Hear me, O Lord, for Thy mercy is good; look upon me according to the multitude of Thy compassions.
And turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; hear me speedily.
Attend unto my soul, and redeem it; deliver me because of mine enemies.
For Thou knowest my reproach, and my shame, and my confusion; all they that afflict me are before Thee.
Reproach has broken my soul and my misery; and I looked for one that would grieve with me, but there was none; and for comforters, but I found none.
And they gave me gall for my food, (cont. on fol. 36r) *Saturday. [Fol. 36r]*

...and in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink.
Let their table become a snare before them, and a recompense, and a stumbling-block.
Let their eyes be darkened, that they see not; and bow down their back continually.
Pour out Thine indignation upon them, and let the wrath of Thine anger overtake them.
Let their habitation be desolate, and in their tents let there be none to dwell.
For they have persecuted him whom Thou hast smitten, and they have added to the grief of my wounds.
Add iniquity unto their iniquity, and let them not enter into Thy righteousness.
Let them be blotted out of the book of the living, and not be written with the righteous.
Poor and in pain am I; the salvation of Thy face, O God, has upheld me.
I will praise the name of my God (cont. on fol. 36v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...with a song, I will magnify Him with thanksgiving.
And this shall please God better than a young bullock that has horns and hoofs.
Let the poor see and be glad; seek ye God, and your soul shall live.
For the Lord has heard the poor, and His bound He has not despised.
Let the heavens and earth praise Him, the sea and everything that creeps therein.
For God will save Zion, and the cities of Judah shall be built; and they shall dwell there, and inherit it;
And the seed of Thy servants shall possess it, and they that love Thy name shall dwell therein.

*Psalm 69:*[^211]
O God, attend unto my help; O Lord, make haste to help me.
Let them be ashamed and (cont. on fol. 37r) *Saturday. [Fol. 37r]*

...confounded that seek after my soul.
Let them be turned backward and brought to confusion that wish me evil.
Let them be turned back immediately in shame that say unto me: Well done! Well done!
Let all those that seek Thee, O God, rejoice and be glad in Thee; and let such as love Thy salvation say continually: The Lord be magnified!
But as for me, I am poor and needy; O God, help me.
Thou art my helper and my deliverer; O Lord, make no tarrying.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Without Alleluia.*
*I believe in one God:* (see fol. 14)[^212]
*Then, the Trisagion. And after the Our Father, the Priest says:*
> **Priest:** "For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen."
*Then, Triadica. These Troparia: Tone 2:*[^213]

*Saturday Midnight Office*

*Troparion 1, Tone 2:*
O Uncreated Nature, Creator of all, open Thou our lips, that we may proclaim Thy praise, crying aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; through the prayers of Thine Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, have mercy on us.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Troparion 2:*
Emulating the powers on high, we on earth offer unto Thee a song of victory, crying aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; through the prayers of all Thy saints, have mercy on us.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Troparion 3 (Theotokion):*
From the bed and sleep hast Thou raised me up, O Lord; enlighten my mind and heart, and open my lips, to hymn Thee, O Holy Trinity: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God; for the sake of the Birthgiver of God, have mercy on us.

*Lord, have mercy (40 times).*
*He Who at all times: [Prayer of Saint Basil the Great]* (see fol. 20)[^214]
*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the (cont. on fol. 38r) Saturday. [Fol. 38r]*

*...Cherubim. In the name of the Lord [bless, father]. Priest:*
> **Priest:** "God be compassionate unto us, and bless us..."
*And we [say]: Master, God, Father Almighty:* (see fol. 22)[^215]
*In cells, however, we say this prayer of the holy Great Martyr Eustratios.*[^216]
