*But in the cathedral community this is not said:*

*Prayer of Saint Eustratios:*
Magnifying I magnify Thee, O Lord, for Thou hast looked upon my lowliness, and hast not shut me up into the hands of the enemies, but hast saved my soul from distresses. And now, O Master, let Thy hand overshadow me, and let Thy mercy come upon me; for my soul is troubled, and is pained at its departure from this my wretched body; lest the evil counsel of the adversary meet and trip it in the darkness, because of the unknown and known sins committed by me in (cont. on fol. 38v) *Saturday Midnight Office*

...this life. Be merciful unto me, O Master, and let not my soul behold the dark gaze of the evil demons; but let Thine angels, bright and radiant, receive it. Give glory unto Thy holy name, and by Thy power lead me up unto Thy divine judgment seat. When I am judged, let not the hand of the prince of this world lay hold of me, to drag me, a sinner, into the depths of hades; but stand by, and be Thou my Savior and Protector. For these bodily torments are a joy unto Thy servants. Have mercy, O Lord, on my soul which has been defiled by the passions of this life; and receive it cleansed, by reason of repentance and confession. For Thou art blessed, (cont. on fol. 39r) *Saturday. [Fol. 39r]*

...and praised in Thine ages of saints, unto ages of ages. Amen.

*We chant:*
*Come, let us worship... [Bows, 3].*
*And the rest in order, as is written in the first Midnight Office: (see fol. 23).[^217]*
*Then, the Dismissal. And all the Mutual Forgiveness, as usual.*[^218]

*(Typographic tailpiece: Symmetrical cruciform strapwork ornament with floral rosettes) (Engraved headpiece: Strapwork cartouche with eagle holding an inscribed banner reading "STRENGTH AND LIFE", flanked by acanthus scrolls and blossoms)*

## SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE

*It should be known that if there is no All-Night Vigil on Sunday, the Midnight Office takes place before Matins.*
*When the Priest has begun, we say: O Heavenly King; Trisagion; after the Our Father, the Priest [says]: For Thine is the kingdom...*
*Then, Lord, have mercy (12 times). Glory... Both now... Then: Come, let us worship (3 times). Psalm 50: Have mercy on me, O God: written in the Daily Midnight Office.*[^219]
*Then, the Canon to the Trinity of the current Tone.*
*We chant the Supplicatory Triadica, the composition of Gregory of Sinai:*[^220]

*Sunday. [Fol. 40r]*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "It Is Truly Meet" (Достойно есть):*
It is truly meet in a manner worthy of God to glorify Thee, O Holy Trinity, boundless and transcendent in essence, and all-good God of ours; Whom all the earth-born glorify, and the Heavenly Powers hymn; Who art faithfully worshiped by all, a Monad according to essence, and in three Hypostases.

The Sovereign Ruler of the Cherubim, and the Godhead beyond compare of the Seraphim, the undivided Trinity in Unity, the existing Sovereign Godhead, Thee do we magnify.

Unto the unoriginate Father and God, unto the co-unoriginate Word, together with the Spirit, do I bow down: undivided, one essence hymned... (cont. on fol. 40v) *Sunday Midnight Office*

...threefold number, one Monad, we the faithful glorify.
Let us all sing in a manner worthy of God, with divine praises: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Divine Spirit, three-hypostatic nature, one Kingdom and Lordship.
Flash forth upon me Thy beam-bright lightnings, O my God, Three-Hypostatic Creator; reveal me as a dwelling of Thine unapproachable glory, bright, light-bearing, and unchanging.
Before Whom the Cherubim tremble and quake, and all the angelic hosts hymn with praise: glorify with fear Christ the Giver of life, Who was ineffably incarnate of the Virgin.
It is meet with all piety to entreat Thee with tears, the All-Merciful and All- (cont. on fol. 41r) *Sunday. [Fol. 41r]*

...Compassionate, and the Savior of our souls.
Receive tears, O King; receive the weeping and wailing of the wretched one; and deliver me from the flame and the outer darkness, and make me a partaker of the Divine Kingdom.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*
O Father Almighty, Word of God, All-Holy Spirit, one Power and Image, one Kingdom, Godhead, and Authority: have mercy and save them that worship Thee.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
Having seen Thy Son risen from the dead in a manner becoming God, O All-Pure Virgin, creation was filled with ineffable joy; glorifying Him, we magnify thee.

*We chant:*
*Trisagion. And after the Our Father, the Priest says:*
> **Priest:** "For Thine is the kingdom..."
*And after this, the Hypakoe of the Tone.[^221] (cont. on fol. 41v) Sunday Midnight Office*

*Or: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us; Glory: O Lord, have mercy on us, for in Thee have we trusted; Both now: Open unto us the doors of compassion: written in Compline: (see fol. 25).[^222]*
*Then, Lord, have mercy (40 times).*
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*More honorable than the Cherubim, and more glorious beyond compare than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, the very Birthgiver of God, thee do we magnify.*
*In the name of the Lord [bless, father]. Priest:*
> **Priest:** "God be compassionate unto us, and bless us..."
*And we say the Prayer to the Holy Trinity, the composition of Mark the Monk.*[^223]
*This same prayer is also said on a great evening [Great Compline] after Compline:*

*Prayer of Mark the Monk:*
All-powerful and Life-creating Holy Trinity, and Source of Light, Who through sole goodness didst bring from non-existence into being earthly and all transcendent creation, and dost sustain it by Thy providence; Who among Thy other ineffable (cont. on fol. 42r) *Sunday. [Fol. 42r]*

...benefactions toward the earthly race, because of the weakness of the flesh, hast granted unto us repentance unto death: leave us not, the wretched ones, to die in our evil deeds, nor to become a laughing-stock unto the author of evil, the envious and destroying enemy. For Thou seest, O Compassionate One, how great are his plots and enmity against us, and how great is our passionateness, laxity, and carelessness. But work Thine unfailing goodness upon us who offend Thee every day and hour by the transgression of Thy venerable and life-creating commandments. And all our transgressions of our whole past life, even unto the present hour, whether in deeds or (cont. on fol. 42v) *Sunday Midnight Office*

...words, or thoughts: remit, forgive. And vouchsafe us to finish the remainder of our life in repentance and compunction, and in the keeping of Thy holy commandments; whether enticed by pleasure we have sinned in manifold ways, or seduced by vile desires, unprofitable and harmful, we have spent our days; whether stirred up by irrational wrath and anger we have grieved any brother of ours; whether by the tongue we have been ensnared in inevitable, crooked, and strong nets; whether by any of our senses, or by all; willingly or unwillingly; knowingly or unknowingly; through rashness and thoughtless delusion (cont. on fol. 43r) *Sunday. [Fol. 43r]*

...we have slipped; or if by evil and vain thoughts we have defiled conscience; or if in any other manner we have sinned, driven by the bent and custom of evil: forgive us and remit all things, O All-Compassionate, All-Good, and Plenteously Merciful One; and grant us for the future vigilance and power to do Thy good, acceptable, and perfect will; that having transformed the nocturnal and dark evil into light-bearing manifestation, walking becomingly as in the day, we may appear cleansed, unworthy though we be, unto Thy love for mankind, singing praise unto Thee and magnifying [Thee] unto all ages.

*Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (twice). Lord, bless.*
*Priest [gives] the Dismissal:*
> **Priest:** "He Who rose from the dead..."
*And we [say]:*
> **Choir:** "Amen."

*Sunday Midnight Office*

*And the Mutual Forgiveness takes place. Then the Litany: Let us pray for our God-preserved... written in the Daily Midnight Office: (see fol. 26).[^224]*
*After this the Hegumen [says]:*
**Hegumen:** Through the prayers of the holy...
*Or if there is no hegumen, then the Priest whose turn it is.*
*And the Acts of the Apostles is read by the Canonarch.*
*When he has said the inscription, the Priest [says]:*
> **Priest:** "Through the prayers of our holy fathers..."[^225]
*It should be known that on the Holy Mount of Athos, immediately after the Canon to the Trinity they sing: It is truly meet... [and the Megalynarion: The Sovereign Ruler of the Cherubim...]; Trisagion; and after the Our Father, the Priest [says]: For Thine is the kingdom... Then the Hypakoe of the Tone; and Lord, have mercy (40 times); Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim... and the rest, and the Dismissal.*[^226]

*(Engraved headpiece: Symmetrical Renaissance strapwork with a central vase and luxuriant acanthus foliage)*

## THE BEGINNING OF MATINS

*The Priest says:*
> **Priest:** "Blessed is our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."
*And we say:*
> **Choir:** "Amen."
*Come, let us worship (3 times): (see fol. 5).[^227]*
*At the end, Psalm of David 19:*[^228]

*Psalm 19:*
The Lord hear thee in the day of trouble; the name of the God of Jacob defend thee.
Send thee help from the sanctuary, and strengthen thee out of Zion.
Remember all (cont. on fol. 44v) *The Order of Matins*

...thy sacrifice, and make fat thy whole-burnt offering.
The Lord grant thee according to thy heart, and fulfill all thy counsel.
We will rejoice in thy salvation, and in the name of the Lord our God will we be magnified.
The Lord fulfill all Thy petitions.
Now know I that the Lord has saved His anointed; He will hear him from His holy heaven, in powers is the salvation of His right hand.
Some [trust] in chariots, and some in horses, but we will call upon the name of the Lord our God.
They are overthrown and fallen, but we are risen and set upright.
O Lord, save the king, and hear us in what day soever we call upon Thee.

*Psalm of David 20:*[^229]

*Matins. [Fol. 45r]*

O Lord, in Thy power the king shall be glad, and in Thy salvation he shall rejoice exceedingly.
Thou hast given him the desire of his heart, and hast not withholden the request of his lips.
For Thou hast prevented him with blessings of goodness; Thou hast set a crown of precious stone upon his head.
He asked life of Thee, and Thou gavest him length of days unto age of age.
Great is his glory in Thy salvation; glory and majesty shalt Thou lay upon him.
For Thou wilt give him blessing unto age of age; Thou wilt gladden him with joy by Thy countenance.
For the king trusts in the Lord, and through the mercy of the Most High he shall not be moved.
Let Thy hand be found (cont. on fol. 45v) *The Order of Matins*

...unto all Thine enemies; let Thy right hand find out all them that hate Thee.
For Thou shalt make them as a fiery furnace in the time of Thy countenance.
The Lord shall trouble them in His wrath, and the fire shall devour them.
Their fruit shalt Thou destroy from the earth, and their seed from among the sons of men.
For they intended evil against Thee; they imagined devices which they were not able to perform.
For Thou shalt make them turn their back; in Thy remnants Thou wilt prepare their face.
Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and hymn Thy powers.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Trisagion. And after the Our Father:*
*These Troparia, Tone 1:*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
O Lord, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting (cont. on fol. 46r) *Matins. [Fol. 46r]*

...victory unto our Orthodox king over adversaries, and preserving Thy commonwealth by Thy Cross.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross of Thine own will, bestow Thy compassions upon the new commonwealth named after Thee, O Christ God; gladden with Thy power our Orthodox king, granting him victory over adversaries, having Thine alliance, an armor of peace, an invincible trophy.

*Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
O awesome and unashamed Champion, disdain not our prayers, O good and all-hymned Birthgiver of God: establish the estate of the Orthodox, and save those whom Thou hast commanded to rule, and grant them from heaven victory, O thou who alone art blessed, having given birth unto God. (cont. on fol. 46v) *The Order of Matins*

*Lord, have mercy (12 times).*
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Glory to God in the highest...*
*But if a Priest is present, he says a Little Litany, for the king only:*[^230]
> **Priest:** "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy."
> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy. *Thrice.*"
> **Priest:** "Again we pray for our pious and God-preserved king."
*And immediately the Exclamation:*
> **Priest:** "For a merciful God and Lover of mankind art Thou..."
*And after this, he proclaims:*
> **Priest:** "Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."
> **Choir:** "Amen."
*The appointed brother reads the Six Psalms:*

*Matins. [Fol. 47r]*

*gently, quietly, and with attention:*
*Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men. (This is said 3 times).*
*Then:*
*O Lord, Thou shalt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise. (Say this twice).*
*Then, the Six Psalms. And the endings of all these Six Psalms are uttered in a quiet undertone:*[^231]

*A Psalm of David, when he fled from the face of Absalom his son. 3:*[^232]
*Psalm 3:*
Lord, why are they multiplied that afflict me?
Many rise up against me.
Many say unto my soul: There is no salvation for him in his God.
But Thou, O Lord, art my helper, my glory, and the lifter up of my head. (cont. on fol. 47v) *The Six Psalms*

...and the lifter up of my head.
With my voice I cried unto the Lord, and He heard me out of His holy mountain.
I laid me down and slept; I awoke, for the Lord will help me.
I will not be afraid of ten thousands of people that set themselves against me round about.
Arise, O Lord; save me, O my God, for Thou hast smitten all them that without cause are mine enemies; the teeth of sinners hast Thou broken.
Salvation is of the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people.

*I laid me down and slept; I awoke, for the Lord will help me.*

*A Psalm of David, for a remembrance, concerning the Sabbath. 37:*[^233]

*Psalm 37:*
O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, neither chasten me in Thy wrath.
For Thine arrows (cont. on fol. 48r) *Matins. [Fol. 48r]*

...are fastened in me, and Thou hast pressed Thy hand upon me.
There is no healing in my flesh because of Thy wrath; neither is there any peace in my bones because of my sins.
For mine iniquities are gone over my head; as a heavy burden they have weighed heavily upon me.
My wounds are grown foul and corrupt because of my foolishness.
I am become wretched, and bowed down to the end; all the day long I went mourning.
For my loins are filled with mockings, and there is no healing in my flesh.
I am afflicted and humbled exceedingly; I have roared by reason of the groaning of my heart.
Lord, before Thee is all my desire, and my groaning is not hidden from Thee.
My heart is trou- (cont. on fol. 48v) *The Six Psalms*

...bled, my strength has failed me; and as for the light of mine eyes, it also is not with me.
My friends and my neighbors drew near and stood opposite me; and my kinsmen stood afar off.
And they that sought after my soul used violence; and they that sought evils for me spoke vanities, and imagined deceits all the day long.
But I, as a deaf man, heard not; and I was as a dumb man that opens not his mouth.
And I was as a man that hears not, and that has no reproofs in his mouth.
For in Thee, O Lord, have I hoped; Thou wilt hear, O Lord my God.
For I said: Lest at any time mine enemies rejoice over me; and when my feet were moved, they spoke boastful words against me.
For I am ready for wounds, (cont. on fol. 49r) *Matins. [Fol. 49r]*

...and my sorrow is continually before me.
For I will declare mine iniquity, and take thought for my sin.
But mine enemies live, and are made stronger than I; and they that hate me wrongfully are multiplied.
They that render me evil for good slandered me, because I pursued goodness.
Forsake me not, O Lord; O my God, depart not from me.
Attend unto my help, O Lord of my salvation.

*Repeat:*
Forsake me not, O Lord; O my God, depart not from me. Attend unto my help, O Lord of my salvation.

*A Psalm of David, when he was in the wilderness of Judah. 62:*[^234] (cont. on fol. 49v) *The Six Psalms*

*Psalm 62:*
O God, my God, unto Thee do I rise early at dawn.
My soul has thirsted for Thee, how often has my flesh [longed] for Thee, in a dry and barren and waterless land.
So have I appeared before Thee in the sanctuary, to see Thy power and Thy glory.
For Thy mercy is better than life; my lips shall praise Thee.
So will I bless Thee while I live; I will lift up my hands in Thy name.
Let my soul be satisfied as with marrow and fatness, and my mouth shall praise Thee with joyful lips.
If I remembered Thee upon my bed, I meditated on Thee in the morning watches.
For Thou hast been my helper, and in the shelter of Thy wings will I rejoice. (cont. on fol. 50r) *Matins. [Fol. 50r]*

...My soul has cleaved after Thee; Thy right hand has upheld me.
But they that sought after my soul in vain shall go into the nether parts of the earth.
They shall be delivered into the hands of the sword; they shall be a portion for foxes.
But the king shall rejoice in God; everyone that swears by Him shall be praised, for the mouth of them that speak unjust things has been stopped.

*Repeat:*
I meditated on Thee in the morning watches, for Thou hast been my helper; and in the shelter of Thy wings will I rejoice. My soul has cleaved after Thee; Thy right hand has upheld me.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Alleluia (3 times), without bows.*[^235]
*Lord, have mercy (3 times).*
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*A Song of a Psalm for the sons of Korah. 87:*[^236] (cont. on fol. 50v) *The Six Psalms*

*And then the Priest goes forth from his place, and says in secret the customary [morning] prayers before the icon of the Master Christ.*[^237]

*Psalm 87:*
O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried by day, and in the night before Thee.
Let my prayer come before Thee; incline Thine ear unto my supplication.
For my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades.
I am counted with them that go down into the pit; I am become as a man without help, free among the dead,
like the slain that sleep in graves, whom Thou rememberest no more, and they are cast off from Thy hand.
They have laid me in the lowest pit, in dark places (cont. on fol. 51r) *Matins. [Fol. 51r]*

...and in the shadow of death.
Thy wrath is pressed hard upon me, and Thou hast brought all Thy waves upon me.
Thou hast put away mine acquaintance far from me; they have made me an abomination unto themselves; I was delivered up, and I went not forth.
Mine eyes failed by reason of poverty.
I cried unto Thee, O Lord, all the day long; I stretched forth my hands unto Thee.
Wilt Thou work wonders for the dead, or shall physicians raise them up, that they may give thanks unto Thee?
Shall any declare Thy mercy in the grave, and Thy truth in destruction?
Shall Thy wonders be known in the dark, and Thy righteousness in a land that is forgotten?
But unto Thee have I cried, O Lord, and in the morning my prayer shall prevent Thee.
Why, O Lord, dost Thou cast off my soul, [and] turnest away (cont. on fol. 51v) *The Six Psalms*

...Thy face from me?
I am poor and in troubles from my youth; and having been exalted, I was brought low and exhausted.
Thy wrath has passed over me, and Thy terrors have troubled me; they encompassed me like water, all the day long they hemmed me in together.
Thou hast put far from me friend and neighbor, and mine acquaintances because of my misery.

*Repeat:*
O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried by day, and in the night before Thee. Let my prayer come before Thee; incline Thine ear unto my supplication.

*A Psalm of David. 102:*[^238] (cont. on fol. 52r) *Matins. [Fol. 52r]*

*Psalm 102:*
Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me bless His holy name.
Bless the Lord, O my soul, and forget not all His benefits:
Who forgives all thine iniquities, Who heals all thine infirmities, Who redeems thy life from corruption;
Who crowns thee with mercy and compassions, Who satisfies thy desire with good things.
Thy youth shall be renewed like an eagle's.
The Lord executes deeds of mercy, and judgment for all that are wronged.
He made known His ways unto Moses, His will unto the sons of Israel.
Compassionate and merciful is the Lord, long-suffering and plenteous in mercy.
He will not be angry unto the end, neither will He be wrathful for ever. (cont. on fol. 52v) *The Six Psalms*

...He has not dealt with us according to our iniquities, nor rewarded us according to our sins.
For according to the height of heaven from the earth, the Lord has established His mercy over them that fear Him.
As far as the east is from the west, so far has He removed our iniquities from us.
Like as a father pities his sons, so has the Lord pitied them that fear Him.
For He knows our frame, He remembers that we are dust.
As for man, his days are as the grass; as a flower of the field, so shall he flourish.
For the wind passes over it, and it is gone, and the place thereof shall know it no more.
But the mercy of the Lord is from age to age upon them that fear Him, and His righteousness unto sons (cont. on fol. 53r) *Matins. [Fol. 53r]*

...of sons, to such as keep His testament, and remember His commandments to do them.
The Lord has prepared His throne in heaven, and His kingdom rules over all.
Bless the Lord, all ye His angels, mighty in strength, that perform His word, to hear the voice of His words.
Bless the Lord, all ye His hosts, ye ministers of His that do His will.
Bless the Lord, all ye His works, in every place of His dominion; bless the Lord, O my soul.

*Repeat:*
In every place of His dominion, bless the Lord, O my soul.

*A Psalm of David, when his son [Absalom] pursued him. 142:*[^239] (cont. on fol. 53v) *The Six Psalms*

*Psalm 142 (cont.):*
Lord, hear my prayer; give ear unto my supplication in Thy truth;
hear me in Thy righteousness, and enter not into judgment with Thy servant, for in Thy sight shall no man living be justified.
For the enemy has persecuted my soul; he has brought down my life to the earth;
he has made me to dwell in darkness, as those that have been long dead.
And my spirit was despondent within me; my heart within me was troubled.
I remembered days of old, I meditated on all Thy works, I pondered on the works of Thy hands.
I stretched forth my hands unto Thee; my soul is unto Thee as a waterless land.
Hear me speedily, O Lord; my spirit has failed.
Turn not away Thy face (cont. on fol. 54r) *Matins. [Fol. 54r]*

...from me, lest I be like unto them that go down into the pit.
Cause me to hear Thy mercy in the morning, for in Thee have I hoped.
Cause me to know, O Lord, the way wherein I should walk, for unto Thee have I lifted up my soul.
Deliver me from mine enemies, O Lord; unto Thee have I fled for refuge; teach me to do Thy will, for Thou art my God.
Thy good Spirit shall lead me into the land of uprightness.
For Thy name's sake, O Lord, shalt Thou quicken me; in Thy righteousness shalt Thou bring my soul out of affliction;
and in Thy mercy shalt Thou destroy mine enemies, and perish all them that afflict my soul, for I am Thy servant.

*Repeat:*
Hear me in Thy righteousness, O Lord, hear me, and enter not into judgment with Thy servant. (cont. on fol. 54v) *The Six Psalms*

...Thy good Spirit shall lead me into the land of uprightness.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Alleluia (3 times).*
*Lord, have mercy (12 times).*
*The Priest or Deacon says the Great Litany:*[^240]
> **Deacon:** "In peace let us pray to the Lord."
*And the Priest exclaims:*
> **Priest:** "For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit."
*Then:*
> **Choir:** "God is the Lord, and has appeared unto us; blessed is he that comes in the name of the Lord."
*In the Tone of the Troparion.*
*Verse 1:* O give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures for ever.[^241]
*Verse 2:* Encompassing me they encompassed me, but in the name of the Lord I drove them back.
*Verse 3:* (cont. on fol. 55r) *The Order. [Fol. 55r]*

...I shall not die, but live, and declare the works of the Lord.
*Verse 4:* The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner; this is the Lord's doing, and it is marvelous in our eyes.
*And to each verse is chanted the refrain:* Blessed is he that comes in the name of the Lord.
*And the choirs after each verse sing:* God is the Lord, and has appeared... *in full to the end, in the Tone of the Troparion.*
*Then, the Troparion of the day, or of the saint, or of the feast.*
*But when it is a Fast, then in place of: God is the Lord, and has appeared... is sung: Alleluia, in the Tone of the Oktoechos.*[^242]
*And the Cantor says these verses: Verse 1:* (cont. on fol. 55v) *Matins*

From night my spirit awakens early unto Thee, O God.
*Verse 2:* Learn righteousness, ye inhabitants of the earth.
*Verse 3:* Bring evils upon them, O Lord; bring evils upon them, even the glorious ones of the earth.
*Verse 4:* So were we unto Thy beloved.
*And after each verse is sung:*
> **Choir:** "Alleluia (3 times)."
*Then they sing the Hymns to the Trinity of the same Tone.*[^243]

*(Engraved headpiece: Symmetrical Renaissance foliate strapwork with scrolling acanthus)*

## HYMNS TO THE TRINITY OF THE EIGHT TONES[^244]

*Tone 1:*
By the childlike forms of the bodiless Powers, being led upward unto the noetic and immaterial Mind, and receiving the radiance of the thrice-composed Godhead with a threefold song, cherubically we cry out: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

With all the heavenly Powers, cherubically unto Him Who is in the highest we cry out, sending up thrice-holy praise: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Having arisen from sleep, we fall down before Thee, O Good One, and the angelic hymn we cry out unto Thee, O Mighty One: Holy, (cont. on fol. 56v) *Matins*

...Holy, Holy art Thou, O God: for the sake of the Birthgiver of God, have mercy on us.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*[^245]
O Lord Who hast shone forth the light, cleanse my soul from every sin, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*It is incumbent to say on Monday:*
By the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.
*On Tuesday:*
By the prayers of Thy Forerunner, and save me.
*On Wednesday and Friday:*
By the power of Thy Cross, and save me.
*On Thursday:*
By the prayers of Thine Apostles, and save me.

*Tone 2:*
O Uncreated Nature, Framer of all, (cont. on fol. 57r) *Hymns to the Trinity. [Fol. 57r]*

...open Thou our lips, that we may declare Thy praise, crying aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Imitating the highest Powers, we earthly ones offer the triumphal hymn unto Thee, O Good One: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

From bed and sleep Thou hast raised me up, O Lord; enlighten my mind and heart, and open my lips to sing unto Thee, O Holy Trinity: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
Send forth Thy co-eternal light, O Lord, and enlighten the secret eyes of my heart, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*Tone 3:*
The Unoriginate Father, and the Co-unoriginate Son, and the Co-eternal Spirit, one Godhead: (cont. on fol. 57v) *Matins*

...daring cherubically to hymn Thy glory, we say: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

O Trinity consubstantial and undivided, Unity in three hypostases and co-eternal: unto Thee as God we cry aloud the angelic hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Suddenly the Judge will come, and the deeds of each will be laid bare; but with fear let us cry aloud at midnight: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
Send forth Thy light, O Christ God, and enlighten my heart, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*Tone 4:*
Thy Unoriginate Father, and Thee, O Christ God, and Thy Most-holy Spirit, daring cherubically to hymn (cont. on fol. 58r) *Hymns to the Trinity. [Fol. 58r]*

...the glory, we say: Holy, Holy, Holy [art Thou, O God].[^246]

As the angelic ranks now in the heavens, and the assemblies of mankind with fear on the earth, we offer the triumphal hymn unto Thee, O Good One: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Imitating Thine intelligent servants,[^247] we mortals offer Thee the hymn, daring to say: Holy, Holy, Holy [art Thou, O God].

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
O Thou Who hast shone forth light unto Thy world, cleanse my soul held fast in darkness from every sin, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*Tone 5:*
It is the time for singing and the hour for prayer; diligently let us cry aloud unto the one God: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God. (cont. on fol. 58v) *Matins*

...daring to form an image of Thine intelligent hosts, O all-unoriginate Trinity, with unworthy lips we cry aloud unto Thee: Holy, Holy, Holy [art Thou, O God].

O Thou Who wast contained within the virginal womb, yet wast not separated from the Father's bosom, receive also us with the angels, O Christ God, who cry aloud unto Thee: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
O Giver of light, O Lord, send forth Thy light, and enlighten my heart, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*Tone 6:*
With bodiless lips, with unceasing doxologies, the six-winged ones sing unto Thee the Thrice-holy hymn, O our God; and we who are on earth, with unworthy lips, send up praise unto Thee: (cont. on fol. 59r) *Hymns to the Trinity. [Fol. 59r]*

...Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Standing before Thee with fear, the Cherubim, and trembling with dread, the Seraphim, offer the Thrice-holy hymn with unceasing voice; with them also we sinners cry aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

The Trinity which is a Unity in Godhead, in unconfused oneness let us glorify, and cry aloud the angelic hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
By the intercession of the bodiless powers, O Lord, send down upon our souls Thy co-eternal light.

*Tone 7:*
Unto the unapproachable in Godhead, the Trinity in Unity,[^248] sending up the Seraphic Thrice-holy (cont. on fol. 59v) *Matins*

...praise, with fear let us cry aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Having laid aside sluggish sleep, O my soul, show forth an amendment unto rising up before the Judge, and with fear cry aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

O Thou Who by the highest power art sung cherubically, and with divine glory art adored angelically, receive also us sinners who cry aloud unto Thee with compunction: Holy, Holy, Holy [art Thou, O God].

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
By the protection of the bodiless powers, O Lord, send down light unto our souls, and great mercy.

*Tone 8:*
Having our hearts lifted up to heaven, let us assimilate ourselves to the angelic rank, and with fear falling down before (cont. on fol. 60r) *Hymns to the Trinity. [Fol. 60r]*

...the Judge, sending up the triumphal praise: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Not daring to gaze upon Thee, the Cherubim, flying, call out with acclaim the divine hymn of the Thrice-holy voice; with them also we sinners cry aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God.

Humbled by the multitude of our transgressions, and not daring to look up unto Thy height, bowing down soul with body, with the angels we cry aloud unto Thee: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God: for the sake of the Birthgiver of God, have mercy on us.

*Exaposteilarion (Exapostilarion):*
Being Thyself the Light, O Christ, enlighten me through Thyself, by the intercession of the bodiless powers, and save me.

*After this, the usual stichology of the Psalter.*[^249] (cont. on fol. 60v) *Matins*

...and the sessional hymns. Then, Psalm 50: *Have mercy on me, O God* (see fol. 6).[^250] After this, the Odes and Canons. At the 9th Ode of the Canon, we sing:

*The Canticle to the Most-holy Birthgiver of God, from Luke:*
My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit has rejoiced in God my Savior.
For He has looked upon the low estate of His handmaiden; for, behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed.
For He that is mighty has done to me great things, and holy is His name; and His mercy is from generation to generation upon them that fear Him.
He has shown strength with His arm; He has scattered the proud in the imagination of their hearts.
He has put down the mighty from their thrones, and exalted them of low degree; He has filled the hungry with good things, (cont. on fol. 61r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 61r]*

...and the rich He has sent empty away.
He has helped His servant Israel, in remembrance of His mercy, as He spoke to our fathers, to Abraham and his seed unto the age.[^251]

*To each verse we sing the refrain:*
*More honorable than the Cherubim...*

*Then, the 9th Ode of the Canon. And after the Canon, we sing in Tone 6:*
It is truly meet to bless thee, the Birthgiver of God, ever-blessed and all-blameless, and the Mother of our God.
*More honorable than the Cherubim... (see fol. 22).[^252]*

*And we all make a prostration together unto the ground. Then, the Exaposteilaria.*

*After this: Psalm 148:*[^253] (cont. on fol. 61v) *Matins*

*Psalm 148:*
Praise the Lord from the heavens; praise Him in the heights.
Praise Him, all His angels; praise Him, all His powers.
Praise Him, sun and moon; praise Him, all the stars and light.
Praise Him, ye heavens of heavens, and the water that is above the heavens; let them praise the name of the Lord.
For He spoke, and they came into being; He commanded, and they were created.
He established them unto the age, and unto ages of ages; He has made a decree, and it shall not pass away.
Praise the Lord from the earth, ye dragons and all abysses;
Fire, hail, snow, ice, stormy wind, fulfilling His word;
Mountains and all hills, fruitful trees and all cedars;
Beasts and all cattle, creeping things and winged birds;
Kings of the earth and all peoples, (cont. on fol. 62r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 62r]*

...princes and all judges of the earth;
Young men and virgins, elders with youths: let them praise the name of the Lord, for His name alone is exalted.
His thanksgiving is upon earth and in heaven, and He shall exalt the horn of His people;
A hymn for all His holy ones, for the children of Israel, a people drawing near unto Him.

*Psalm 149:*[^254]
Sing unto the Lord a new song; His praise is in the assembly of the holy ones.
Let Israel rejoice in Him that made him, and let the sons of Zion be glad in their King.
Let them praise His name in the choir; let them sing unto Him with timbrel and psaltery.
For the Lord takes pleasure (cont. on fol. 62v) *Matins*

...in His people, and He will exalt the meek unto salvation.
The holy ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.
The high praises of God are in their throat, and two-edged swords in their hands;
To execute vengeance among the nations, punishments among the peoples;
To bind their kings with chains, and their nobles with iron fetters of the hands;
To execute among them the judgment written: this glory shall be unto all His holy ones.

*Psalm 150:*
Praise God in His holy ones; praise Him in the firmament of His power.
Praise Him for His mighty acts; praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness.
Praise Him (cont. on fol. 63r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 63r]*

...with the sound of the trumpet; praise Him with psaltery and harp.
Praise Him with timbrel and choir; praise Him with strings and organs.
Praise Him with tuneful cymbals; praise Him with cymbals of jubilation. Let every breath praise the Lord.

*After this, the Stichera, if there be any; but if not, we say:*
*Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.*
*Glory to God in the highest, all to the end. But if it be a fast, we say this:*
To Thee glory is due, O Lord our God, and unto Thee we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.* 3 prostrations.[^255] (cont. on fol. 63v) *Matins*

Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men.
We praise Thee, we bless Thee, we worship Thee, we glorify Thee, we give thanks to Thee for Thy great glory.
O Lord, heavenly King, God the Father Almighty; and O Lord, the Only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ; and O Holy Spirit.
O Lord God, Lamb of God, Son of the Father, that takest away the sins of the world, have mercy on us; Thou that takest away the sins of the world, receive our prayer; Thou that sittest at the right hand of the Father, have mercy on us.
For Thou only art holy, Thou only art the Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.
Every day will I bless Thee, and I will praise Thy name unto the age, and unto ages of ages.
Lord, Thou hast been our refuge from generation to generation. (cont. on fol. 64r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 64r]*

...I said: O Lord, have mercy on me; heal my soul, for I have sinned against Thee.
Lord, unto Thee have I fled: teach me to do Thy will, for Thou art my God; for with Thee is the fountain of life: in Thy light shall we see light.
Continue Thy mercy unto them that know Thee.
Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this day without sin.
Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages. Amen.
Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have set our hope on Thee.
Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach us Thy statutes.
Blessed art Thou, O Master: make us understand Thy statutes.[^256]
Blessed art Thou, O Holy One: (cont. on fol. 64v) *Matins*

...enlighten us with Thy statutes.
Lord, Thy mercy is unto the ages; despise not the works of Thy hands.
To Thee praise is due, to Thee a song is due, to Thee glory is due: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*After this, the Litany:* **"Let us complete our morning prayer..."** *Then, at the Aposticha, the Stichera from the Octoechos. And we say the verses:*

*Verse 1:*
We have been satisfied in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we have rejoiced and been glad. In all our days we have been glad, for the days wherein Thou hast humbled us, for the years wherein we have seen evils; and look upon Thy servants (cont. on fol. 65r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 65r]*

...and upon Thy works, and guide their sons.

*Verse 2:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and establish Thou the works of our hands upon us, yea, establish Thou the work of our hands.

*And thereafter: Glory... Both now... Theotokion. Then:*
It is a good thing to give thanks unto the Lord, and to sing praises unto Thy name, O Most High; to proclaim Thy mercy in the morning, and Thy truth by night.

*If it be the Great Fast, we say it twice. Then, the Trisagion. And after Our Father: Troparion of the Saint or of the Day. Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone. And the Litany. And the Dismissal. And immediately the First Hour is said.*
*But if it be the Fast, then we say this Troparion:*[^257] (cont. on fol. 65v) *Matins*

Standing in the temple of Thy glory, we seem to stand in heaven; O Birthgiver of God, heavenly gate, open unto us the doors of thy mercy.

*Then:*
Lord, have mercy (40). Lord, bless.

> **Priest:** "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God, always, now and ever..."

*And we say:*
O Heavenly King, strengthen our faithful sovereigns; confirm the faith; subdue the nations; pacify the world; and well preserve this holy Monastery; and settle our fathers and brethren who have gone before us in the tabernacles of the righteous; and receive us in the Orthodox faith and repentance, O Lord, and have mercy, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Then:* Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father]:* (cont. on fol. 66r) *The Order of Services. [Fol. 66r]*

> **Priest:** *Through the prayers of our holy fathers... (see fol. 27).[^258]*

*And we say the Prayer of Saint Ephrem secretly:*
*O Lord and Master of my life, a spirit of sloth... (see fol. 22), making 3 great prostrations, and 12 other [small bows], saying to ourselves:* "O Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, have mercy on me, a sinner." *And after this we say the aforesaid prayer in its entirety, and the twelfth prostration.*

*And immediately we begin the First Hour.* (cont. on fol. 66v)

---

# THE ORDER OF THE FIRST HOUR

## THE BEGINNING OF THE FIRST HOUR[^259]

*O come, let us worship... (3).*

*Psalm 5:*
Give ear to my words, O Lord; consider my cry.
Attend unto the voice of my supplication, my King and my God; for unto Thee will I pray, O Lord.
In the morning Thou shalt hear my voice; in the morning I will stand before Thee and look up; for Thou art not a God that has pleasure in wickedness.
Neither shall the evil-doer dwell with Thee, nor shall the transgressors continue before Thine eyes. (cont. on fol. 67r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 67r]*

...Thou hast hated all them that work iniquity; Thou shalt destroy all them that speak a lie.
The Lord abhors the man of blood and deceit.
But as for me, in the multitude of Thy mercy I will enter into Thy house; I will worship toward Thy holy temple in Thy fear.
Lead me, O Lord, in Thy righteousness, because of mine enemies; make my way straight before Thee.
For there is no truth in their mouth; their heart is vain; their throat is an open sepulchre; with their tongues they have dealt deceitfully. Judge them, O God;
Let them fall from their counsels; cast them out according to the multitude of their ungodliness, for they have provoked Thee, O Lord.
And let all them that hope in Thee rejoice; they shall be glad unto the ages, and (cont. on fol. 67v) *The First Hour*

...Thou shalt dwell in them, and all they that love Thy name shall boast in Thee. For Thou, O Lord, wilt bless the righteous; Thou hast crowned us as with a shield of good pleasure.

*A Prayer of Moses, the Man of God. Psalm 89:*[^260]
Lord, Thou hast been our refuge from generation to generation.
Before the mountains were brought forth, or the earth and the world were formed, even from age to age Thou art.
Turn not man away unto humiliation; for Thou hast said: Turn again, ye sons of men.
For a thousand years in Thine eyes, O Lord, are but as yesterday when it is past, and as a watch in the night.
Their years shall be things of naught: in the morning as grass (cont. on fol. 68r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 68r]*

...it shall pass away. In the morning let it bloom and pass away; in the evening let it fall, become hardened, and wither.
For we have consumed away in Thy wrath, and in Thine anger have we been troubled.
Thou hast set our iniquities before Thee; our lifetime into the light of Thy countenance.
For all our days are failed, and in Thine anger we have consumed away.
Our years were reckoned as a spider's web; the days of our years are seventy years, and if we be in strength, eighty years, and what is more than these is labor and sorrow.
For meekness is come upon us, and we shall be chastened.
Who knows the power of Thy wrath, and from Thy fear to number Thine anger?
So make known to me Thy right hand, (cont. on fol. 68v) *The First Hour*

...and them that are chained in heart to wisdom.
Turn Thee, O Lord, how long? And be Thou entreated for Thy servants.
We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad.
In all our days let us be glad, for the days wherein Thou hast humbled us, for the years wherein we have seen evils.
And look upon Thy servants and upon Thy works, and guide their sons.
And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct Thou the works of our hands upon us; yea, the work of our hands direct Thou.

*Psalm of David, 100:*[^261]
Of mercy and judgment will I sing unto Thee, O Lord.
I will sing and have understanding in a blameless way, when Thou shalt come unto me. (cont. on fol. 69r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 69r]*

...I walked in the innocence of my heart in the midst of my house.
I set no unlawful thing before mine eyes; them that commit transgressions I hated.
A crooked heart cleaved not unto me; the deceitful man who turned away from me I knew not.
Whoso secretly slandered his neighbor, him I drove away.
With him that had a proud eye and an insatiable heart I did not eat.
Mine eyes were upon the faithful of the earth, that they might sit with me; he that walked in a blameless way, this one ministered unto me.
He that works pride dwelt not in the midst of my house; he that speaks unjust things directed not his way before mine eyes.
In the morning I slew all the sinners of the earth, (cont. on fol. 69v) *The First Hour*

...to destroy out of the city of the Lord all them that work iniquity.

Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). Lord, have mercy (3). Glory...

*Troparion of the Day, or of the Saint. Both now...:*
*What shall we call thee... But if it be a fast, we say this Troparion, in Tone 6:*[^262]
In the morning hear my voice, O my King and my God.
*Verse 1:* Give ear to my words, O Lord.
*Verse 2:* For unto Thee will I pray, O Lord.

*Prostrations: 3. Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
What shall we call thee, O thou that art full of grace? Heaven, for thou hast shone forth the Sun of righteousness; Paradise, for thou hast blossomed the flower of incorruption; Virgin, for thou didst remain incorrupt; pure Mother, for (cont. on fol. 70r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 70r]*

...thou hadst in thy holy arms a Son, Who is the God of all: entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

Order my steps according to Thy word, and let not any iniquity have dominion over me. Deliver me from the slander of men, and I will keep Thy commandments. Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thy statutes.

Let my mouth be filled with praise, that I may sing of Thy glory and of Thy majesty all the day long.

*Trisagion. And after Our Father: Kontakion of the Saint, or of the Day.*

*When it is a fast, we say these Troparia, in Tone 4:*[^263]
Speedily prevent us before we are enslaved by enemies that blaspheme Thee and threaten us, O Christ our God; but overcome by Thy Cross them that wage war (cont. on fol. 70v) *The First Hour*

...that fight against us, that they may understand how powerful is the Orthodox faith, through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, O Thou only Lover of mankind.

Glory...
*Troparion:*
O Guide of wisdom and Giver of understanding, Instructor of the unwise and Defender of the poor: confirm and give understanding to my heart, O Master. Give me speech, O Thou Word of the Father, Only-begotten; for behold, I will not refrain my lips from crying unto Thee, O Merciful One: have mercy on me that am fallen.

Both now... *Theotokion:*
The all-glorious Mother of Christ, and more holy than the holy angels, let us unceasingly praise with heart and lips, confessing thee to be the Birthgiver of God, as having truly brought forth for us God incarnate, and praying unceasingly for our souls. (cont. on fol. 71r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 71r]*

...Lord, have mercy (40).
*Thou Who at every time and every hour...*
Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "God be bountiful unto us, and bless us..."
*Then this prayer:*[^264]
O Christ, the true Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that comes into the world: let the light of Thy countenance be marked upon us, that walking in it we may behold the unapproachable light; and direct our steps to the fulfilling of Thy commandments, through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother and of all Thy saints. Amen.
Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3). Lord, bless.
*And the final Dismissal.*
*If it be a fast, after 'God be bountiful unto us' we make 3 great (cont. on fol. 71v) The First Hour*

...prostrations, with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem: *O Lord and Master of my life... (see fol. 22).[^265]* After this, 11 other small bows, saying secretly: *'O Lord Jesus Christ, Son [of God, have mercy on me, a sinner].'* And we say also this: *'O God, be merciful unto me, a sinner. O God, cleanse my sins and have mercy on me. Without number have I sinned, O Lord, forgive me.'* And again we say the whole aforesaid prayer: *O Lord and Master of my life...* and the twelfth great prostration.
Then, the Trisagion. And after Our Father: Lord, have mercy (12). And the prayer: *O Christ, the true Light...*
Then the **Priest:** Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.
And we: Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3). Lord, bless.
*And the usual Dismissal.* (cont. on fol. 72r)

## THE MID-HOUR OF THE FIRST HOUR[^266]

*If thou wishest to chant in church, then after the prayer 'O Christ, the true Light...' and after the dismissal, we begin:* O come, let us worship... (3). *If it be a fast, 3 prostrations.*
*But let this also be known: that the Mid-Hour of the First Hour we never chant in church; but after the dismissal of Matins and the First Hour in church, having gone each to his cell, and having made the Trisagion and after Our Father:* O come, let us worship... (3). *Then:*
*To the end, for the sons of Korah, concerning secret things. Psalm 45:*[^267] (cont. on fol. 72v) *The Mid-Hour of the First Hour*

God is our refuge and strength, a help in afflictions that have found us exceedingly.
Therefore will we not fear when the earth is troubled, and when mountains are removed into the hearts of the seas.
Their waters roared and were troubled; the mountains were troubled by His strength.
The rushing of the river gladdens the city of God; the Most High has sanctified His tabernacle.
God is in the midst thereof, and it shall not be moved; God shall help it early in the morning.
The nations were troubled, the kingdoms tottered; the Most High uttered His voice, the earth shook.
The Lord of hosts is with us; our defender is the God of Jacob.
Come and see the works of God, (cont. on fol. 73r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 73r]*

...what wonders He has wrought upon the earth.
He makes wars to cease unto the ends of the earth; He will crush the bow, and shatter the weapon, and burn the shields with fire.
Be still, and know that I am God; I will be exalted among the nations, I will be exalted in the earth.
The Lord of hosts is with us; our defender is the God of Jacob.

*A Psalm of a Song on the Sabbath Day, 91:*[^268]
It is a good thing to give thanks unto the Lord, and to sing praises unto Thy name, O Most High; to proclaim Thy mercy in the morning, and Thy truth every night; upon a psaltery of ten strings, with a song upon the harp.
For Thou, O Lord, hast made me glad through Thy work, and in the works of Thy hands will I rejoice. For (cont. on fol. 73v) *The Mid-Hour of the First Hour*

...Thy works are magnified, O Lord; Thy thoughts are made very deep.
A senseless man shall not know, and a fool shall not understand this:
When the sinners spring up like the grass, and all the workers of iniquity appear, that they may be destroyed unto ages of ages.
But Thou, O Lord, art the Most High unto the ages.
For lo, Thine enemies, O Lord, for lo, Thine enemies shall perish, and all the workers of iniquity shall be scattered.
But my horn shall be exalted like the horn of a unicorn, and mine old age shall be anointed with rich oil.
And mine eye has looked upon mine enemies, and mine ear shall hear of the wicked that rise up against me.
The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon, (cont. on fol. 74r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 74r]*

...those that are planted in the house of the Lord, shall flourish in the courts of our God.
They shall still increase in a ripe old age, and they shall be full of vigor, to proclaim that the Lord our God is upright, and there is no unrighteousness in Him.

*For the Sabbath Day, when the earth was first inhabited. A Praise of a Song by David, 92:*[^269]
The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty; the Lord is robed in strength, and has girt Himself.
For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken.
Thy throne is prepared from of old; Thou art from everlasting.
The floods have lifted up, O Lord, the floods have lifted up their voices;
The floods will lift up their waves with the voices (cont. on fol. 74v) *The Mid-Hour of the First Hour*

...of many waters. Marvelous are the billows of the sea; marvelous in the heights is the Lord.
Thy testimonies are made very sure; holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.

Glory... Both now... *without Alleluia. Trisagion. If it be a fast, 3 prostrations. And after Our Father, these Troparia, in Tone 6:*[^270]
Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us; for, destitute of all defense, we sinners offer this supplication unto Thee as Master: have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us.

Glory...
Lord, have mercy on us, for in Thee have we put our trust; be not exceedingly wroth with us, neither remember our iniquities, but look upon us even now as Thou art compassionate, and deliver us from our enemies; (cont. on fol. 75r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 75r]*

...for Thou art our God, and we are Thy people; and we are all the work of Thy hands, and we call upon Thy name.

Both now... *Theotokion:*
Open unto us the doors of thy loving-kindness, O blessed Birthgiver of God and Virgin; hoping in thee, let us not perish, but by thee let us be delivered from tribulations, for thou art the salvation of the Christian race.

Lord, have mercy (30).[^271] Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."
*And we make 3 great prostrations, if it be a fast, with the prayer: O Lord and Master of my life... (see fol. 22). And immediately this prayer, of Saint Basil the Great:*[^272] (cont. on fol. 75v) *The Mid-Hour of the First Hour*

O Eternal, Unoriginate, and Ever-existing Light, Maker of all creation, Fountain of mercy, Ocean of goodness, and Unsearchable Abyss of the love of mankind: show forth upon us the light of Thy countenance. Shine forth, O Lord, in our hearts the noetic Sun of righteousness, and fill our souls with Thy gladness; and teach us to proclaim Thy judgments, and to meditate continually to confess unto Thee, our Master and Benefactor. Direct the works of our hands unto Thy will, and prosper us to do that which is well-pleasing and beloved unto Thee, that even by us unworthy ones Thy most holy name may be glorified, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, of one Godhead (cont. on fol. 76r) *The First Hour. [Fol. 76r]*

...and Kingdom: unto Whom is due all glory, honor, and worship, unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Another Prayer, of the same [Saint Basil]:*[^273]
O Thou Who sendest forth light, and it goes, Who makest the sun to rise upon the righteous and the unrighteous, upon the evil and the good, Who makest the morning and enlightenest the whole world: enlighten our hearts, O Master of all, grant us on this present day to be well-pleasing unto Thee, preserving us from all sin and from every evil deed, delivering us from every arrow that flies by day, and from every adverse power, through the prayers of our most holy Lady, the Birthgiver of God, and of Thine immaterial ministers, and of all (cont. on fol. 76v) *The Mid-Hour of the First Hour*

...the saints that from the ages have been well-pleasing unto Thee. For Thine it is to have mercy and to save us, O our God, and unto Thee we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Lord, have mercy (2).[^274] Lord, bless. *And there is the final Dismissal. And with the dismissal we say:*
The Lord God receive the supplications of us sinners, and have mercy on us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind. (cont. on fol. 77r)

---

# THE ORDER OF THE THIRD HOUR

## THE BEGINNING OF THE THIRD HOUR[^275]

*If alone in the cell, we begin thus:*
Through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother, and of all the saints, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*This verse is said also at every beginning when there is no priest. But if we chant the Third Hour and the Sixth Hour in church, then it is chanted in the narthex, with their Mid-Hours. When the priest has made the exclamation, we say:* Amen. *Trisagion.* (cont. on fol. 77v) *The Third Hour*

*Trisagion. All-holy Trinity, have mercy on us. Our Father... Lord, have mercy (12). Glory... Both now... O come, let us worship... (3). Then:*

*A Prayer of David, 16:*[^276]
Hear, O Lord, my righteousness; attend unto my supplication. Give ear unto my prayer, that is not in deceitful lips.
Let my judgment come forth from Thy presence; let mine eyes behold uprightness.
Thou hast proved my heart; Thou hast visited it by night; Thou hast tried me by fire, and no unrighteousness has been found in me.
That my mouth may not speak of the works of men, for the sake of the words of Thy lips I have kept hard ways.
Order Thou my steps in (cont. on fol. 78r) *The Third Hour. [Fol. 78r]*

Thy paths, that my footsteps be not shaken.
I have cried, for Thou hast heard me, O God; incline Thine ear unto me, and hear my words.
Make marvelous Thy mercies, O Thou that savest them that hope in Thee.
From them that resist Thy right hand keep me, O Lord, as the apple of an eye.
In the shelter of Thy wings Thou wilt cover me, from the face of the ungodly that have afflicted me.
Mine enemies have compassed my soul; they have enclosed their own fat; their mouth has spoken pride.
They that cast me out have now encircled me; they have set their eyes to bow down unto the earth.
They have caught me like a lion ready for prey, and like a lion's whelp lurking in secret places.
Arise, O Lord, prevent them, (cont. on fol. 78v) *The Third Hour*

and cast them down. Deliver my soul from the ungodly: Thy sword from the enemies of Thy hand, O Lord.
From the few from the earth, divide them in their life, and of Thy hidden things their belly was filled.
They were satisfied with children, and have left the remnants unto their infants.
But as for me, in righteousness shall I appear before Thy face; I shall be satisfied when Thy glory shall appear unto me.

*A Psalm of David, 24:*[^277]
Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul; O my God, in Thee have I trusted; let me not be put to shame unto the ages, neither let mine enemies laugh me to scorn.
For all they that wait for Thee shall not be put to shame; let them be put to shame that act (cont. on fol. 79r) *The Third Hour. [Fol. 79r]*

iniquitously in vain.
Show me Thy ways, O Lord, and teach me Thy paths.
Lead me into Thy truth, and teach me, for Thou art God my Savior, and for Thee have I waited all the day.
Remember Thy compassions, O Lord, and Thy mercies, for they are from the ages.
The sins of my youth and of mine ignorance remember not; according to Thy mercy remember Thou me, for Thy goodness' sake, O Lord.
Good and upright is the Lord; therefore will He set a law for them that sin in the way.
He will guide the meek in judgment, He will teach the meek His ways.
All the ways of the Lord are mercy and truth, unto them that seek His covenant and His testimonies.
For Thy name's sake, O Lord, also pardon my sin, (cont. on fol. 79v) *The Third Hour*

for it is great.
What man is he that fears the Lord? He will set him a law in the way that he has chosen.
His soul shall abide in good things, and his seed shall inherit the earth.
The Lord is the strength of them that fear Him, and His covenant will He make manifest unto them.
Mine eyes are ever toward the Lord, for He will pluck my feet out of the snare.
Look upon me and have mercy on me, for I am an only-begotten and poor.
The afflictions of my heart are multiplied; bring me out of my distresses.
Behold my humility and my travail, and forgive all my sins.
Behold mine enemies, for they are multiplied, and with an unrighteous hatred have they hated me.
Keep my soul and deliver me; let me not be put to shame, for (cont. on fol. 80r) *The Third Hour. [Fol. 80r]*

I have hoped in Thee.
The innocent and the upright have cleaved unto me, for I have waited for Thee, O Lord.
Deliver Israel, O God, out of all his afflictions.

*Psalm 50:*[^278]
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy... *Written in the Midnight Office (see fol. 6).[^279]*

Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... *Troparion of the occurring day, or of the Saint. Both now... Theotokion: 'O Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine...' But when it is a fast, we chant these Troparia, in Tone 6:*[^280]

*Troparion:* O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit at the third hour upon Thine Apostles: Him, O Good One, take not away from us, but renew Him in us who pray unto Thee.

*Verse 1:* (cont. on fol. 80v) *The Third Hour*

*Verse 1:* Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within my inward parts.
*Verse 2:* Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.
*And at these verses we make one prostration at each, singing the refrain:* 'O Lord, Who didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit...' *the whole unto the end. Then: Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:* O Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine that didst bud forth for us the Fruit of life. Thee do we entreat: intercede, O Lady, together with the holy Apostles, that our souls may find mercy.

Blessed is the Lord God, blessed is the Lord day by day; the God of our salvation shall make our way prosperous; our God is the God to save.[^281] Then, Trisagion. And after (cont. on fol. 81r) *The Third Hour. [Fol. 81r]*

Our Father: *Kontakion of the day, or of the Saint. If it be a Feast of the Lord, then of the Feast. But if it be a fast, then we say these Troparia, in Tone 8:*[^282]

*Troparion, Tone 8:* Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who didst reveal the fishermen to be most wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them didst draw the net over the universe; O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee.

Glory...
*Kontakion, Tone 8:* When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all into unity, that with one accord we may glorify the All-holy Spirit.

Both now... *Theotokion:* Hope and protection and refuge of Christians, an invincible wall, for them that faint and travail unto the (cont. on fol. 81v) *The Third Hour*

sea, a calm haven, art thou, O most pure Birthgiver of God; but as thou deliverest the world by thine unceasing prayer, remember us, O all-hymned Virgin.

*Another Theotokion, if thou wishest. Kontakion from the Akathist to the Most Holy Birthgiver of God:*[^283]
*Kontakion, Tone 8:* To thee, the champion leader, we thy servants ascribe thank-offerings of victory, for we have been delivered from evil, O Birthgiver of God. But as thou hast invincible might, deliver us from all dangers, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O unwedded Bride!

Lord, have mercy (40). Then, the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: *Thou Who at every time...* Then: Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the (cont. on fol. 82r) The Third Hour. [Fol. 82r]*

*Cherubim...* and one bow. Then: *In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "God be bountiful unto us..."
*All these things are written in the Midnight Office (see fol. 22).[^284] And if it be a fast, we say secretly the prayer of Saint Ephrem: 'O Lord and Master of my life...' with prostrations, even as was indicated in the First Hour (see fol. 74).[^285] Then, this prayer of Saint Mardarios: 'O Master God, Father Almighty...' Written in the Midnight Office (see fol. 22).[^286]* (cont. on fol. 82v)

## THE MID-HOUR OF THE THIRD HOUR[^287]

*It is fitting to know that the Mid-Hour of the Third Hour, and of the Sixth, is always chanted in church, except when there is a vigil, or a Saint who has a Great Doxology; likewise also on Saturday and on Sunday.*
*The beginning is likewise as that of the First Hour:* O come, let us worship... (3).

*A Psalm of David, A Song at the Dedication of the House, 29:*[^288]
I will exalt Thee, O Lord, for Thou hast lifted me up, and hast not made mine enemies to rejoice over me.
O Lord my God, I cried unto Thee, and Thou hast healed me.
O Lord, Thou hast brought (cont. on fol. 83r) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour. [Fol. 83r]*

up my soul from hades; Thou hast saved me from them that go down into the pit.
Sing unto the Lord, O ye His venerable ones, and confess the remembrance of His holiness.
For wrath is in His anger, and life in His good will; weeping may lodge at evening, and joy in the morning.
But as for me, I said in my prosperity, I shall never be moved.
O Lord, by Thy will give strength unto my beauty.
But Thou didst turn away Thy face, and I was troubled.
Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, and unto my God will I make supplication.
What profit is there in my blood, when I go down to corruption?
Shall the dust confess unto Thee, or declare Thy truth?
The Lord heard, and had mercy on me; the Lord was made my helper.
Thou hast turned (cont. on fol. 83v) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour*

my mourning into joy for me; Thou hast rent my sackcloth, and girded me with gladness;
that my glory may sing unto Thee, and I may not be pierced with sorrow.
O Lord my God, I will confess unto Thee unto the ages.

*A Psalm of David, of Instruction, 31:*[^289]
Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered.
Blessed is the man unto whom the Lord imputes no sin, and in whose mouth there is no deceit.
For I kept silence, mine old bones waxed old through my crying all the day long.
For day and night Thy hand was heavy upon me; I was turned into misery when the thorn was pierced into me.
My transgression have I acknowledged, and my sin (cont. on fol. 84r) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour. [Fol. 84r]*

have I not hidden.
I said: I will confess against myself mine iniquity unto the Lord; and Thou forgavest the ungodliness of my heart.
For this shall every venerable one pray unto Thee in a seasonable time; yet in a flood of many waters they shall not come near unto him.
Thou art my refuge from the affliction that encompasses me; O my Joy, deliver me from them that have encircled me.
I will instruct thee, and teach thee in this way wherein thou shalt go; I will fix Mine eyes upon thee.
Be ye not as the horse and the mule, which have no understanding; whose jaws thou must hold with bit and bridle, lest they come near unto thee.
Many are the scourges of the sinner; but mercy (cont. on fol. 84v) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour*

shall encompass him that hopes in the Lord.
Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous, and boast, all ye upright of heart.

*To the end, in songs, A Psalm of David, 60:*[^290]
Hear my supplication, O God, attend unto my prayer.
From the ends of the earth have I cried unto Thee, when my heart was in despondency; Thou hast lifted me up upon a rock.
Thou hast guided me, for Thou hast been my hope, a tower of strength from the face of the enemy.
I will dwell in Thy tabernacle unto the ages; I will be covered in the shelter of Thy wings.
For Thou, O God, hast heard my prayers; Thou hast given an inheritance unto them that fear Thy name.
Days unto days of the king shalt Thou add, (cont. on fol. 85r) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour. [Fol. 85r]*

his years unto the day of generation and generation.
He shall abide unto the age before God; as for His mercy and truth, who shall search them out?
So will I sing unto Thy name unto the ages, that I may pay my vows day by day.

Then: Glory... Both now... Trisagion. Prostrations (3). And after Our Father: *these Troparia, in Tone 4:*

*Troparion, Tone 4:* O God of our fathers, Who dealest with us ever according to Thy gentleness: take not Thy mercy away from us, but by their intercessions direct our life in peace.[^291]

Glory...
*Troparion of the Martyrs, Tone 4:* Thy Martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received crowns of incorruption from Thee, our God; for having Thine invincible strength, they cast down the tormentors; they shat- (cont. on fol. 85v) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour*

tered the feeble insolence of the demons. By their prayers, save our souls.

Both now... *Theotokion:* Thou art an invincible wall unto us Christians, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; for fleeing unto thee, we remain unhurt; and when again we sin, we have thee as an intercessor. Therefore giving thanks we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

Lord, have mercy (30). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."
*And we make 3 great prostrations, if it be a fast, with the prayer: 'O Lord and Master of my life...' (see fol. 22).[^292] And after this, this prayer, of Saint Basil the Great:*[^293] (cont. on fol. 86r) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour. [Fol. 86r]*

O Lord our God, Who hast given Thy peace unto men, and didst send down the gift of the Most Holy Spirit upon Thy disciples and apostles, and by Thy power didst open their lips with tongues of fire: open Thou also the lips of us sinners, and teach us how and for what we ought to pray. Direct our life, O calm haven of them that are tossed by tempests; and show us the way wherein we should walk. Renew a right spirit within our inward parts, and with a governing spirit establish the infirmity of our minds: that being guided every day by Thy good Spirit unto that which is profitable, we may be accounted worthy to fulfill Thy commandments, (cont. on fol. 86v) *The Mid-Hour of the Third Hour*

and ever to remember Thy glorious coming, which examines the deeds of men; and not to be deceived by the corrupting beauties of this world, but strengthen us to long for the enjoyment of future treasures. For Thou art blessed and praised by all Thy saints, unto ages of ages. Amen. (cont. on fol. 87r)

---

# THE ORDER OF THE SIXTH HOUR

## THE BEGINNING OF THE SIXTH HOUR[^294]

*O come, let us worship... (3).*
*And 3 prostrations. Then:*

*To the end, in songs of instruction, of David; when the Ziphites came and said to Saul: 'Doth not David hide himself with us?' Psalm 53:*[^295]
Save me, O God, in Thy name, and judge me in Thy power.
O God, hear my prayer, give ear to the words of my mouth.
For strangers have risen up against me, and the mighty have sought after my soul, and have not set God before (cont. on fol. 87v) *The Sixth Hour*

[before them.] For behold, God helps me, and the Lord is the protector of my soul.
He shall turn evil upon mine enemies; consume Thou them by Thy truth.
Willingly will I sacrifice unto Thee; I will confess unto Thy name, O Lord, for it is good.
For Thou hast delivered me out of every affliction, and mine eye has looked down upon mine enemies.

*To the end, in songs of instruction, of Asaph, 54:*[^296]
Give ear, O God, unto my prayer, and despise not my supplication; attend unto me, and hear me.
I was grieved in my sorrow, and was troubled by the voice of the enemy, and by the oppression of the sinner.
For they brought iniquity upon me, and in wrath they were hostile against me.
My heart (cont. on fol. 88r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 88r]*

was troubled within me, and the fear of death fell upon me.
Fear and trembling came upon me, and darkness covered me.
And I said: Who will give me wings like a dove? And I will fly away, and be at rest.
Lo, I have fled far off, and lodged in the wilderness.
I waited for God that saves me from faintheartedness and tempest.
Drown them, O Lord, and divide their tongues, for I have seen iniquity and gainsaying in the city.
Day and night it shall go round about it upon its walls; and iniquity and toil are in the midst thereof, and injustice;
and usury and deceit have not ceased from its streets.
For if an enemy had reproached me, I would have borne it;
and if he that hates me had magnified himself against me, I would have hidden myself (cont. on fol. 88v) *The Sixth Hour*

from him.
But it was thou, a man like-minded, my guide and my familiar friend,
who didst sweeten food together with me; in the house of God we walked in concord.
Let death come upon them, and let them go down alive into hades;
for wickedness is in their dwellings, in the midst of them.
As for me, I cried unto God, and the Lord heard me.
Evening, and morning, and at noonday, will I tell and declare it, and He shall hear my voice.
He shall deliver my soul in peace from them that draw near unto me, for they were in many with me.
God shall hear, and humble them, He that exists before the ages.
For there is no change for them, because they have not feared God.
He has stretched forth His hand in retribution; they have profaned (cont. on fol. 89r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 89r]*

His covenant. They were divided by the wrath of His countenance, and their hearts drew near.
Their words were softer than oil, yet are they darts.
Cast thy care upon the Lord, and He shall sustain thee; He shall not suffer the righteous to be shaken unto the ages.[^297]
But Thou, O God, shalt bring them down into the pit of corruption.
Men of blood and deceit shall not live out half their days;
but as for me, O Lord, I will hope in Thee.

*A Praise of a Song, of David, not inscribed among the Hebrews, 90:*[^298]
He that dwells in the help of the Most High shall abide in the shelter of the God of heaven.
He shall say unto the Lord: Thou art my protector and my refuge (cont. on fol. 89v) *The Sixth Hour*

my God, and in Him will I hope.
For He shall deliver thee from the snare of the hunters, and from troubling words.
He shall overshadow thee with His shoulders, and under His wings shalt thou hope; His truth shall encircle thee with a shield.
Thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night, nor for the arrow that flies by day,
nor for the thing that walks in darkness, nor for the encounter and the noonday demon.
A thousand shall fall at thy side, and ten thousand at thy right hand, but it shall not come near thee.
Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold, and see the reward of sinners.
For Thou, O Lord, art my hope; Thou hast made the Most High thy refuge.
No evil shall come unto thee, and no scourge shall draw near unto thy (cont. on fol. 90r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 90r]*

dwelling.
For He shall give His Angels charge over thee, to keep thee in all thy ways.
In their hands they shall bear thee up, lest at any time thou dash thy foot against a stone.
Thou shalt tread upon the asp and basilisk, and the lion and dragon shalt thou trample under foot.
Because he has set his hope upon Me, I will also deliver him; I will shelter him, because he has known My name.
He shall call upon Me, and I will hear him; I am with him in affliction; I will deliver him, and glorify him.
With length of days will I satisfy him, and show him My salvation.

Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... *Troparion of the day, or of the Saint. Both now... Theotokion:* 'Seeing that we have no boldness...'[^299]
*And if it be a fast, we say these Troparia, in Tone 6:*[^300] (cont. on fol. 90v) *The Sixth Hour*

*Troparion, Tone 6:* Thou Who on the sixth day and hour didst nail to the Cross the sin which Adam daringly committed in paradise: tear asunder also the handwriting of our sins, O Christ God, and save us.
*Verse 1:* As for me, I cried unto God, and the Lord heard me.
*Verse 2:* Evening, and morning, and at noonday, will I tell and declare it, and He shall hear my voice.
*And at these verses we make one prostration at each, singing the refrain:* 'Thou Who on the sixth day and hour...' *the whole unto the end. Then: Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Theotokion:* Seeing that we have no boldness because of our many sins, do thou entreat Him that was born of thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; for the supplication of a mother avails much unto the pleading of the Master. Despise not the supplications of sinners, O all-pure one; for He is merciful and able to save, Who willed to suffer (cont. on fol. 91r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 91r]*

for our sake.
Let Thy tender compassions speedily prevent us, O Lord, for we are become exceedingly poor. Help us, O God our Savior, for the sake of the glory of Thy name; O Lord, deliver us, and purge away our sins, for Thy name's sake.[^301]
Then, Trisagion. And after Our Father: *Kontakion of the day, or of the Saint. But if it be a fast, we say these Troparia, in Tone 2:*[^302]
*Troparion, Tone 2:* Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God, having stretched out Thine all-pure hands upon the Cross, gathering all the nations who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee!
Glory...
*Troparion, Tone 2:* We worship Thine all-pure icon, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our transgressions, O Christ God; for of Thine own will (cont. on fol. 91v) *The Sixth Hour*

Thou didst deign to ascend the Cross, that Thou mightest deliver them whom Thou hadst created from the bondage of the enemy. Therefore giving thanks we cry unto Thee: Having filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Thou art come to save the world!
Both now... *Theotokion:*
Being a well-spring of tender compassion, vouchsafe mercy unto us, O Birthgiver of God. Look down upon the people that have sinned; manifest as ever thy power; for hoping in thee, we cry unto thee: Rejoice! even as once did Gabriel, the supreme commander of the bodiless hosts.
*And if it be Wednesday or Friday, say this Stavrotheotokion:*[^303]
*Stavrotheotokion:* Most glorified art thou, O Birthgiver of God; we praise thee; for by the Cross of thy Son hades was overthrown, and death was slain; we that were dead have risen, (cont. on fol. 92r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 92r]*

and have been accounted worthy of life; we have received paradise, our ancient enjoyment. Therefore giving thanks we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

Lord, have mercy (40). Then, the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: *Thou Who at every time and at every [hour]...* Then: Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "Through the prayers of our holy fathers..."
*And if it be the Great Fast, make 3 great prostrations with the prayer of Saint Ephrem: 'O Lord and Master of my life...' and the other 12 [prostrations], even as was indicated in the First Hour (see fol. 74).[^304] Then, the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^305] (cont. on fol. 92v) *The Sixth Hour*
