O God and Lord of hosts, and Maker of all creation, Who through the tender compassion of Thine ineffable mercy didst send down Thine Only-begotten Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, for the salvation of our race, and by His precious Cross didst tear asunder the handwriting of our sins, and thereby didst triumph over the principalities and powers of darkness: do Thou Thyself, O Master, Lover of mankind, accept from us sinners these thanksgiving prayers, and deliver us from every all-destructive and dark transgression, and from all them that seek to harm us, enemies visible and invisible. Nail our flesh with the fear of Thee, and incline not our hearts unto words or reason- (cont. on fol. 93r) *The Sixth Hour. [Fol. 93r]*

ings of malice; but pierce our souls with Thy love, that looking ever unto Thee and guided by the light that is from Thee, beholding Thee, the unapproachable and everlasting Light, we may send up unceasing confession and thanksgiving unto Thee: the Father without beginning, with Thine Only-begotten Son, and Thine All-holy, and Good, and Life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And if it be a fast, then after the versicle, from the priest:* We make the 15 usual prostrations, according to what was previously indicated (see fol. 22).[^306] *After this, the Prayer:* 'O Master God, Father Almighty...' *(see fol. 22).[^307] And the Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour.* (cont. on fol. 93v)

## THE MID-HOUR OF THE SIXTH HOUR

*The beginning is likewise as that of the others:*
O come, let us worship... (3). And 3 prostrations.

*To the end, for the people that were removed far off from the holy things, by David, for a pillar-inscription, when the foreigners seized him in Gath, 55:*[^308]
Have mercy on me, O God, for man has trodden me down; fighting all the day long, he has afflicted me.
Mine enemies have trodden me down all the day long, for they be many that fight against me from on high.
By day I shall not fear, but I will hope in Thee. In God will I (cont. on fol. 94r) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour. [Fol. 94r]*

praise my words, in God have I hoped; I will not fear what flesh shall do unto me.
All the day long they abominated my words; all their thoughts were against me for evil.
They will dwell and hide; they will watch my heel,
even as they have waited for my soul. On no account shalt Thou deliver them; in anger shalt Thou bring down the peoples, O God.
My life have I declared unto Thee; Thou hast set my tears before Thee, even as in Thy promise.
Let mine enemies be turned back; in what day soever I shall call upon Thee, behold, I know that Thou art my God.
In God will I praise the saying, in the Lord will I praise the word;
in God have I hoped, I will not fear what man shall do unto me. In me, (cont. on fol. 94v) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour*

O God, are vows, which I will render in praise of Thee.
For Thou hast delivered my soul from death, mine eyes from tears, and my feet from falling;
I will be well-pleasing before the Lord in the light of the living.

*To the end, destroy not, by David, for a pillar-inscription, when he fled from the face of Saul into the cave, 56:*[^309]
Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me, for my soul has hoped in Thee;
and in the shadow of Thy wings will I hope, until iniquity pass away.
I will cry unto God Most High, unto God that has dealt bountifully with me.
He sent from heaven and saved me; He gave them to reproach that trod upon me.
God sent forth His mercy and His truth, and delivered my soul from the midst (cont. on fol. 95r) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour. [Fol. 95r]*

of lions' whelps; I fell asleep troubled.
As for the sons of men, their teeth are weapons and arrows, and their tongue a sharp sword.
Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory above all the earth.
They prepared a snare for my feet, and bowed down my soul;
they digged a pit before my face, and fell into it themselves.
Ready is my heart, O God, ready is my heart; I will sing and chant in my glory.
Awake up, my glory; awake, psaltery and harp; I myself will awake early.
I will confess Thee among the peoples, O Lord; I will chant unto Thee among the nations.
For Thy mercy is magnified unto the heavens, and Thy truth unto the clouds.
Be Thou exalted above the heavens, O God, and Thy glory above all the earth. (cont. on fol. 95v) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour*

*To the end, of David, in remembrance, that the Lord may save me, 69:*[^310]
O God, attend unto my help; O Lord, make haste to help me.
Let them be put to shame and confounded that seek after my soul.
Let them be turned back and brought to shame that wish me evil; let them be turned back immediately shameful, who say unto me: Aha, aha!
Let them be glad and rejoice in Thee, all they that seek Thee, O God;
and let them say continually: The Lord be magnified, who love Thy salvation.
But as for me, I am poor and needy; O God, help me.
Thou art my helper and my deliverer, O Lord, make no long tarrying. (cont. on fol. 96r) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour. [Fol. 96r]*

Glory... Both now... Trisagion, with three prostrations, if it be a fast. And after Our Father:
*These Troparia, in Tone 1:*
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance... *(see fol. 45).[^311]*
Glory...
*Troparion of the Martyrs, Tone 1:* By the sufferings of the Saints which they endured for Thy sake, be Thou entreated, O Lord; and heal all our sicknesses and passions, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee.
Both now...
*Theotokion, Tone 1:* Through the prayers, O Lord, of all the Saints and of the Birthgiver of God, give us Thy peace, and have mercy on us, as Thou alone art compassionate.

Lord, have mercy (30). Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].*
> **Priest:** "Through the prayers of our holy fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us."
*And we make three great prostrations, if it be a fast,* (cont. on fol. 96v) *The Mid-Hour of the Sixth Hour*

with the prayer: 'O Lord and Master of my life...' *(see fol. 22).[^312]* After this, the Prayer: 'O God and Lord of hosts, and Maker of all creation...' *Written at the end of the Sixth Hour (see fol. 92).[^313]*
Then, the Obednitsa. But when it is a fast, we first chant the Ninth Hour, and after that, the Obednitsa.[^314]
*(Decorative tailpiece vignette: mascaron with coronet and Baroque foliage)* (cont. on fol. 97r) *(Engraved headpiece: Christ the Man of Sorrows in the tomb with Cross and Passion instruments)*

---

# THE ORDER OF THE TYPIKA: WHICH IS THE OBEDNITSA

## THE ORDER OF THE TYPIKA: WHICH IS THE OBEDNITSA[^315]

*And its beginning we say thus:* Bless the Lord, O my soul; blessed art Thou, O Lord.
*Then, Psalm of David, 102:* Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me bless His holy name... *Written in the Six Psalms (see fol. 52).[^316]*
*And at the end we say:* Glory... Both now... *In a higher voice:* Bless the Lord, O my soul, (cont. on fol. 97v) *The Order of the Typika*

and all that is within me bless His holy name; blessed art Thou, O Lord.
*Then:* Glory...
*Alleluia. Of Haggai and Zechariah, 145:*[^317]
Praise the Lord, O my soul; I will praise the Lord in my life, I will chant unto my God while I have my being.
Hope not on princes, on the sons of men, in whom there is no salvation.
His spirit shall go forth, and he shall return to his earth; in that day all his thoughts shall perish.
Blessed is he whose helper is the God of Jacob, whose hope is in the Lord his God,
Who made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that is in them;
Who keeps truth unto eternity, Who executes judgment for the wronged, Who gives food to the hungry. (cont. on fol. 98r) *The Typika. [Fol. 98r]*

The Lord looses the fettered; the Lord makes wise the blind;
the Lord raises up the fallen; the Lord loves the righteous; the Lord preserves the sojourners;
He shall adopt the orphan and the widow, and the way of sinners He will destroy.
The Lord shall reign unto eternity; thy God, O Zion, unto generation and generation.
Both now...
Only-begotten Son and Word of God, Who art immortal, and didst deign for our salvation to become incarnate of the holy Birthgiver of God and Ever-Virgin Mary, and without change didst become man; and wast crucified, O Christ God, trampling down death by death; Who art One of the Holy Trinity, glorified together with the Father and the Holy Spirit: save us.[^318]
*Then, the Beatitudes:* (cont. on fol. 98v) *The Order of the Typika*

In Thy Kingdom when Thou comest, remember us, O Lord.
Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the Kingdom of heaven.
Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.
Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.
Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.
Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.
Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.
Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called sons of God.
Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the Kingdom of heaven. (cont. on fol. 99r) *The Typika. [Fol. 99r]*

Blessed are ye when they shall reproach you, and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil word against you falsely, for My sake.
Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in the heavens.
Glory... Both now...
*After this, the Epistle and the Gospel.[^319] Then:*
Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.
Remember us, O Master, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.
Remember us, O Holy One, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.
The heavenly choir chants unto Thee and says: Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord of Sabaoth, heaven (cont. on fol. 99v) *The Order of the Typika*

and earth are full of Thy glory. *Verse:*
Come unto Him and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.[^320]
The heavenly choir chants unto Thee and says: Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord of Sabaoth, heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.
Glory...
The choir of Holy Angels and Archangels, with all the heavenly powers, chants unto Thee and says: Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord of Sabaoth, heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.
Both now...
*The Confession of the Orthodox Faith, of the First and Second Ecumenical Councils:*[^321]
I believe in one God, the Father Almighty... *Written in the Midnight Office (see fol. 14).[^322]*
*Then, the Prayer:* (cont. on fol. 100r) *The Typika. [Fol. 100r]*

Remit, pardon, forgive, O God, our offenses, voluntary and involuntary, in word and in deed, in knowledge and in ignorance, in mind and in thought, by day and by night: forgive us all, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.
*After this: Our Father. Kontakion of the day, or of the Saint; but if there be none for the Saint, then of the Temple. Glory...*
With the Saints give rest, O Christ... *(see fol. 24).[^323]*
*Both now...*
O Protection of Christians unashamed, Mediatress unto the Creator unwavering: despise not the voices of prayer of us sinners, but anticipate, as thou art good, to our help, who faithfully cry unto thee; hasten to intercession (cont. on fol. 100v) *The Order of the Typika*

and make speed to supplication, thou that ever protectest, O Birthgiver of God, them that honor thee.
Lord, have mercy (12).
And: *All-holy Trinity, consubstantial...* and the rest.
*If it be a Fast, we say:* Lord, have mercy (40).
Glory... Both now... *More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord [bless, father].* And we make 3 great prostrations, and the rest, as has been indicated many times in the preceding.[^324] *After these, the Trisagion. And after Our Father:* Lord, have mercy (12). *Then, this Prayer:*
All-holy Trinity, consubstantial, sovereign dominion, and undivided Kingdom, the cause of all good things: be well-pleased at this present hour toward me, a sinner; and wash away all my defilements, and enlighten my (cont. on fol. 101r) *The Typika. [Fol. 101r]*

mind, that I may ever chant, and glorify, and say: One is Holy, One is the Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.
*Then:*
Blessed be the name of the Lord, from henceforth and unto the age. *(Say this 3 times. If it be a fast, also 3 prostrations.)*
*Then: Glory... Both now...*
*Psalm of David, when he changed his countenance before Abimelech, and he let him go, and he departed, 33:*[^325]
I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall continually be in my mouth.
In the Lord shall my soul be praised; let the meek hear and be glad.
Magnify the Lord with me, and let us exalt (cont. on fol. 101v) *The Order of the Typika*

His name together.
I sought the Lord, and He heard me, and delivered me from all my afflictions.
Draw near unto Him and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.
This poor man cried, and the Lord heard him, and saved him out of all his afflictions.
The Angel of the Lord shall encamp round about them that fear Him, and shall deliver them.
Taste and see that the Lord is good; blessed is the man that hopes in Him.
Fear the Lord, all ye His saints, for there is no want to them that fear Him.
The rich have become poor and suffered hunger, but they that seek the Lord shall not want any good thing.
Come, ye children, give ear unto me; I will teach you the fear of the Lord.
What man is he that desires life, loving (cont. on fol. 102r) *The Typika. [Fol. 102r]*

to see good days?
Keep thy tongue from evil, and thy lips from speaking guile.
Turn away from evil, and do good; seek peace, and pursue it.
The eyes of the Lord are upon the righteous, and His ears are open unto their prayer.
But the face of the Lord is against them that do evil, to root out the remembrance of them from the earth.
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them, and delivered them out of all their tribulations.
The Lord is nigh unto them that are of a contrite heart, and He will save the humble of spirit.
Many are the tribulations of the righteous, but the Lord shall deliver them out of them all.
The Lord keeps all their bones; not one of them shall be broken.
The death of sinners is evil; and they that hate the righteous shall do amiss. (cont. on fol. 102v) *The Order of the Typika*

The Lord will redeem the souls of His servants, and none of them that hope in Him shall do amiss.
*Then:*
It is truly meet to bless thee... *(see fol. 74).[^326]*
More honorable than the Cherubim... *(see fol. 22).[^327]*
Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (20). Lord, bless.
*And the Dismissal.*

*WHEN GOING TO THE REFECTORY,*[^328]
*we say this Psalm:*
*Praise, of David, 144:*[^329]
I will exalt Thee, O my God, my King, and I will bless Thy name unto eternity, and unto ages of ages.
Every day will I bless Thee, and I will praise Thy name unto eternity, and unto ages of ages.
Great is the Lord, and (cont. on fol. 103r) *Before the Meal. [Fol. 103r]*

greatly to be praised, and of His greatness there is no end.
Generation and generation shall praise Thy works, and shall declare Thy power.
They shall speak of the magnificence of the glory of Thy holiness, and shall tell of Thy marvelous works.
And they shall speak of the power of Thy terrible acts, and shall tell of Thy greatness.
They shall pour forth the memory of the abundance of Thy goodness, and shall rejoice in Thy righteousness.
Compassionate and merciful is the Lord, longsuffering and of great mercy.
The Lord is good to all, and His compassions are over all His works.
Let all Thy works confess Thee, O Lord, and let Thy righteous ones bless Thee.
They shall speak of the glory of Thy Kingdom, and talk of Thy power, to make known to the sons (cont. on fol. 103v) *Thanksgiving*

of men Thy power, and the glory of the magnificence of Thy Kingdom.
Thy Kingdom is a Kingdom of all the ages, and Thy dominion is in every generation and generation.
Faithful is the Lord in all His words, and holy in all His works.
The Lord upholds all that are falling, and raises up all that are bowed down.
The eyes of all look with hope unto Thee, and Thou givest them their food in due season.
Thou openest Thy hand, and fillest every living thing with favor.
Righteous is the Lord in all His ways, and holy in all His works.
The Lord is nigh unto all that call upon Him, unto all that call upon Him in truth.
He will fulfill the desire of them that fear (cont. on fol. 104r) *Before the Meal. [Fol. 104r]*

Him, and He will hear their prayer, and will save them.
The Lord preserves all them that love Him, and all sinners He will destroy.
My mouth shall speak the praise of the Lord, and let all flesh bless His holy name, unto eternity and unto ages of ages.

*When the Psalm is ended, and the Abbot and the brethren have arrived at the refectory: if they are to elevate the Panagia,[^330] the Priest says:*
> **Priest:** "Through the prayers of our holy fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us."
*After this:*
Today is the beginning of our salvation, and the manifestation of the mystery from eternity: the Son of God becomes the Son of the Virgin, and Gabriel announces the good tidings of grace. Therefore let us also cry aloud with him to the Birthgiver of God: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee.[^331] (cont. on fol. 104v) *Thanksgiving*

*Then the Abbot or the weekly server says:*[^332]
Our Father... Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (20). Bless.
*The Priest blesses the food and drink, saying:*
> **Priest:** "O Christ God, bless the food and drink for Thy servants, for Thou art holy always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."
*And we all say:* Amen.
*If there be no priest, we say after the Lord, have mercy (20), 'Lord, bless', then:*
Through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother... *(see fol. 77).[^333]*
*The eating ought to take place with much silence, and with attention to the reading of the Divine Scriptures.*

*AND WHEN WE ARISE FROM THE REFECTORY,*
*we elevate the Panagia thus:* (cont. on fol. 105r) *After the Meal. [Fol. 105r]*

*The Cellarer proclaims in a loud voice, or the Refectorian:*[^334]
Through the prayers of our holy fathers...
*The clerics say:* Amen. *Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (20). Lord, bless. Then we say:*
Blessed is God, Who has mercy upon us and nourishes us from our youth, Who gives food to all flesh; fill our hearts with joy and gladness, that always having all sufficiency, we may abound in every good work, in Christ Jesus our Lord;
with Whom unto Thee is due glory, dominion, honor, and worship, with the Holy Spirit, unto ages. Amen.
Glory to Thee, O Lord; glory to Thee, O Holy One; glory to Thee, O King; for Thou hast given us meats for gladness: fill us also with the Holy Spirit, that (cont. on fol. 105v) *Thanksgiving*

we may be found well-pleasing before Thee, and not put to shame, when Thou shalt render unto each according to his works.
Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3). Lord, bless.
*Then he that is to elevate the Panagia makes a bow and says:*
Bless me, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner.
*And they that stand by say:*
May God forgive thee and have mercy on thee.
*Then, taking the bread that lies upon the panagiarion with three fingers of both hands, having elevated it slightly, he says in a loud voice:*[^335]
Great is the name! *And we say:* Of the Holy Trinity. *And again with the bread making the sign of the cross over the panagiarion,* (cont. on fol. 106r) *After the Meal. [Fol. 106r]*

*he says thus:* All-holy Birthgiver of God, help us!
*And we say:* Through her prayers, O Christ God, have mercy on us and save us.
*And we partake of the Panagia [the Most Pure bread], one by one.*[^336]
*And the Deacon reads:* All generations bless thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God... *Written in the Midnight Office (see fol. 24).[^337] And after this, the clerics chant:*
All generations bless thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, most blessed and most undefiled Mother of our God: thee, O Birthgiver of God, all generations bless, for thou dost pray for us to Christ our God.
*Then:* It is truly meet... *(see fol. 74).[^338]* And: More honorable than the Cherubim... *(see fol. 22).[^339]*
*But if it be a Feast of the Lord: we chant the Heirmos of the 9th Ode.*
*Then the Priest says:* (cont. on fol. 106v) *Thanksgiving*

Unto the abundant supplications of our most blessed Lady, the Birthgiver of God and Ever-Virgin Mary.
*And we:* Through her prayers, O God, have mercy on us and save us.
*And we drink after the Panagia.[^340] Then:*
The Lord is merciful and compassionate; He has given food to them that fear Him.
*Then the Trisagion. After Our Father: Kontakion of the Feast, or of the commemorated Saint; but if there be none, the Hypakoe; but on all ordinary days, we say these Troparia:*[^341]
We thank Thee, O Christ our God, for Thou hast filled us with Thy terrestrial good things; deprive us not of Thy heavenly Kingdom, but as Thou camest into the midst of Thy disciples, O Savior, giving them peace, come also unto us and save us.
*Glory...* O God of our fathers... *(see fol. 85).[^342]*
*Both now...* Through the prayers, O Lord, of all the Saints... *(see fol. 96).[^343]* (cont. on fol. 107r) *After the Meal. [Fol. 107r]*

Lord, have mercy (3). Lord, bless.
> **Priest:** "Blessed is God, Who has mercy upon us and nourishes us from His bountiful gifts, by His grace and love for mankind, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen."
*Afterwards, the litany for the Fathers and Founders, and others whom he wishes, he commemorates.[^344] And we:*
May God forgive and have mercy.
*If there be no priest, we give thanks after the meal thus:*
Blessed is God, Who has mercy upon us and nourishes us from our youth... *Then:* Glory to Thee, O Lord; glory to Thee, O Holy One... *We sing:* We thank Thee, O Christ our God... *Then:* Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3). Lord, bless. *Then:* Through the prayers of Thy most pure [Mother]... *(see fol. 77).[^345]*

*A TALE FROM THE CHRONICLER*
*Concerning the Elevation of the All-Holy [Panagia]: How, and When It Was, and for What Reason.*[^346]

After the awesome Resurrection of our Savior Jesus Christ, and the Descent of the Holy Spirit, and even unto the dispersal of the holy Apostles unto preaching, all the Apostles were together in one accord. And whenever, after prayer, they sat down at the meal: they would leave an empty place, upon which they placed a cushion, and upon the cushion a portion of bread, from that which they ate, in honor of Christ. And after the meal, arising, having prayed and given thanks, they would take up the portion of bread which they had set apart in honor of the Lord. And elevating this, they would say: Glory to Thee, (cont. on fol. 108r) *Concerning the Same. [Fol. 108r]*

O our God, glory to Thee; Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. And instead of saying 'Great is the name', they would say: 'Christ is risen', even unto the Ascension. Thereafter: 'Great is the name of the Holy Trinity; O Lord Jesus Christ, help us.' And these things they performed thus all the year round. And each of them did this also wherever they happened to be, even unto the Dormition of the Birthgiver of God. And when, for the universal Dormition of our all-holy Lady the Birthgiver of God, they were gathered upon the clouds, and had accomplished what was meet; and when, after her burial, they were eating on the third day, and after the meal were elevating according to custom the portion of bread lying in the name of Christ, and proclaiming the Great Name: (*O most glorious wonder!*)[^347] (cont. on fol. 108v) *Tale concerning the Same*

that alive upon a cloud, with light-bearing Angels attending her, she appears in the air, saying unto them: 'Rejoice, for I am with you all the days; this gift have I received from my Son.' And the disciples, marveling at the miracle, cried aloud instead of 'O Lord Jesus Christ': 'All-holy Birthgiver of God, help us!' After this, having come to the tomb, and not finding her most pure body, they were assured of a truth that alive with her body, having on the third day arisen from the dead as also did her Son, she had passed over unto the heavenly realms.[^348] (cont. on fol. 109r) *The Beginning of the Hours. [Fol. 109r]*
*The Ninth Hour*

*We sing the Ninth Hour thus, when it is sung apart from the other Hours. After the blessing of the Priest, we begin: O Heavenly King... Trisagion. All-holy Trinity... Our Father... Lord, have mercy (12). O come, let us worship... (3). Then:*
*To the end, concerning the winepresses, for the sons of Korah, a Psalm, 83:*[^349]
How beloved are Thy dwellings, O Lord of hosts! My soul longs and faints for the courts of the Lord; my heart and my flesh have rejoiced in the living God. For the bird (cont. on fol. 109v) *The Ninth Hour*

has found herself a house, and the turtledove a nest for herself, where she may lay her young: Thine altars, O Lord of hosts, my King, and my God. Blessed are they that dwell in Thy house; unto ages of ages they will praise Thee. Blessed is the man whose help is from Thee; he has set ascents in his heart, in the vale of weeping, in the place which he has appointed. For the lawgiver will give blessings; they shall go from strength to strength; the God of gods shall appear in Zion. O Lord God of hosts, hear my prayer; give ear, O God of Jacob. Behold, O God our protector, and look upon the face of Thine Anointed. For better is one day in Thy courts than thousands (cont. on fol. 110r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 110r]*

elsewhere. I have chosen rather to be cast down in the house of my God than to dwell in the tents of sinners. For the Lord loves mercy and truth; God will give grace and glory; the Lord will not withhold good things from them that walk in innocence. O Lord God of hosts, blessed is the man that hopes in Thee.

*To the end, for the sons of Korah, a Psalm, 84:*[^350]
Thou hast been well-pleased, O Lord, with Thy land; Thou hast turned back the captivity of Jacob. Thou hast forgiven the iniquities of Thy people; Thou hast covered all their sins. Thou hast made all Thy wrath to cease; Thou hast turned away from the wrath of Thine indignation. Turn us back, O God of our salvations, and turn away Thine anger from us. (cont. on fol. 110v) *The Ninth Hour*

Wilt Thou be angry with us unto the ages? Or wilt Thou stretch out Thy wrath from generation to generation? O God, Thou wilt turn and quicken us, and Thy people shall rejoice in Thee. Show us Thy mercy, O Lord, and grant us Thy salvation. I will hear what the Lord God will speak in me: for He will speak peace unto His people, and unto His saints, and unto them that turn their hearts unto Him. Surely His salvation is near unto them that fear Him, that glory may dwell in our land. Mercy and truth have met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other. Truth has sprung out of the earth, and righteousness has looked down from heaven. For the Lord will give goodness, and our land shall yield her fruit. Righteousness shall go before Him, (cont. on fol. 111r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 111r]*

and shall set His steps in the way.

*A Prayer of David, 85:*[^351]
Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me, for poor and needy am I. Preserve my soul, for I am holy; save Thy servant, O my God, that hopes in Thee. Have mercy on me, O Lord, for unto Thee will I cry all the day. Rejoice the soul of Thy servant, for unto Thee have I lifted up my soul. For Thou, O Lord, art good, and longsuffering, and plenteous in mercy unto all them that call upon Thee. Give ear, O Lord, unto my prayer, and attend unto the voice of my supplication. In the day of my trouble have I cried unto Thee, for Thou hast heard me. There is none like unto Thee among the gods, (cont. on fol. 111v) *The Ninth Hour*

O Lord, and there are no works like unto Thy works. All nations whom Thou hast made shall come and worship before Thee, O Lord, and shall glorify Thy name; for great art Thou, and workest wonders; Thou art God alone. Guide me, O Lord, in Thy way, and I will walk in Thy truth; let my heart rejoice, that it may fear Thy name. I will confess Thee, O Lord my God, with all my heart, and I will glorify Thy name unto eternity; for great is Thy mercy upon me, and Thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest hades. O God, transgressors have risen up against me, and an assembly of mighty ones has sought after my soul, and have not set Thee before them. But Thou, O Lord my God, art compassionate and (cont. on fol. 112r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 112r]*

merciful, longsuffering, and plenteous in mercy, and true. Look upon me, and have mercy on me; give Thy strength unto Thy servant, and save the son of Thine handmaid. Work with me a sign unto good, and let them that hate me see it and be put to shame, for Thou, O Lord, hast helped me and comforted me.

*Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). Lord, have mercy (3).*
*Glory: Troparion of the day, or of the Saint. Both now: Theotokion:* He Who for our sake... *(see fol. 112v).*[^352]
*But if it be a fast, we say these Troparia:*
*Tone 8:*
O Thou Who at the ninth hour for our sake in the flesh didst taste of death, mortify the carnal mind of our flesh, O Christ God, and save us.[^353] (cont. on fol. 112v) *The Ninth Hour*

*First Verse:*
Let my prayer draw near before Thee, O Lord; give me understanding according to Thy word.[^354]

*Second Verse:*
Let my supplication enter before Thee, O Lord; deliver me according to Thy word.[^355]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death, and didst manifest the Resurrection as God: despise not them whom Thou hast fashioned with Thy hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Birthgiver of God who bore Thee, who prays for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.[^356]

Do not deliver us up utterly, for Thy name's (cont. on fol. 113r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 113r]*

sake, and break not Thy covenant, and withdraw not Thy mercy from us, for the sake of Abraham Thy beloved, and for the sake of Isaac Thy servant, and Israel Thy holy one.[^357]

*Then, Trisagion. And after Our Father: Kontakion of the day, or of the Saint. But if it be a fast, we say these Troparia:*
*Tone 8:*
Beholding the Author of life hanging upon the Cross, the thief said: If He that is crucified with us were not God incarnate, the sun would not have hidden its rays, nor would the quaking earth have trembled. But O Thou Who endurest all things, remember me, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.

*Glory...*
In the midst of two thieves, Thy Cross was found to be a righteous (cont. on fol. 113v) *The Ninth Hour*

balance: for the one was led down into hades by the weight of his blasphemy, while the other was lightened of sins unto the knowledge of theology. O Christ God, glory to Thee.

*Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Savior of the world upon the Cross, she that bore Thee said weeping: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my maternal heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for all, O my Son and my God.

*Then: Lord, have mercy (40). The Prayer of Basil the Great: Thou Who at every time and at every hour... (see fol. 75).[^358] Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim... In the Name of the Lord,* (cont. on fol. 114r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 114r]*

*Father, bless.*
> **Priest:** "God be compassionate unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us."
*When the Hours are sung together, he says: Through the prayers of our holy fathers...*

*Then this Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^359]
Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who art longsuffering over our sins, and hast brought us even unto the present hour, in which, hanging upon the life-creating Wood, Thou didst open the entrance into Paradise unto the prudent thief, and by death didst destroy death: cleanse us, Thy sinful and unworthy servants; for we have sinned and (cont. on fol. 114v) *The Ninth Hour*

done iniquity, and are not worthy to lift up our eyes and look upon the height of heaven, because we have forsaken the way of Thy righteousness, and have walked according to the desires of our hearts. But we implore Thy lovingkindness: spare us, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy mercy, and save us for Thy holy name's sake, for our days have vanished in vanity. Pluck us out of the hand of the adversary, and forgive us our transgressions, and mortify our carnal mind, that, having put off the old man, we may put on the new, and may live unto Thee, our Master and Benefactor; and thus, following Thy commandments, unto eternal rest (cont. on fol. 115r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 115r]*

we may attain, where is the dwelling of all that rejoice. For Thou art truly the true joy and gladness of them that love Thee, O Christ our God; and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3). Bless. And the Dismissal.*
*But if it be a fast, then after the verse from the Priest:*
*We make 15 prostrations with the prayer [of St. Ephrem], as was previously shown.*[^360] *After this, this prayer:*
*Master, God, Father Almighty... (see fol. 22).[^361]*
*And immediately we begin the Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour.* (cont. on fol. 115v) *THE MID-HOUR OF THE NINTH HOUR*[^362]

*The beginning is likewise as in the other [Mid-Hours]:*
*O come, let us worship... (3).*
*If it be a fast, with prostrations.*
*Alleluia. Psalm 112:*[^363]
Praise the Lord, O ye servants, praise the name of the Lord. Blessed be the name of the Lord, from henceforth and unto the ages. From the rising of the sun unto the going down of the same, the name of the Lord is to be praised. High above all nations is the Lord, and His glory is above the heavens. Who is like unto the Lord our God, Who dwells on high, and looks down upon the humble, in heaven and on earth? Raising up the poor from the earth, (cont. on fol. 116r) *The Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour. [Fol. 116r]*

and lifting up the beggar from the dunghill, to seat him with princes, with the princes of His people; Who makes the barren woman to dwell in a house, a joyful mother of children.

*A Psalm of David, of Haggai and Zechariah, 137:*[^364]
I will confess Thee, O Lord, with all my heart, and before the Angels will I chant unto Thee, for Thou hast heard all the words of my mouth. I will worship toward Thy holy temple, and will confess Thy name, for Thy mercy and Thy truth, for Thou hast magnified Thy holy name above all. In what day soever I shall call upon Thee, speedily hear me; Thou wilt abundantly (cont. on fol. 116v) *The Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour*

endue my soul with strength. Let all the kings of the earth confess Thee, O Lord, for they have heard all the words of Thy mouth. And let them sing in the ways of the Lord, for great is the glory of the Lord. For the Lord is high, and regards the humble, and the high He knows from afar. If I should walk in the midst of affliction, Thou wilt quicken me; against the wrath of mine enemies Thou hast stretched forth Thy hand, and Thy right hand has saved me. The Lord will render recompense on my behalf; O Lord, Thy mercy endures unto eternity; despise not the works of Thy hands.

*To the end, a Psalm of David, 139:*[^365]
Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man; rescue me from the unrighteous man, who have devised unrighteousness in their heart; (cont. on fol. 117r) *The Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour. [Fol. 117r]*

all the day they prepared wars. They have sharpened their tongue like a serpent's; the venom of asps is under their lips. Keep me, O Lord, from the hand of the sinner; rescue me from unrighteous men, who have devised to trip up my steps. The proud have hidden a snare for me, and with cords they have stretched out a net for my feet; they have set stumbling blocks for me beside the path. I said unto the Lord: Thou art my God; give ear, O Lord, unto the voice of my supplication. O Lord, Lord, the strength of my salvation: Thou hast overshadowed my head in the day of battle. Deliver me not up, O Lord, from my desire unto the sinner; they have plotted against me; forsake me not, lest they be exalted. As for the head of their encirclement, the toil of their own lips shall (cont. on fol. 117v) *The Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour*

shall cover them. Burning coals shall fall upon them; Thou wilt cast them down into afflictions, and they shall not stand. A talkative man shall not prosper upon the earth; evils shall hunt an unrighteous man unto destruction. I know that the Lord will execute judgment for the needy, and vengeance for the poor. Surely the righteous shall confess Thy name, and the upright shall dwell with Thy face.

*Glory... Both now... Without Alleluia. Trisagion with prostrations, if it is the fast. And after Our Father, these Troparia, Tone 8:*

*Troparion:* O Thou Who hast enlightened earthly beings by the Cross, and called sinners unto repentance: separate me not from Thy flock, O good Shepherd, but (cont. on fol. 118r) *The Ninth Hour. [Fol. 118r]*

seek me who have gone astray, O Master, and number me with Thy holy flock, as Thou alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:* Confessing like the thief, I cry unto Thee, O Good One: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom; and number me with him, O Thou Who of Thine own will didst deign to suffer for our sake.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:* O come, all ye people, let us hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou dost endure crucifixion, Thou art my Son and my God.

Lord, have mercy (40). *Glory... Both now...* More honorable than the Cherubim: (see fol. 22)[^366] In the name of the Lord (cont. on fol. 118v) *The Mid-Hour of the Ninth Hour*

bless, Father. **Priest:** *Verse:* O Lord Jesus Christ, our God, have mercy on us. **Reader:** Amen. And 3 great prostrations with the prayer of Saint Ephrem: *O Lord and Master of my life...* Then this prayer: *O Master, Lord Jesus Christ, our God, Who art longsuffering over our sins...* (written at the end of the Ninth Hour, see fol. 114)[^367]

---

# THE ORDER OF VESPERS AND COMPLINE

*The Beginning of Vespers with a Priest:*[^368]
*[When the priest has blessed,]* and we say: Amen.

*And we begin: if it is Alleluia, Trisagion with prostrations. And after Our Father: Lord, have mercy (12). Glory... Both now...*

O come, let us worship... (3). Then the Psalm of David, concerning the worldly creation, 103:[^369] (cont. on fol. 119v) *The Order of Vespers*

Bless the Lord, O my soul! O Lord my God, Thou hast been magnified exceedingly; Thou hast clothed Thyself with confession and majesty, Who coverest Thyself with light as with a garment, Who stretchest out the heaven like a curtain; Who coverest His chambers with waters, Who makest the clouds His chariot, Who walkest upon the wings of the wind; Who makest His Angels spirits, and His ministers a flame of fire. Who founded the earth upon its firm foundation; it shall not be shaken unto ages of ages. The abyss like a garment is its vesture; upon the mountains the waters shall stand. At Thy rebuke they shall flee; at the voice of Thy thunder they shall be afraid. The mountains rise, and the plains descend (cont. on fol. 120r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 120r]*

unto the place which Thou hast established for them. Thou hast set a bound which they shall not pass over, neither turn again to cover the earth. Who sendest forth springs in the valleys; between the mountains the waters shall run. They give drink to all the wild beasts of the field; the wild asses await them for their thirst. By them shall the birds of the heaven lodge; from the midst of the rocks they shall give voice. Who waterest the mountains from His chambers; the earth shall be satisfied with the fruit of Thy works. Who causest grass to spring up for the cattle, and green herb for the service of men, that Thou mayest bring forth bread out of the earth; and wine makes glad the heart of man, to make his face cheerful with oil, and bread strengthens man's heart. The trees of the plain shall be satisfied, (cont. on fol. 120v) *The Order of Vespers*

even the cedars of Lebanon which Thou hast planted. There the sparrows make their nests; the house of the heron takes the lead among them. The high mountains are for the harts; the rock is a refuge for the hares. He made the moon for seasons; the sun knows its going down. Thou appointedst the darkness, and it was night, wherein all the wild beasts of the forest will go abroad: young lions roaring to snatch prey, and to seek their food from God. The sun arose, and they were gathered together, and they shall lie down in their dens. Man goes forth unto his work, and unto his labor until evening. How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all; the earth is filled with Thy creation. Yonder is the sea, great and wide; there are creeping things (cont. on fol. 121r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 121r]*

whereof there is no number, living creatures small with great. There the ships go; there is that Leviathan, whom Thou hast formed to make sport of him. All things look unto Thee, to give them food in due season. When Thou givest it them, they will gather it; when Thou openest Thy hand, all things shall be filled with goodness. But when Thou turnest away Thy face, they will be troubled; Thou wilt take away their spirit, and they will fail, and will return unto their dust. Thou wilt send forth Thy Spirit, and they shall be created, and Thou wilt renew the face of the earth. Let the glory of the Lord endure unto the ages; the Lord shall rejoice in His works. He looks upon the earth, and makes it tremble; He touches the mountains, and they smoke. I will sing unto the Lord in my life, (cont. on fol. 121v) *The Order of Vespers*

I will chant unto my God as long as I have my being. May my speech be sweet unto Him, and I will be glad in the Lord. Let sinners disappear from the earth, and the unrighteous, so that they be no more. Bless the Lord, O my soul!

*Then this, twice:*
The sun knows its going down; Thou appointedst the darkness, and it was night. How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all.

*Then:* Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). *If alone, instead of the litany, there is said: Lord, have mercy (12)[^370]. Glory... Both now... But if in church: then the Great Litany, by the priest or by the deacon: In peace let us pray to the Lord. Then the appointed Kathisma. And after the Kathisma, the Little Litany. Then:* (cont. on fol. 122r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 122r]*

*Then we chant the verses: Psalm 140:*[^371]
Lord, I have cried unto Thee, hear me; attend unto the voice of my supplication, when I cry unto Thee.
Let my prayer be set forth as incense before Thee; the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice.
Set a watch, O Lord, before my mouth, and a door of enclosure about my lips.
Incline not my heart unto words of malice, to make excuses in sins, with men that work iniquity; and I will not join with their chosen ones.
The righteous man shall chasten me with mercy, and reprove me; but let not the oil of the sinner anoint my head. (cont. on fol. 122v) *The Order of Vespers*

For yet my prayer also shall be in their pleasures. Their judges have been swallowed up close by the rock; they shall hear my words, for they are sweet. As a clod of earth is broken upon the ground, their bones have been scattered beside hades. For mine eyes are unto Thee, O Lord, O Lord; in Thee have I hoped, take not away my soul. Keep me from the snare which they have laid for me, and from the stumbling blocks of the workers of iniquity. The sinners shall fall into their own net; I am alone until I pass by.

*Of understanding, for David, when he was in the cave: a Prayer, 141:*[^372]
With my voice cried I unto the Lord; with my voice unto the Lord did I make supplication.
I will pour out my supplication before Him; (cont. on fol. 123r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 123r]*

and mine affliction will I declare before Him.
When my spirit failed from me, then Thou knewest my paths.
In this way wherein I walked have they hidden a snare for me.
I looked on my right hand and beheld, and there was none that knew me.
Flight failed me, and there is none that cares for my soul.
I cried unto Thee, O Lord; I said: Thou art my hope, my portion art Thou in the land of the living.
Attend unto my supplication, for I am brought very low.
Deliver me from them that persecute me, for they are stronger than I.
Bring my soul out of prison, that I may confess Thy name.
The righteous shall await me, until Thou shalt recompense me.

*An Ode of Ascents, 129:*[^373] (cont. on fol. 123v) *The Order of Vespers*

Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice.
Let Thine ears be attentive unto the voice of my supplication.
If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee there is forgiveness.
For Thy name's sake have I waited for Thee, O Lord; my soul has waited for Thy word, my soul has hoped in the Lord.
From the morning watch until night, from the morning watch, let Israel hope in the Lord.
For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption; and He shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities.

*Alleluia, 116:*[^374]
O praise the Lord, all ye nations; praise Him, all ye peoples. (cont. on fol. 124r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 124r]*

For His mercy is confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord endures unto eternity.

*Then the Stichera of the current day. Glory... Both now... Theotokion, according to the Tone.*

*After this, this hymn, the Work of the Holy Hieromartyr Athenogenes:*[^375]
O Gladsome Light of the holy glory of the immortal, heavenly, holy, blessed Father: O Jesus Christ! Having come unto the setting of the sun, having beheld the evening light, we hymn the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, God. Worthy art Thou at all times to be hymned with reverent voices, O Son of God, Giver of life unto all the world; therefore all the world glorifies Thee.[^376] (cont. on fol. 124v) *The Order of Vespers*

*Then the Daily Prokeimena, according to the Tone.*
*The Canonarch, having made a bow toward the Superior, stands before the Holy Doors and sings the Daily Prokeimenon, or Alleluia:*[^377]
*On Sunday evening, Alleluia is not sung, but we sing this Prokeimenon:*
Behold now, bless ye the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord. *Verse:* Ye that stand in the temple of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God.[^378]

*If it be on Monday evening, when there is a Troparion to a Saint in the Typikon, this Prokeimenon is sung:*
The Lord will hear me when I cry unto Him. *Verse:* When I called, the God of my righteousness heard me.[^379] (cont. on fol. 125r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 125r]*

*On Tuesday evening, Prokeimenon:*
Thy mercy, O Lord, shall follow me all the days of my life. *Verse:* The Lord is my shepherd, and there is nothing I shall lack.[^380]

*On Wednesday evening, Prokeimenon:*
O God, in Thy name save me, and in Thy strength judge me. *Verse:* O God, hear my prayer, give ear unto the words of my mouth.[^381]

*On Thursday evening, Prokeimenon:*
My help comes from the Lord, Who made heaven and earth. *Verse:* I have lifted up mine eyes unto the hills, from whence my help comes.[^382]

*On Friday evening, Prokeimenon:* (cont. on fol. 125v) *The Order of Vespers*

O God, Thou art my helper, and Thy mercy shall go before me. *Verse:* Deliver me from mine enemies, O God, and redeem me from them that rise up against me.[^383]

*On Saturday evening, Prokeimenon:*
The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty. *Verse 1:* The Lord is clothed with strength and has girded Himself. *Verse 2:* For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken. *Verse 3:* Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days. *And again:* The Lord is King.[^384]

*And so the Canonarch makes a bow toward the Holy Doors. And afterwards, turning toward the west, having his hands folded (cont. on fol. 126r) The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 126r]*

*upon his breast, he stands waiting for the end of the Prokeimenon. And after the Prokeimenon has been sung, he makes a bow to both sides, one to each.*[^385]

*When there is no Troparion to a Saint in the Typikon, then instead of the Prokeimenon, there is sung:*
Alleluia, in the Tone of the Octoechos.[^386]

*And we say these verses:*
*On Monday evening:*
O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger.[^387]

*On Tuesday and Thursday evening:*
Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship at His footstool, for He is holy.[^388]

*On Wednesday evening:*
Their sound has gone forth into all the earth, and (cont. on fol. 126v) *The Order of Vespers*

their words unto the ends of the world.[^389]

*And so the Singer says:* And unto ages of ages. Amen.
*And we with a very loud voice sing:* Alleluia (3).
*And immediately the Singer sings again:* Alleluia.

*And after these:*
Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin: (see fol. 64)[^390]

*And after this:*
*Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord.*

*Then the Stichera at the Aposticha. After this:*
*An Ode of Ascents, 122. Verse 1:*[^391]
Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven. Behold, as the eyes of servants look unto the hands of their masters, as the eyes of the handmaid look unto the hands of her mistress: so do our eyes look unto the Lord our God, (cont. on fol. 127r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 127r]*

until He have mercy on us.

*Verse 2:*
Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us, for we are exceedingly filled with contempt; yea, our soul is exceedingly filled: let the reproach be to them that are at ease, and contempt to the proud.[^392]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*And the Canonarch makes a metania in the midst, toward the icons; likewise to both choirs, on both sides, one to each.*

*Then the Prayer of Saint Symeon the God-Receiver:*[^393]
Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word; for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, which Thou hast pre- (cont. on fol. 127v) *The Order of Vespers*

pared before the face of all people, a light to lighten the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.[^394]

*Then the Trisagion. And after Our Father, the Troparion of the Day, or of the Saint, and the rest, and the Dismissal.*

*If it is the fast, we say these Troparia, Tone 4:*

*Troparion:* Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, Mary full of grace, the Lord is with thee; blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb, for thou hast borne the Savior of our souls. *1 prostration. Glory...*

*Troparion:* O Baptist of Christ, we pray thee, remember us all, that we may be delivered from our iniquities; for unto thee grace has been given to pray for us. (cont. on fol. 128r) *The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 128r]*

*1 prostration. Both now...*

*Troparion:* Pray for us, holy Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, and all saints, that we may be delivered from dangers and afflictions; for we all have acquired you as fervent intercessors before the Savior. *1 prostration.*

*Then the Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:* Beneath thy compassion we take refuge, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; despise not our prayers in afflictions, but deliver us from dangers, O thou who alone art pure and blessed.[^395]

*At this verse, we do not make a prostration.*

*Then:* Lord, have mercy (40), *in a meek and quiet voice, and all together equally. Glory... Both now...* More honorable than the Cherubim: *In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.* **Priest:** He that is blessed and most glorified, Christ (cont. on fol. 128v) *The Order of Vespers*

our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. And we say this prayer: *O Heavenly King... strengthen our faithful kings:* (see fol. 65)[^396]

*Then make 15 prostrations with the prayer, according to what was written before in the Example, at the First Hour: (see fol. 74)*[^397]

*Then the Trisagion, and 3 prostrations. And after Our Father: Lord, have mercy (12). Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (2). Bless, Father. And the Dismissal.*

*And thus we do at the Hours, and at Compline, and at Matins, during the holy fasts.*

*In the Forty Days of the Holy Great Fast, on every Sunday evening, from Cheese- (cont. on fol. 129r) The Order of Vespers. [Fol. 129r]*

*fare Sunday even unto the Sixth Sunday, after the conclusion of* O Heavenly King, *nothing is said, save that having made three great prostrations, immediately follows the Dismissal.*[^398]

*(Tailpiece — Woodcut Renaissance grotesque mask cartouche with foliate scrolls and crown) (Headpiece — Woodcut foliate headpiece with blossoming floral scroll)*

*Going to the Refectory to dine, we say: Verse:*
The poor shall eat and be satisfied, and they shall praise the Lord that seek Him; their heart shall live unto ages of ages.[^399]

*Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (2). Bless, Father.*

*Then the priest blesses the trapesa, having first made the Sign of the Cross:*
O Christ God, bless the food and drink: (see fol. 104)[^400]

*If there is no priest, we say after* Bless, Father: *Through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother, and of all saints.*

*After Rising from the Refectory:*

*If we wish to elevate the Panagia, he that is about to elevate it approaches and (cont. on fol. 130r) After Supper. [Fol. 130r]*

*says the verse:* Through the prayers of the holy fathers.[^401]

*And we say:* Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (2). Bless, Father. *And he that is the server, having received the blessing, elevates the Panagia according to custom, as was shown before.*[^402] *And he takes up the Panagia, saying:*
Thy womb became a holy Table, holding the Heavenly Bread, of Whom whosoever eats dies not, as said the Nourisher of all, O Birthgiver of God.[^403]

*And immediately there is sung:* All generations call thee blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, most blessed and most pure: (see fol. 106)[^404]

*Then:* More honorable than the Cherubim: (see fol. 22)[^405]

*And we drink after the Panagia. Then we say: (cont. on fol. 130v) Thanksgiving*

Thou hast made us glad, O Lord, in Thy creation, and in the works of Thy hands we will rejoice. The light of Thy countenance has been signed upon us, O Lord; Thou hast put gladness into my heart: by the fruit of their corn, wine, and oil they have been increased. In peace together I will lie down and sleep, for Thou, O Lord, alone hast made me dwell in hope.[^406]

*Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (2). Bless, Father.* **Priest:** God is with us, through His grace and love toward mankind, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*He commemorates the ktitors and others whom he desires. Then:* Through the prayers of the holy fathers.

*When there is no priest at the trapesa, (cont. on fol. 131r) After Supper. [Fol. 131r]*

*after supper, we thus give thanks:*
Thou hast made us glad, O Lord, in Thy creation... *Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (2). Bless, Father.* Through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother, and of all saints, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.[^407]

*(Tailpiece — Woodcut four-petaled floral cruciform ornament) (Fol. 131v — Ornamental woodcut floral vignette with foliate rosettes within a double linear border, without text) (Headpiece — Woodcut headpiece featuring a central urn vase flanked by scrolling Renaissance acanthus leaves and blossoming flowers)*

## THE BEGINNING OF GREAT COMPLINE[^408]
*AS IT IS SUNG IN GREAT LENT*

*When the priest has blessed, we say:* O Heavenly King... *Trisagion. And after Our Father: Lord, have mercy (12). Glory... Both now...*

O come, let us worship... (3).

*And if it is the First Week of the Great Fast, we say Psalm 4:* (cont. on fol. 132v) *Great Compline*

O God, attend unto my help: (see fol. 66)[^409]

*And after this, the Great Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete.*[^410] *And after the Canon, we say Psalm 4:* When I called... *and all the rest in order.*

*On the other days of the holy Fast, after* O come, let us worship..., *we say this Psalm:*

*To the end, in hymns, a Psalm of David, 4:*[^411]
When I called, the God of my righteousness heard me; in affliction Thou hast enlarged me. Have mercy on me, and hear my prayer. O ye sons of men, how long will ye be heavy-hearted? Why love ye vanity, and (cont. on fol. 133r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 133r]*

seek a lie? But know ye that the Lord has made marvelous His holy one; the Lord will hear me when I cry unto Him. Be ye angry, and sin not; what ye say in your hearts, be pricked with compunction upon your beds. Sacrifice the sacrifice of righteousness, and hope in the Lord. Many say: Who will show us good things? The light of Thy countenance has been signed upon us, O Lord; Thou hast put gladness into my heart: by the fruit of their corn, wine, and oil they have been increased. In peace together I will lie down and sleep, for Thou, O Lord, alone hast made me dwell in hope.

*To the end, in hymns, upon the eighth, a Psalm of David, 6:*[cont. on fol. 133v][^412] *Great Compline*

O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, neither chasten me in Thy wrath. Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am weak; heal me, O Lord, for my bones are troubled, and my soul is troubled exceedingly; and Thou, O Lord, how long? Turn Thee, O Lord, deliver my soul; save me for Thy mercy's sake. For in death there is no one that remembers Thee, and in hades who shall confess Thee? I am weary with my groaning; every night will I wash my bed, I will water my couch with my tears. Mine eye is troubled because of wrath; I am grown old among all mine enemies. Depart from me, all ye that work (cont. on fol. 134r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 134r]*

iniquity; for the Lord has heard the voice of my weeping, the Lord has heard my supplication, the Lord has received my prayer. Let all mine enemies be put to shame and be troubled exceedingly; let them turn back and be put to shame right speedily.

*To the end, a Psalm of David, 12:*[^413]
How long, O Lord, wilt Thou forget me unto the end? How long wilt Thou turn away Thy face from me? How long shall I take counsel in my soul, having sorrows in my heart day and night? How long shall mine enemy be exalted over me? Look upon me, and hear me, O Lord my God; lighten mine eyes, lest I sleep the sleep of death; lest (cont. on fol. 134v) *Great Compline*

mine enemy say: I have prevailed against him. They that afflict me will rejoice if I be moved. But I have hoped in Thy mercy; my heart shall rejoice in Thy salvation. I will sing unto the Lord Who has dealt bountifully with me, and I will chant unto the name of the Lord Most High.

*Then:* Glory... Both now... *Alleluia (3), and 3 metanias. Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... Psalm 24:* Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul: *written, (see fol. 78)*[^414]

*To the end, a Psalm of David, of ecstasy, 30:*[^415]
In Thee, O Lord, have I hoped, let me never be ashamed; in Thy righteousness deliver me, and rescue me. Incline Thine ear unto me, make haste to deliver me; (cont. on fol. 135r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 135r]*

be Thou unto me a God protector, and a house of refuge to save me. For Thou art my strength and my refuge; and for Thy name's sake Thou wilt guide me and nourish me. Thou wilt bring me out of this snare which they have hidden for me, for Thou art my protector, O Lord. Into Thy hands I commit my spirit; Thou hast redeemed me, O Lord God of truth. Thou hast hated them that regard vain vanities, but I have hoped in the Lord. I will be glad and rejoice in Thy mercy, for Thou hast looked upon my low estate; Thou hast saved my soul from afflictions, and hast not shut me up into the hands of the enemy; Thou hast set my feet in a spacious place. Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am in (cont. on fol. 135v) *Great Compline*

affliction; mine eye is troubled because of wrath, my soul and my belly. For my life is spent with grief, and my years with sighing; my strength has grown weak with poverty, and my bones are troubled. I became a reproach among all mine enemies, and exceedingly so to my neighbors, and a fear to my acquaintances; they that saw me without fled from me. I am forgotten as a dead man out of mind; I am become like a broken vessel. For I have heard the slander of many that dwell round about; when they assembled together against me, they took counsel to take away my life. But I have hoped in Thee, O Lord; I said: Thou art my God; in Thy hands are my lots. (cont. on fol. 136r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 136r]*

Deliver me from the hand of mine enemies, and from them that persecute me. Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and save me in Thy mercy. O Lord, let me not be ashamed, for I have called upon Thee; let the ungodly be ashamed, and let them be brought down into hades. Let the deceitful lips be put to silence, which speak iniquity against the righteous with pride and contempt. How great is the multitude of Thy goodness, O Lord, which Thou hast hidden for them that fear Thee! which Thou hast wrought for them that hope in Thee before the sons of men! Thou wilt hide them in the secret of Thy countenance from the disturbance of man; Thou wilt shelter them in a tabernacle from the contradiction of tongues. Blessed be the Lord, for He has made marvelous His mercy in (cont. on fol. 136v) *Great Compline*

a strong city. But I said in mine ecstasy: I am cast away from before the face of Thine eyes; therefore Thou didst hear the voice of my prayer when I cried unto Thee. Love the Lord, all ye His saints, for the Lord requires truth, and repays plentifully them that act with pride. Be of good courage, and let your heart be strengthened, all ye that hope in the Lord.

*Psalm 90:* He that dwells in the help of the Most High: *written, (see fol. 84)*[^416]

*Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3), and 3 prostrations. Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now...*

*After this, the Canticle of the Prophecy of the Holy Prophet Isaiah:*[^417]

*The precenting choir begins: (cont. on fol. 137r) Great Compline. [Fol. 137r]*

**First Choir:** God is with us, understand, O ye nations, and submit yourselves: for God is with us!

*And the other choir responds the same:*

**Second Choir:** God is with us, understand, O ye nations, and submit yourselves: for God is with us!

*And we sing these verses in chant, with a loud voice, one choir chanting these, and the other responding:*[^418]

Hear ye even unto the ends of the earth: for God is with us!

Ye that are mighty, submit yourselves: for God is with us!

If again ye shall strengthen yourselves, again ye shall be conquered: for God is with us!

And whatever counsel ye shall take, the Lord shall bring it to naught: for God is with us!

And the word whatsoever ye shall speak shall not abide in you: for God is with us! (cont. on fol. 137v) *Great Compline*

And your fear we shall not fear, neither shall we be troubled: for God is with us!

Sanctify the Lord our God Himself, and He shall be your fear: for God is with us!

And if thou trust in Him, He shall be unto thee for sanctification: for God is with us!

And we will put our trust in Him, and we shall be saved by Him: for God is with us!

Behold, I and the children whom God has given me: for God is with us!

The people that walk in darkness have seen a great light: for God is with us!

Ye that dwell in the land and shadow of death, a light shall shine upon you: for God is with us!

For unto us a Child is born, unto us a Son is given: for God is with us! (cont. on fol. 138r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 138r]*

Whose government is upon His shoulder: for God is with us!

And of His peace there is no end: for God is with us!

And His Name shall be called the Angel of Great Counsel:[^419] for God is with us!

Wonderful Counselor: for God is with us!

Mighty God, Ruler, Prince of Peace: for God is with us!

Father of the age to come: for God is with us!

*Glory:* for God is with us!

*Both now:* for God is with us!

*Then we say the 1st verse, three times:*

God is with us, understand, O ye nations, and submit yourselves: for God is with us!

*Then:*[^420]

Having passed through the day, I thank Thee, O Lord; I pray that the evening with the night may be without sin; grant this (cont. on fol. 138v) *Great Compline*

unto me, O Savior, and save me.

*Glory:*

Having passed through the day, I glorify Thee, O Master; I pray that the evening with the night may be without offense; grant this unto me, O Savior, and save me.

*Both now:*

Having passed through the day, I hymn Thee, O Holy One; I pray that the evening with the night may be without sin; keep me, O Savior, and save me.

*Then we say:*[^421]

The bodiless nature of the Cherubim glorifies Thee with unceasing hymns.

And the six-winged living creatures, the Seraphim, extol Thee with endless voices.

All the angelic hosts also praise Thee with thrice-holy hymns. For before the ages Thou art the existing Father, and hast Thy Son co-unoriginate with Thee, and bearing the equally honored (cont. on fol. 139r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 139r]*

Spirit of life, Thou dost manifest the undivided Trinity.

O all-holy Virgin, Mother of God, and ye eyewitnesses and ministers of the Word,[^422] with all the choirs of prophets and martyrs, having immortal life, pray ye diligently for all, for we are all in tribulations; that being delivered from the delusion of the evil one, we may cry the angelic hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy, Thrice-Holy Lord, have mercy on us and save us.

*Then in a low voice we say:*[^423]

I believe in one God, the Father Almighty, Maker of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible. And in one Lord Jesus Christ, the Son of God, the Only-begotten, (cont. on fol. 139v) *Great Compline*

begotten of the Father before all ages; Light of Light, true God of true God, begotten, not made, of one essence with the Father, by Whom all things were made; Who for us men and for our salvation came down from heaven, and was incarnate of the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary, and became man; and was crucified for us under Pontius Pilate, and suffered, and was buried; and rose again on the third day according to the Scriptures; and ascended into heaven, and sits at the right hand of the Father; and He shall come again with glory to judge the living and the dead, Whose kingdom shall have no end. And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord, the Giver of Life, Who proceeds from the Father, Who with the Father (cont. on fol. 140r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 140r]*

and the Son together is worshipped and glorified, Who spoke by the prophets. In one, holy, catholic, and apostolic Church. I confess one baptism for the remission of sins. I look for the resurrection of the dead, and the life of the age to come. Amen.

*After this, these verses, twice, with prostrations. The beginning choir says:*[^424]

O all-holy Lady Theotokos, pray to God for us sinners.

*One prostration. And the other choir says the same verse, with a prostration. And the beginning choir says:*

All ye heavenly hosts, holy angels and archangels, pray to God for us sinners.

Holy, great John, prophet and forerunner and (cont. on fol. 140v) *Great Compline*

Baptist of the Lord, pray to God for us sinners.

Holy glorious apostles, prophets, and martyrs, and all saints, pray to God for us sinners.

Venerable and God-bearing fathers of ours, shepherds and teachers of the universe, pray to God for us sinners.

Venerable fathers of ours, Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves,[^425] pray to God for us sinners.

*Here say also of the saint of the temple.*[^426]

O invincible and divine power of the precious and life-creating Cross of the Lord, forsake not us sinners that hope in thee.

*And both choirs say this in secret:*

God, be merciful to us sinners.

God, cleanse our sins and have mercy on us. (cont. on fol. 141r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 141r]*

*(3 times). And 3 prostrations. Then, Trisagion, without prostrations. After Our Father:*

*On Monday and Wednesday evening, these Troparia, aloud and with singing, Tone 2:*[^427]

Enlighten mine eyes, O Christ God, lest I sleep unto death, lest mine enemy say: I have prevailed against him.[^428]

*Verse:* Look upon me and give ear unto me, O Lord my God.[^429]

*Again the same Troparion:* Enlighten mine eyes, O Christ God...

*Glory:*

Be Thou the defender of my soul, O God, for I walk in the midst of many snares; deliver me from them, and save me, O Good One, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Both now. Theotokion:*

For as much as we have no boldness: *written, (see fol. 38)*[^430] (cont. on fol. 141v) *Great Compline*

*Other Troparia, on Tuesday and Thursday evening, so as to alternate them every other day, Tone 8:*[^431]

The sleeplessness of mine invisible enemies Thou knowest, O Lord, and the infirmity of my wretched flesh Thou knowest, Who didst fashion me; therefore into Thy hands I commend my spirit; shelter me under the wings of Thy goodness, lest I sleep unto death; and enlighten the eyes of my mind with the delight of Thy divine words, and raise me up at an opportune time unto Thy doxology: for Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Verse:* Look upon me and give ear unto me, O Lord my God.

*Troparion:*

How terrible is Thy judgment, O Lord, when angels (cont. on fol. 142r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 142r]*

stand by, men are led in, the books are unfolded, the deeds are examined, the thoughts are searched out! What judgment shall be for me who was conceived in sins? Who shall extinguish the flame for me? Who shall enlighten my darkness, unless Thou, O Lord, have mercy on me, O Good One, for Thou art the Lover of mankind?

*Glory:*

Grant me tears, O God, as once to the sinful woman,[^432] and vouchsafe me to wet Thy feet, which have delivered me from the path of deceit, and to offer unto Thee fragrant myrrh, a pure life acquired through repentance, that I also may hear Thy gracious voice: Thy faith has saved thee; go in peace.

*Both now. Theotokion:* (cont. on fol. 142v) *Great Compline*

Having thine unashamed hope, O Theotokos, I shall be saved, and having acquired thy protection, O Most Pure, I shall not fear; but I will pursue mine enemies and conquer them, having clothed myself as in a breastplate in thy shelter and thine all-powerful help; and praying, I cry unto thee, O Lady: Save me by thy supplications, and raise me up from dark sleep unto thy doxology, by the power of Christ God Who was incarnate of thee.

*Lord, have mercy (40). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim... In the Name of the Lord [bless, father]. Priest:*

*Through the prayers of our holy fathers...*

*Then, The Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^433]

O Lord, deliver us from every arrow that flies by day, deliver us from every (cont. on fol. 143r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 143r]*

thing that walks in darkness;[^434] receive the evening sacrifice, the lifting up of our hands; vouchsafe us also to pass through the course of the night without blemish, untempted of evil things; deliver us from every perturbation and fear which comes upon us from the devil; grant unto our souls compunction, and to our thoughts mindfulness of the trial at Thy dread and righteous judgment; nail down our flesh in Thy fear,[^435] and mortify our earthly members,[^436] that even in the silence of sleep we may be enlightened by the contemplation of Thy judgments; take away from us every unseemly fantasy and harmful lust; and raise us up at the time (cont. on fol. 143v) *Great Compline*

of prayer, confirmed in the faith, and progressing in Thy commandments; through the good pleasure and goodness of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy and good and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Then: Come, let us worship (3).*

*Psalm 50:* Have mercy on me, O God: *written, (see fol. 6)*[^437]

*A Prayer of the poor man when he is downcast, and pours out his supplication before God. Psalm 101:*[^438]

O Lord, hear my prayer, and let my cry come unto Thee. Turn not away Thy face from me; in what day soever I am afflicted, incline Thine ear unto me; in what day soever I call upon Thee, (cont. on fol. 144r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 144r]*

hear me speedily. For my days have vanished like smoke, and my bones are dried up like firewood. I am smitten like grass, and my heart is withered, for I forgot to eat my bread. By reason of the voice of my groaning, my bone has cleaved to my flesh. I am become like a pelican of the wilderness; I am become like an owl in a ruined house; I have watched, and am become like a sparrow that sits alone upon the housetop. Mine enemies reproached me all the day, and they that praised me swore against me. For I have eaten ashes like bread, and mingled my drink with weeping, because of the face of Thine anger and Thy wrath; for having lifted me up, Thou hast cast me down. My days have declined like a shadow, (cont. on fol. 144v) *Great Compline*

and I am withered like grass. But Thou, O Lord, endurest unto the age, and Thy memorial unto generation and generation. Thou shalt arise and have compassion upon Zion; for it is time to have compassion upon her, for the time is come. For Thy servants have taken pleasure in her stones, and they shall pity her dust. And the nations shall fear Thy Name, O Lord, and all the kings of the earth Thy glory. For the Lord shall build up Zion, and He shall appear in His glory. He has had regard unto the prayer of the humble, and has not despised their supplication. Let this be written for another generation, and the people that is being created shall praise the Lord. For He has looked down from His holy height; the Lord looked down from heaven upon the earth, to hear the groaning of the fettered, to loose the sons of the (cont. on fol. 145r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 145r]*

slain; to declare the Name of the Lord in Zion, and His praise in Jerusalem, when the peoples are gathered together, and the kings, to serve the Lord. He answered Him in the way of His strength: Declare unto me the shortness of my days. Take me not away in the midst of my days; Thy years are unto generation and generation. In the beginning, O Lord, Thou didst lay the foundation of the earth, and the heavens are the works of Thy hands. They shall perish, but Thou endurest; and all of them shall wax old like a garment, and like a vesture shalt Thou fold them, and they shall be changed; but Thou art the same, and Thy years shall not fail. The sons of Thy servants shall dwell securely, and their seed shall be guided aright unto the ages.

*After this, The Prayer of Manasseh, King of Judah:*[^439] (cont. on fol. 145v) *Great Compline*

O Lord Almighty, God of our fathers, of Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, and of their righteous seed; Who hast made heaven and earth with all the array thereof; Who hast bound the sea by the word of Thy commandment; Who hast shut up the deep, and sealed it by Thy fearful and glorious Name; Whom all things fear and tremble before the face of Thy power;[^440] and unbearable is the anger of Thy threatening toward sinners, yet immeasurable and unsearchable is the mercy of Thy promise; for Thou art the Most High Lord, compassionate, longsuffering, and plenteous in mercy, and repenting of the evils of men; but Thou, O Lord, (cont. on fol. 146r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 146r]*

according to the multitude of Thy goodness, hast promised repentance and forgiveness to them that have sinned against Thee; and in the multitude of Thy compassions hast appointed repentance unto sinners for salvation. Thou therefore, O Lord, God of the righteous, hast not appointed repentance unto the righteous, unto Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, who have not sinned against Thee; but Thou hast appointed repentance unto me a sinner; for I have sinned against Thee above the number of the sand of the sea. Mine iniquities are multiplied, O Lord, mine iniquities are multiplied, and I am not worthy to look up and see the height of heaven by reason of the multitude of mine unrighteousnesses. I am bowed down with many iron chains, so that I cannot lift up (cont. on fol. 146v) *Great Compline*

my head, and there is no respite for me; because I have provoked Thy wrath, and done evil before Thee, and did not do Thy will, neither kept I Thy commandments; and now I bow the knees of my heart, beseeching the grace that is from Thee. I have sinned, O Lord, I have sinned, and I know mine iniquities; but I ask and pray Thee: Forgive me, O Lord, forgive me, and destroy me not with mine iniquities, neither reserve evils for me, being hostile for ever, nor condemn me into the nether parts of the earth. For Thou art God, the God of them that repent, that even in me Thou mayest show all Thy goodness; for Thou wilt save me who am unworthy, according to the greatness of Thy mercy; (cont. on fol. 147r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 147r]*

and I will praise Thee continually all the days of my life; for all the heavenly hosts hymn Thee, and Thine is the glory unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Trisagion. And after Our Father: these Troparia, Tone 6:*[^441]

Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us:

*Glory:*

O Lord, have mercy on us, for in Thee have we trusted:

*Both now:*

Open unto us the gates of compassion: *written in the Mid-Hour of the First Hour, (see fol. 74)*[^442]

*Lord, have mercy (40). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim... In the Name of the Lord bless, father. Priest, verse:*

*Through the prayers of our holy fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.*

*Then, The Prayer:*

O Master God, Father Almighty: *written, (see fol. 22)*[^443] *Great Compline*

*Then: Come, let us worship (3).*

*If it be not the first week of the Great Fast,[^444] we say Psalm 69:*

O God, attend unto my help; O Lord, [make haste to help me]: *written, (see fol. 95)*[^445]

*Then, Psalm 142:*

O Lord, hear my prayer; give ear unto [my supplication in Thy truth]: *written, (see fol. 53)*[^446]

*Then, this:*

Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men. Lord, Thou hast been our refuge: Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this night: *written in Matins, (see fol. 63)*[^447]

*After this, the Supplicatory Canon:* (cont. on fol. 148r) *On How We Always Sing the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos at Compline. [Fol. 148r]*[^448]

*It is fitting to know that always at Complines we sing the Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos. Let this also be known, that the Paraklesis to the Holy Theotokos is placed here for this reason: since certain persons, laboring diligently in the church rule, sing the venerable Paraklesis to the Holy Theotokos at Compline, and especially at Small Compline, in this order: after Come, let us worship; O God, attend unto my help; and the rest of Small Compline. Then, after I believe in one (cont. on fol. 148v) Instruction [on Compline and the Paraklesis]*

*God: they begin to chant: God is the Lord; and the Troparia of the Supplication. Then: Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great [mercy]; and we sing the Supplicatory Canon with its customary order, and the Stichera. After them, Trisagion; and the customary Troparia both of Compline and of the Supplication; and the final Litany of the Supplication. And after the end of these: Lord, have mercy (40); Thou Who at all times; and the rest of Compline.*
