*If, however, the Paraklesis is not sung at Compline, then after I believe in one God, at Small Compline, or at Great Compline after the end of Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this night without sin: we immediately begin the Canon, without the customary order of the Paraklesis; and the Sessional Hymn: As the all- (cont. on fol. 149r) Concerning the Supplication. [Fol. 149r]*

*immaculate; and the Kontakion: Unashamed Protectress of Christians; with the Ikos: Spread out thy hands; we say in the customary manner. And after Ode 9: It is truly meet; after this, Trisagion; and the customary order of Compline.*

*Again, let this also be known: that from Monday of the second week of the Fast of the holy Great Forty Days,[^449] we sing at the Psalters—that is to say, at Complines—the Canons of the saints from the Menaion that fall from Lazarus Saturday until the Sunday of the Breaking of Bread (that is, of Thomas), together with the Canons to the Theotokos. First we sing the Canon of the Theotokos, then of the Menaion. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn of the Menaion. After Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Theotokos and the Kontakion [of the Saint]. At the end, the Canon to the saints in the Menaion.*

---

# KANONIKON: THE VENERABLE PARAKLESIS TO THE MOST HOLY THEOTOKOS [^450]

*Sung for Every Petition*

> **Priest:** "Blessed is our God, [always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.]"

*Then, Trisagion. All-Holy Trinity. After Our Father:*

> **Priest:** "For Thine is the kingdom:"

*Then: Lord, have mercy (12). Then: Glory... Both now... Come, let us worship (3). Then we say Psalm 142:* (cont. on fol. 150r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 150r]*

O Lord, hear my prayer; give ear unto [my supplication in Thy truth]: *written, (see fol. 53)*[^451]

*After this: Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3). Then:*

God is the Lord, and has appeared unto us; blessed is He that comes in the Name of the Lord. *Tone 4.*

*After this, Troparia, Tone 4:*

Unto the Theotokos let us sinners and humble ones now diligently run, and let us fall down in repentance, crying from the depth of the soul: O Lady, help, having had compassion upon us; make haste, for we perish by reason of the multitude of sins; turn not thy servants away empty, for thee alone do we have as protectress. *(Twice).*

*Glory... Both now...*

Never shall we be silent, O Theotokos, of speaking thy mighty acts, we who are unworthy; for if thou hadst not stood interceding for us, who would have delivered us (cont. on fol. 150v) *Supplication*

from so many dangers? Who would have preserved us free until now? We will not depart from thee, O Lady; save thy servants ever from every adversity, O thou who alone art blessed.

*Then, Psalm 50:* Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy: *written, (see fol. 6)*[^452]

*After this, The Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos. The composition of Kyr Theophanes.[^453] Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!*

*Refrain:* Most Holy Theotokos, save us.

*Troparia:*

Held fast by many temptations, unto thee do I flee, seeking salvation: O Mother of the Word and Virgin, save me from cruel and grievous things. (cont. on fol. 151r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 151r]*

The assaults of passions trouble me, and many sorrows fill my soul; bring peace, O Maiden, by the calm of thy Son and God, O all-immaculate one.

*Glory:*

Having given birth unto the Savior and God, I beseech thee, O Virgin: deliver me from cruel things; for unto thee do I now flee, stretching out both soul and thought.

*Both now:*

Being in sorrow and anguish, vouchsafe me the visitation of divine providence which is from thee, O only Mother of God, as thou art good, the bearer of the Good One.

*Katavasia:*

*Save from dangers thy servants, O Theotokos; for all of us after God flee unto thee, as (cont. on fol. 151v) Supplication*

an unbreakable rampart and protectress.

*This Katavasia is sung after each Ode.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

*O Lord, Vault-Framer of the heavenly dome and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires and Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.*

*Troparia:*

As the protection and shelter of my life have I set thee, O Bearer of God; do thou, O Virgin, steer me into thy haven, who art the cause of good things and confirmation of the faithful, O only all-praised one.

I beseech thee, O Virgin, to disperse the trouble of my soul and the tempest of my grief; for thou, O Bride of God, hast given birth to Christ, the Prince of peace, O only all-pure one.

*Glory:* (cont. on fol. 152r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 152r]*

Having given birth to the Benefactor, the Cause of good things, pour out the wealth of beneficence unto all; for thou canst do all things, being mighty in strength, as having given birth to Christ, O divinely blessed one.

*Both now:*

Assailed by cruel infirmities and painful passions, O Virgin, do thou help me; for I know thee to be an inexhaustible treasury of healings, unstolen and unspent.

*Katavasia:* Save from dangers thy servants, O Theotokos...

*Then, Little Litany, in which commemoration is made for whomsoever the Supplication is sung. Priest or Deacon:* Have mercy on us, O God: *and the rest according to the need. Lord, have mercy (40). Then, after the prayer, the exclamation:* Hear us, O God our Savior. *Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:* (cont. on fol. 152v) *Supplication*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*

Fervent prayer, and rampart unassailable, fountain of mercy, haven of the world; earnestly do we cry unto thee, O Lady Theotokos: Hasten, and deliver us from dangers, O thou who alone dost swiftly protect.[^454]

*Sessional Hymn, which we sing only at Compline. If thou wilt, read it also here. Tone 8:*[^455]

As the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as the unwedded Mother, the receptacle of the Deliverer, as being [the temple] of the Comforter, O all-praised one: since I am a vile dwelling of lawlessness and have consciously become a plaything of demons, hasten to deliver me from their evildoing, making me a radiant dwelling by virtues, O giver of light (cont. on fol. 153r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 153r]*

and undefiled one; disperse the cloud of passions, and vouchsafe me by thy prayers to partake of what is highest and of the never-setting light.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity.*

*Troparia:*

Having given birth to the Lord Who is the Helmsman, calm the disturbance of my passions and the tempest of my transgressions, O Bride of God.

O thou who didst bear the Tenderhearted One, the Savior of all who hymn Thee, grant unto me who call upon Thee the abyss of thy compassions.

*Glory:*

Having acquired thee, O all-immaculate one, as hope, confirmation, and an immovable rampart of salvation, we are delivered from all cruel evils.

*Both now:* (cont. on fol. 153v) *Supplication*

Lying in sins upon the bed of my sickness, as thou art a lover of mankind, help me, O Theotokos, Mother and ever-virgin.

*Katavasia:* Save from dangers...

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

*Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm; and grant Thy peace unto us, O only Lover of mankind.*

*Troparia:*

Fill our life with gladness, O pure one, giving thine undefiled joy, as having given birth to the cause of gladness.

Deliver us from dangers, O pure Theotokos, who didst bear the eternal deliverance, and the peace that passes all understanding.

*Glory:*

Disperse the darkness of my transgressions, O Bride of God, by the radiance of thy brightness, O thou who didst give birth to the Light (cont. on fol. 154r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 154r]*

divine and pre-eternal.

*Both now:*

Heal the infirmity of my soul, O pure one, having visited it with thy visitation, and grant me health by thy prayers.

*Katavasia:* Save from dangers...

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

*My prayer will I pour out unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrow; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades; but like Jonah I pray: From corruption, O my God, raise me up.*

*Troparia:*

As He has saved [me] from death and corruption, having surrendered Himself unto death, do thou, O Virgin, pray to the Lord and thy Son to deliver me from the evildoing of enemies, since my nature was held fast by corruption and death. (cont. on fol. 154v) *Supplication*

I know thee as the protectress of my life and a steadfast guardian, O Virgin, who dost dispel the turmoil of temptations and drive away the assaults of demons; and I pray thee ever to deliver me from my passions, O all-immaculate one.

*Glory:*

We have acquired thee as a rampart and refuge, and as perfect salvation for our souls, and as relief in afflictions, O Maiden; and in thy light we ever rejoice: O Lady, save us now from passions and dangers.

*Both now:*

Upon a bed of infirmity do I now lie prostrate, and there is no healing for my flesh; but as thou hast given birth unto God and the Savior of the world, and the Deliverer from infirmities, I pray thee, the good one: raise me up from the corruption of diseases.

*Katavasia:* Save from dangers thy servants... (cont. on fol. 155r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 155r]*

*Little Litany:* Again and again:

> **Priest:** "For Thou art the King of peace:"

*After this, Kontakion of the Theotokos, Tone 6:* Unashamed protectress of Christians: *written, (see fol. 100)*[^456]

*After this, Another Kontakion, Same Tone:*[^457]

We have no other help, we have no other hope, but thee, O all-pure Virgin; do thou help us; in thee do we hope, and in thee do we boast, for we are thy servants: let us not be put to shame.

*Then, the Ikos, which we read only at Compline; if thou wilt, read it also here:*[^458]

Spread out thy hands, with which thou didst hold the Master of all as an infant, by reason of the multitude of goodness; forsake us not who ever hope (cont. on fol. 155v) *Supplication*

in thee; by thy vigilant prayer and boundless simplicity have compassion upon us, and grant unto our souls thy mercy, pouring it forth unto the ages; for thee do we sinners have as protectress against the dangers and evils that come upon us; but as having the compassions of mercy, hasten unto prayer and speed unto supplication, ever protecting, O Theotokos, them that honor thee.

*After this, Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.[^459]

*Verse:* Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear; and forget [also thine own people, and thy father's house].[^460]

*Then, Deacon:* Let us pray to the Lord.

> **Priest:** "For holy art Thou, O our God:"

*Then:* Let every breath praise the Lord.

*After this, Deacon:* And that we may be vouchsafed (cont. on fol. 156r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 156r]*

[to hear the Holy Gospel]... Wisdom. Stand upright.

> **Priest:** "Peace be unto all."

*And again the Priest:* The Reading of the Holy Evangelist [Luke / N.].[^461]

> **Deacon:** "Let us attend."

*The Priest reads whatever Gospel he desires, according to each specific need; and to the Theotokos, this from Luke:* In those days, Mary arose and went into the hill country: *unto the end according to the custom.*[^462]

*And at the conclusion of the Gospel, we sing, Glory, Tone 2:*

O Father, Word, and Spirit, Holy Trinity: cleanse the multitude of our sins.

*Both now:*

Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One: cleanse the multitude of our sins.

Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions (cont. on fol. 156v) *Supplication*

cleanse our transgressions.

*Then, Sticheron. Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^463]

Cast us not away unto human protection, O Most Pure Lady, but receive the supplications of thy servants, for afflictions hold us fast; we cannot bear the demonic arrow-shootings; shelter we have not acquired, nor anywhere to flee, miserable ones that we are, ever vanquished; consolation we have none save thee, O Lady of the world, hope and protectress of the faithful: despise not our supplications, but make them profitable.

*The Deacon [says] The Prayer:*

Save, O God, Thy people: *in its entirety unto the end.*

> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy (12)."

**Priest, exclamation:** By the mercy and compassions: (cont. on fol. 157r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 157r]*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

*The youth who came from Judea to Babylon of old, established by faith, trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparia:*

When Thou didst desire to accomplish our salvation, O Savior, Thou didst dwell in the Virgin's womb, whom Thou didst reveal unto the world as protectress: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

O Mother who didst bear the Lover of mercy, beseech Him now to deliver me from transgressions and spiritual defilement, as I cry unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory:*

A treasury of salvation, and fountain of incorruption, a pillar of confirmation, and door of repentance hast Thou revealed her that bore Thee unto them that cry: (cont. on fol. 157v) *Supplication*

O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Both now:*

Bodily infirmities and spiritual sins, O Mother of God, vouchsafe to heal for them that with love draw near unto thy protection, O Virgin, who didst bear Christ the Savior for us.

*Save thy servants from perils, O Virgin Theotokos:*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

*The King of heaven, Whom all the angelic hosts praise: praise ye and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^464]*

*Troparia:*

Despise not them that require help from thee, O Virgin, who praise and supremely exalt thee, O Maiden, unto the ages.

Thou healest the infirmity of my soul and the bodily sicknesses, O Virgin, that we may praise and supremely exalt thee, the pure one, unto the ages.

*Glory:*

A wealth of healings dost thou pour forth (cont. on fol. 158r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 158r]*

unto them that faithfully praise thee, O Virgin, and supremely exalt thee unto all ages.

*Both now:*

Thou drivest away the assaults of temptations and the onslaughts of passions, O Virgin, that we may praise and supremely exalt thee, the pure one, unto the ages.

*Save thy servants from perils, O Virgin Theotokos:*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*Truly confessing thee to be the Theotokos, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, together with the heavenly hosts do we magnify thee.[^465]*

*Troparia:*

Turn not away from the stream of my tears, O Virgin who didst bear Christ, Who hath taken away every tear from every face.

Fill my heart with joy, O Virgin, who didst receive the fullness of joy, consuming sinful sorrow.

*Glory:* (cont. on fol. 158v) *Supplication*

Illumine with the rays of thy light, O Virgin, driving away the darkness of ignorance, them that faithfully confess thee to be the Mother of God.

*Both now:*

In the place of affliction, heal him that lies low in infirmity, O Virgin, transforming sickness into health.

*Katabasia:*[^466]

O most radiant cloud, Mother of God, vanquish them that conquer us by thy sovereign and almighty right hand; and stand before them that are in afflictions; protect the wronged; and loose the sins of thy supplicants: for whatsoever thou desirest, thou art able to do.

*Then:*

It is truly meet [to bless thee, the Theotokos]:

*Then, Supplicatory Stichera. Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва тя̀ мр҃тва, а҆рїмаѳе́й):*[^467] (cont. on fol. 159r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 159r]*

Thou prayest for all, O Good One, who with faith flee unto thy sovereign protection; for we sinners have no other continuous deliverance unto God in perils and afflictions, burdened with many sins, O Mother of God the Most High: therefore do we fall down before thee: deliver thy servants from every distress.

*Verse:* I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.[^468]

Joy of all that grieve, and protectress of the wronged, nourisher of the hungry, comfort of wanderers, haven of the storm-tossed, visitation of the sick, shelter and protectress of the infirm, staff of old age, O Mother of God the Most High, (cont. on fol. 159v) *Supplication*

thou art, O Most Pure One: make haste, we pray, that thy servants may be saved.

*Verse:* Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear; and forget thine own people, and thy father's house.[^469]

Rejoice, O most pure Virgin; rejoice, honorable scepter of Christ the King; rejoice, who didst bud forth the mystical cluster of grapes; rejoice, heavenly gate and bush unburned; rejoice, light of all the world; rejoice, joy of all; rejoice, salvation of the faithful; rejoice, protectress and refuge of all Christians, O Lady.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8:*

Rejoice, boast of the universe; rejoice, temple of the Lord; rejoice, overshadowing mountain; rejoice, refuge; rejoice, golden lampstand; (cont. on fol. 160r) *To the Theotokos. [Fol. 160r]*

rejoice, glory of the Orthodox, O Pure One; rejoice, Mary, Mother of Christ God; rejoice, paradise; rejoice, divine table; rejoice, temple; rejoice, golden jar; rejoice, joy of all!

*After this, Trisagion.*

*All-Holy Trinity. After Our Father: Troparion: Unto the Theotokos let us now run diligently: Glory... Both now... We shall never be silent, O Theotokos: seek backward, (see fol. 150)*[^470]

*Then, the Deacon's [Litany]:*

Have mercy on us, O God: *and all the rest according to the custom, for whatsoever one prays.*

*And: Lord, have mercy (50).*

*Then, the Priest [says] the prayer of exclamation:*

Hear us, O God our Savior:

*Or:* For a merciful God and Lover of mankind [art Thou]: *After this:* More honorable than the Cherubim, and more glorious [beyond compare than the Seraphim]: *Then:* (cont. on fol. 160v) *Supplication*

Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (3).

*Then, the Priest [gives] the Dismissal:*

Christ our true God:

*End of the Supplicatory Service to the Holy Theotokos.*

*At Compline, therefore, whether there be a Paraklesis or not, concerning this it was indicated before (see fol. 148)*[^471] *For if there be only the Canon, then after the Canon we say: It is truly meet; Trisagion; and after Our Father: Troparion of the day, and of the Saint whose temple it is, and the other Troparia as thou hast concerning these in Small Compline. But if it be the holy Forty Days, we sing these Troparia in Tone 6:*

*The right choir begins:*

O Lord of hosts, be with us, for none other helper in afflictions have we save (cont. on fol. 161r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 161r]*

Thee; O Lord of hosts, have mercy on us.

*And the other choir says the same verses: O Lord of hosts, be with us...*

*After this, the choir that began previously sings:*

*Verse 1:* Praise God in His saints; praise Him in the firmament of His power.[^472]

*And again the second choir sings: O Lord of hosts, be with us. And thus the initial choir says the remaining four verses, and the second choir responds at every verse: O Lord of hosts, be with us.*

*Verse 2:* Praise Him for His mighty acts; praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness.[^473] *O Lord of hosts...*

*Verse 3:* Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet; praise (cont. on fol. 161v) *Great Compline*

Him with the psaltery and harp. *O Lord of hosts, be with us.*

*Verse 4:* Praise Him with the timbrel and dance; praise Him with strings and flute.[^474] *O Lord of hosts, be with us.*

*Verse 5:* Praise Him with tuneful cymbals; praise Him with cymbals of jubilation. Let every breath praise the Lord.[^475] *O Lord of hosts, be with us.*

*Then, both choirs together sing this first verse with a most elevated voice:*

Praise God in His saints; praise Him in the firmament of His power. *O Lord of hosts, be with us.*

*Glory: Troparion, in the same tone [Tone 6]:*

Had we not Thy saints as intercessors, and Thy goodness (cont. on fol. 162r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 162r]*

having mercy on us, how should we have dared, O Savior, to hymn Thee, Whom angels unceasingly glorify? O Knower of hearts, spare our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion:*

Great are the multitudes of my sins, O Theotokos; unto thee have I fled, O Pure One, requiring salvation: visit my ailing soul, and pray unto thy Son and our God to grant me forgiveness of the evils I have committed, O only blessed one.

*Then:*

All-Holy Theotokos, in the time of my life forsake me not, and commit me not unto human protection; but do thou thyself defend me and have mercy on me.

*After this:*

All my hope I place in thee, Mother (cont. on fol. 162v) *Great Compline*

of God, preserve me under Thy shelter.

*Then: Lord, have mercy (40). After this, the Prayer of Saint Basil: Thou Who at all times:*

*Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim: In the name of the Lord [father, bless]. Priest: God be bountiful unto us [and bless us]: And we make 3 great prostrations, with the prayer of Saint Ephrem: O Lord and Master of my life: and 12 other [small bows], slowly, so that all may be in unison (see fol. 22)*[^476]

*And after this, the Prayer to the Most Holy Theotokos, of Saint Basil the Great, or of Paul, the first [superior] of the Monastery of the Evergetis:[^477]*

Spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Lady, Bride of God, who by thy glorious birthgiving didst unite God the Word unto men, (cont. on fol. 163r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 163r]*

and joined the fallen nature of our race unto heavenly things; who art the only hope of the hopeless, and helper of the vanquished; the ready protection of them that flee unto thee, and the refuge of all Christians: despise me not, a sinner and defiled, who by foul thoughts and words and deeds and acts have made myself altogether unprofitable, and by slothful disposition have become a slave unto pleasures and dejection; but as the Mother of the God Who loves mankind, show thy love for mankind and have compassion upon me, a sinner and prodigal, and accept my supplication offered unto thee from defiled lips; and beseech thy Son, our Master and Lord, with thy maternal (cont. on fol. 163v) *Great Compline*

boldness, that He may open unto me also the loving-kindness of His goodness; and, overlooking my countless transgressions, turn me unto repentance, and show me forth an approved doer of His commandments. And be thou ever present with me, merciful, compassionate, and full of loving-kindness in this present life, a fervent protectress and helper, repulsing the assaults of adversaries and guiding me unto salvation; and at the time of my departure, preserving my wretched soul, and driving far away from it the dark visages of evil demons; and in the dread day of judgment, delivering me from eternal torment, and making me an heir of the ineffable (cont. on fol. 164r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 164r]*

glory of thy Son and our God: which may I obtain, O my Lady, most holy Theotokos, through thy mediation and protection, by the grace and love for mankind of Thine Only-Begotten Son, our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ: unto Whom is due all glory, honor, and worship, with His Father Who is without beginning, and with His All-Holy, Good, and Life-Creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*If Great [Compline] is sung, we say the final Trisagion; 3 prostrations; after Our Father:*

*Lord, have mercy (12). Then this Prayer of Saint Antiochus the Monk:[^478]* (cont. on fol. 164v) *Great Compline*

And grant unto us, O Master, as we depart unto sleep, rest of soul and body; and preserve us from the gloomy sleep of sin, and from all dark and nocturnal sensuality. Calm the impulses of passions, and quench the fiery darts of the evil one which are deceitfully hurled against us. Assuage the rebellions of our flesh, and lull to sleep all our earthly and carnal mind. And grant unto us, O God, a watchful mind, a chaste thought, a sober heart, a light sleep, and free from every satanic fantasy. And raise us up at the time of prayer, established in Thy commandments, and holding within ourselves the remembrance (cont. on fol. 165r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 165r]*

of Thy judgments continually. And grant unto us all-night doxology, to hymn, and bless, and glorify Thy most honorable and majestic name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

O most glorious, ever-virgin Theotokos, Mother of Christ our God: accept our prayers, and bring them before thy Son and our God, that for thy sake He may save and enlighten our souls.

*Then, the Prayer of Saint Joannicius:[^479]*

God is our hope, and Christ is our refuge, and the Holy Spirit is our protector.[^480]

*Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (20). Lord, bless.*

*Priest: Dismissal: Christ our true God: (see fol. 25)*[^481]

*And after the Amen, the brethren make bows, saying:* (cont. on fol. 165v) *Great Compline*

Bless, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner, wherein I have sinned in word, and in deed, and in thought, and with all my senses.

*And they respond:* May God forgive thee, venerable father.

*Then the brethren also bend their knees unto the earth, saying this mutual forgiveness:[^482]*

Forgive me, holy father, and bless all wherein I have sinned all the days of my life; and this day I have sinned countlessly, in soul and in body, through sleep and sloth, through demonic darkening, in unclean thoughts, in forgetfulness of mind, and in condemnation; I have sinned in heart, and with all my senses: by hearing and sight, voluntarily and involuntarily; and there is no sin which I have not committed, yet of all do I repent: (cont. on fol. 166r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 166r]*

forgive me, holy father, and bless, and pray for me, a sinner.

*The Priest responds:*

By His grace may God forgive and have mercy [upon you].

*And after this, the Priest says the Litany:*

Let us pray for orthodox Christians: *(see fol. 26)*[^483] *And the Dismissal.*

*If, however, there be no Priest, it is concluded thus:*

O Lord Jesus Christ our God, through the prayers of Thy most pure Mother, and of our venerable and God-bearing fathers, and for the sake of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*And instead of the mutual forgiveness, he says:*

Remit, pardon, forgive, O God, our offenses, voluntary and involuntary, in word and deed and thought, in (cont. on fol. 166v) *Great Compline*

by day and by night, and at every hour: forgive all, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Then we say this Prayer:*

Them that hate and wrong us: *(see fol. 27)*[^484]

*If it be the Great Fast, after Lord, bless, instead of the Dismissal, the Priest says this Prayer:[^485]*

O Master of many mercies, Lord Jesus Christ our God: through the intercessions of our most pure Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary; by the power of the precious and life-creating Cross; by the protection of the precious bodiless heavenly hosts; of the precious and glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John; of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles; of the holy and victorious Martyrs; (cont. on fol. 167r) *Great Compline. [Fol. 167r]*

of our venerable and God-bearing fathers; of the holy and righteous Ancestors of God, Joachim and Anna; and of Saint *[Name]*, whose temple it is; and of all Thy saints: make our prayer acceptable; grant unto us forgiveness of our transgressions; shelter us under the shelter of Thy wings; drive away from us every enemy and adversary; bring peace unto our life; O Lord, have mercy on us and on Thy world, and save our souls, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*And while this is being said, we all fall prostrate upon the earth, praying within ourselves. And when we have arisen, the Priest makes a bow unto the earth to the brethren, saying:* (cont. in cohort 20) *Great Compline*

Bless, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner, wherein I have sinned in all this Forty Days [Lent], in word, or in deed, or in thought, and with all my senses.

*And all the brethren respond together:* May God forgive thee and have mercy on thee, holy father.

*And after this the Priest says the Litany:* Let us pray for orthodox Christians: *And the rest: (see fol. 26).*[^486] *And after this, all the brethren make a bow unto the earth to the superior, two by two, from both sides according to their rank, each one saying the same words as he, having first bowed, spoke unto them.*

*And after this, the Priest says:* Through the prayers of our holy fathers:

*And we depart unto our own cells.*

*It Is Necessary Also to Know This:*

*That in the other fasts also, when there is Alleluia, we chant the same Great Methimon,[^487] except for "O Lord of hosts, be with us": for the customary Troparia are said, as is indicated below in Small Compline, (see fol. 104).[^488] When, however, there is a Troparion to a saint, or a Vigil, then throughout the whole year we chant Small Compline, which is as follows:*

*The Beginning of Small Compline*

*When the Priest has blessed, we say according to the custom as follows:*

O Heavenly King: *Trisagion.* All-Holy Trinity: *And after Our Father:* Lord, have mercy (12). *Glory... Both now...*

O come, let us worship: *(thrice).*

Psalm 50: *Have mercy on me, O God:* *(see fol. 6).*[^489]

Psalm 69: *O God, draw near unto my help:* *(see fol. 36).*[^490]

Psalm 142: *Lord, hear my prayer, give ear unto my supplication:* *(see fol. 53).*[^491]

*After this:* Glory to God in the highest:

Lord, Thou hast been our refuge from generation [to generation]: (cont. on fol. 169r) *Small Compline. [Fol. 169r]*

Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this night: *it is written at Matins, (see fol. 73).*[^492] I believe in one God: *it is written at the Midnight Office, (see fol. 14).*[^493] *After this, the Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, (see fol. 144).*[^494] *Then after the Canon:* It is truly meet to bless thee: *(see fol. 74).*[^495] *Trisagion.* All-Holy Trinity: *After Our Father: the Troparion of the day, and of the saint, and of the temple. But if it be a temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, say that of the temple first. Then these Troparia, Tone 4:*

*Troparion:* O God of our fathers, Who always dealest with us: *(see fol. 85).*[^496]

*Another Troparion, the same Tone:*

As with purple and fine linen, Thy Church, O Lord, is adorned with the blood of Thy martyrs in all the world; through them (cont. on fol. 169v) *Small Compline*

she cries unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people, and grant peace unto Thy city, and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory:* With the saints give rest, O Christ: *(see fol. 24).*[^497]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Through the prayers, O Lord, of all: *(see fol. 96).*[^498]

*If, however, it be Friday evening, we say this Troparion, Tone 2:*

O holy Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, venerable and righteous ones, who have finished the course well and kept the faith: having boldness toward the Savior, we pray you, make supplication unto Him on our behalf, as He is good, that He may save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Kontakion, Tone 8:*

As the first-fruits of nature unto the Creator of creation, the inhabited world offers unto Thee, O Lord, the God-bearing martyrs; through their prayers, in deep peace, (cont. on fol. 170r) *Small Compline. [Fol. 170r]*

preserve Thy Church and Thy city for the sake of the Theotokos, O Thou Who alone art greatly merciful.

*If, however, it be Saturday evening, say the Hypakoe of the Tone. But if it be a Feast of the Lord or of a renowned saint, we say his Kontakion. Lord, have mercy (40). Thou Who at every time: (see fol. 21).*[^499] *Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now...* More honorable than the Cherubim: *In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.* **Priest:** *Verse:* Through the prayers of our holy fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, [have mercy on us].

*Then this Prayer:* Spotless, undefiled: *(see fol. 162).*[^500] *And the other Prayer:* And grant unto us, O Master, as we depart unto sleep:

*And after the prayer, instead of "Most glorious Ever-Virgin," we say these Troparia, Tone 6: Have mercy on us (cont. on fol. 170v) Small Compline*

on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: *(see fol. 74).*[^501]

*Then the Priest, having come before the Holy Doors, taking his epitrachelion, says:*[^502]

> **Priest:** "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee: give ear and have mercy."

> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy (3)."

> **Priest:** "Again we pray that this place, and this holy monastery, and every city and country may be preserved from famine, pestilence, earthquake, flood, fire, the sword, the invasion of foreign enemies, and civil strife; and that our good and man-befriending God may be merciful, propitious, and easily entreated concerning our sins, and turn away all His righteous wrath that moves (cont. on fol. 171r) *Small Compline. [Fol. 171r]*"

against us, and have mercy on us.

*And if there be a drought, say this also:*

> **Priest:** "And that He may send down rain upon the earth, good in its goodness: let us all say:"

> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy (40)."

*Exclamation:*

> **Priest:** "Give ear unto us, O God our Savior, the hope of all the ends of the earth and of them that are far off upon the sea; and be merciful, be merciful unto us concerning our sins, and have mercy on us; for Thou art a merciful and man-befriending God, and unto Thee do we send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."

> **Reader:** "Amen. *Glory... Both now...* Lord, have mercy *(thrice).* Bless, Father."

*Dismissal. On Sunday, after the Trinitarian Canon:* He Who rose from the dead: (cont. on fol. 171v) *Small Compline*

*And if it be on another day, say thus:* Christ our true God, through the prayers of Thy most pure [Mother]:

*And thus he puts off the epitrachelion, and turning toward the people, makes the forgiveness, as written in Great Compline, (see fol. 165),*[^503] *except for the fast-rubrics indicated there. In this manner, chanting the troparia: "Have mercy on us, O Lord," and the litany, some conclude on Sunday after the Trinitarian Canon, and on every day after every Compline and Midnight Office. After this, if thou wishest, also the Prayer to the Most Holy Trinity, written at Sunday Midnight Office, (see fol. 41).*[^504] *The Pomianyk [diptychs] is written in the Midnight Office, (see fol. 26);*[^505] *there also is: "Them that hate and wrong us, forgive them."*

---

# TROPARIA AND KONTAKIA: RESURRECTIONAL TROPARIA, THEOTOKIA, AND HYPAKOAI OF THE EIGHT TONES [^506]

*Tone 1. Troparion:*

While the stone was sealed by the Jews and soldiers were guarding Thy most pure body, Thou didst arise on the third day, O Savior, granting life unto the world. Therefore the heavenly powers cried aloud unto Thee, (cont. on fol. 172v) *Resurrectional Troparia*

O Giver of life: Glory to Thy resurrection, O Christ! Glory to Thy kingdom! Glory to Thy dispensation, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*

When Gabriel announced unto thee: "Rejoice, O Virgin!" together with the voice the Master of all became incarnate in thee, the holy ark, as the righteous David said; thou wast shown to be more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who has freed us through thy childbearing!

*These Troparia Are Chanted on Sunday After the Blameless (Kathisma 17), Tone 5. Refrain:*[^507]

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.*

*Troparia:* (cont. on fol. 173r) *Resurrectional Evlogetaria. [Fol. 173r]*

The assembly of the Angels was amazed, beholding Thee numbered among the dead; yet Thou, O Savior, destroying the power of death, didst raise up Adam with Thyself, and free all from Hades.

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.*

Why do ye mingle the myrrh with tears of pity, O disciples? the radiant Angel within the tomb spoke unto the myrrh-bearing women: Behold the tomb and understand; for the Savior has risen from the tomb.

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.*

Very early in the morning the myrrh-bearing women ran unto Thy tomb, O Savior, lamenting; but the Angel stood before them and said: The time for lamentation has ceased, weep not; but announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles.

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.* (cont. on fol. 173v) *Resurrectional Evlogetaria*

The myrrh-bearing women, having come with myrrh unto Thy tomb, O Savior, lamenting, heard the Angel speaking unto them: Why do ye reckon the Living One with the dead? For as God, He has risen from the tomb!

*Glory:*

Let us worship the Father, and also His Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Holy Trinity in one essence, crying aloud with the Seraphim: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now:*

Having borne the Giver of life, O Virgin, thou didst deliver Adam from sin, and didst grant joy unto Eve in place of sorrow; and He Who became incarnate from thee, God and Man, directs him that had fallen away back unto life.

Alleluia, Alleluia, [Alleluia,] glory to Thee, O God! *(thrice).*

*Hypakoe:* (cont. on fol. 174r) *Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 174r]*

*Tone 1. Hypakoe:*

The repentance of the thief opened Paradise, and the weeping of the myrrh-bearing women proclaimed joy: that the Lord has risen, granting great mercy unto the world.

*This Troparion Is Said Before Psalm 50 on All Sundays, Unless the Typikon Commands Otherwise:*[^508]

Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the holy Lord Jesus, the only sinless One. We worship Thy Cross, O Christ, and Thy holy Resurrection do we hymn and glorify; for Thou art our God, for beside Thee we know no other, and we call upon Thy name. Come, all ye faithful, (cont. on fol. 174v) *Resurrectional Hymns*

let us worship the holy Resurrection of Christ; for behold, through the Cross joy has come into all the world. Ever blessing the Lord, we hymn His Resurrection; for having endured crucifixion, He has destroyed death by death.

*After Psalm 50. Glory:*

Through the prayers of the holy Apostles, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our offenses.

*Both now:*

Through the prayers of the most pure Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our offenses.

*Then:*

Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy; and according to the multitude of Thy compassions, blot out my transgressions, O God my Savior.

*After this, Resurrectional Sticheron, Tone 6:*

Jesus, having risen from the tomb as He foretold, has granted unto us (cont. on fol. 175r) *Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 175r]*

eternal life, peace, and great mercy.

*Theotokion, Chanted on Sunday Before the Great Doxology, and Whenever the Typikon Commands:*[^509]

*Glory: Evangelical Sticheron, or another according to the Typikon. Both now: Tone 2:*

Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos; for through Him Who became incarnate from thee, Hades has been led captive, Adam recalled, the curse abolished, Eve set free, death put to death, and we have been made alive. Therefore, hymning, we cry aloud: Blessed is Christ our God Who has been well pleased thus!

*These Two Resurrectional Troparia Are Said After the Great Doxology at the Trisagion on All Sundays. When in the Octoechos it is (cont. on fol. 175v)*[^510] *Resurrectional Dismissal Troparia*

*Tone 1, 3, 5, or 7 in the Octoechos, we chant:*

Today salvation is come unto the world: let us hymn Him Who has risen from the tomb, the Author of our life, Christ our God; for having destroyed death, He gave us the victory and great mercy.

*And when it is Tone 2, 4, 6, or 8, we say:*

Having risen from the tomb and burst the bonds asunder, Thou didst destroy the condemnation of death, O Lord, delivering all from the snares of the enemy; and showing Thyself unto Thine Apostles, Thou didst send them forth to preach, and through them hast granted Thy peace unto the inhabited world, O Thou Who alone art greatly merciful.

*Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 176r]*

*Tone 2. Troparion:*[^511]

When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal, then didst Thou slay Hades with the radiance of Thy Divinity; and when Thou didst also raise the dead from the nethermost depths, all the heavenly hosts cried aloud unto Thee: O Giver of life, Christ our God, glory to Thee!

*Theotokion:*

All beyond comprehension, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos! Sealed in purity and guarded in virginity, thou wast acknowledged to be a true Mother, having borne the true God: make supplication unto Him that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 2. Hypakoe:*

Going after Thy Passion unto the tomb to anoint Thy body, the women, O Christ God, beheld Angels in the tomb and were terrified; for they heard a voice (cont. on fol. 176v) *Resurrectional Hymns*

from them, that the Lord has risen, granting great mercy unto the world.

*Tone 3. Troparion:*[^512]

Let the heavens rejoice, let the earth be glad; for the Lord has shown strength with His arm, trampling death by death; He became the firstborn of the dead; He has delivered us from the belly of Hades, and grants great mercy unto the world.

*Theotokion:*

We hymn thee, who didst intercede for the salvation of our race, O Virgin Theotokos; for Thy Son and our God, having accepted suffering through the Cross in the flesh which He took from thee, has delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 3. Hypakoe:*

Astonishing by his appearance, refreshing with his words, (cont. on fol. 177r) *Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 177r]*

the radiant Angel spoke unto the myrrh-bearing women: Why do ye seek the Living One in the tomb? He has emptied the graves by His rising! Know Him as the Transformer of corruption, the Changeless One; say unto God: How terrible are Thy works! For Thou hast saved the human race.

*Tone 4. Troparion:*[^513]

Having learned the radiant proclamation of the Resurrection from the Angel, and cast off the ancestral condemnation, the women disciples of the Lord exultantly said unto the Apostles: Death is overthrown! Christ God has risen, granting great mercy unto the world!

*Theotokion:*

The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to Angels was revealed through thee, O Theotokos, unto them that are upon the earth: God becoming incarnate in unconfused union, (cont. in cohort 21) *Resurrectional Hymns*

and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby He raised up the first-fashioned and saved our souls from death.

*Tone 4. Hypakoe:*

Preceding Thy most glorious Resurrection, the myrrh-bearing women ran and proclaimed the good tidings unto the Apostles: that Christ is risen as God, granting great mercy unto the world.

*Tone 5. Troparion:*[^514]

Let us, the faithful, hymn and worship the Word, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, Who was born of the Virgin for our salvation; for He was well pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, and to endure death, and to raise the dead by His glorious Resurrection.

*Theotokion:*

Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, (cont. on fol. 178r) *Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 178r]*

wall and protection of them that flee unto thee! Rejoice, unstormy haven and unwedded Bride, who in the flesh didst bear Thy Creator and God! Despise not Thy supplication for them that hymn and worship Thy childbearing.

*Tone 5. Hypakoe:*

Perplexed in mind by the angelic appearance, but enlightened in soul by the divine Resurrection, the myrrh-bearing women proclaimed the good tidings unto the Apostles: Declare among the nations the Resurrection, the Lord working together with you by miracles, and granting great mercy unto us.

*Tone 6. Troparion:*[^515]

The angelic powers were upon Thy tomb, and they that guarded it became as dead men; and Mary stood (cont. on fol. 178v) *Resurrectional Hymns*

at the tomb, seeking Thy most pure body. Thou didst despoil Hades, being not tempted by it; Thou didst meet the Virgin, granting life. O Thou Who art risen from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Theotokion:*

He that called thy blessed one His Mother came unto His Passion by His own will, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the Angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost coin is found! O Thou Who hast ordered all things in wisdom, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 6. Hypakoe:*

By Thy voluntary and life-creating death, O Christ God, having shattered the gates of Hades as God, Thou hast opened ancient Paradise unto us; and having risen from the dead, Thou hast delivered our life from corruption.

*Resurrectional Hymns. [Fol. 179r]*

*Tone 7. Troparion:*[^516]

Thou didst destroy death by Thy Cross; Thou didst open Paradise unto the thief; Thou didst transform the weeping of the myrrh-bearing women, and didst command the Apostles to preach: that Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world.

*Theotokion:*

As the treasury of our resurrection, O all-hymned one, lead up from the pit and the depth of transgressions them that hope in thee; for thou hast saved them that were guilty of sin, having given birth to our Salvation: thou who before childbearing wast a Virgin, and in childbearing a Virgin, and after childbearing again a Virgin.

*Tone 7. Hypakoe:*

O Thou Who didst take our form, and didst endure crucifixion in the flesh, save me by Thy Resurrection, O Christ God, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Resurrectional Hymns*

*Tone 8. Troparion:*[^517]

From on high Thou didst descend, O Compassionate One, and didst accept three days' burial, that Thou mightest free us from the passions. O our Life and Resurrection, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Theotokion:*

He Who for our sake was born of the Virgin: (written in the Sixth Hour, see fol. 121).[^518]

*Tone 8. Hypakoe:*

The myrrh-bearing women, standing before the life-giving tomb, sought the Immortal Master among the dead; and having received the joy of the good tidings from the Angel, they declared unto the Apostles: Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world!

## DAILY TROPARIA, THEOTOKIA, AND KONTAKIA OF THE WEEK[^519]

*Daily Hymns. [Fol. 180r]*[^520]

*On Monday: To the Angels. Tone 4. Troparion:*[^521]

Supreme Commanders of the heavenly hosts, we, the unworthy, beseech you now that by your prayers ye may compass us about beneath the shelter of the wings of your immaterial glory, guarding us who fall down and cry out earnestly: (cont. on fol. 180v) *Daily Hymns*

and cry aloud: Deliver us from dangers, as ye are the commanders of the hosts on high.

*Theotokion:*

As thou art superior to all rulers, O Theotokos, we who are at a loss to hymn thee worthily beseech thee freely: have mercy on us!

*Tone 2. Kontakion:*

Supreme Commanders of God, ministers of divine glory, guides of angels and instructors of men: ask for that which is profitable unto us, and great mercy, as ye are supreme commanders of the bodiless hosts.

*On Tuesday: To the Forerunner. Tone 2. Troparion:*[^522]

The memory of the righteous is celebrated with hymns of praise, but the testimony of the Lord suffices for thee, O Forerunner; for thou wast shown forth in truth as the most honorable of the prophets, (cont. on fol. 181r) *Daily Hymns. [Fol. 181r]*

in that thou wast vouchsafed to baptize in the streams Him Whom thou didst preach. Therefore, having suffered for the truth with joy, thou didst proclaim the good tidings even unto them that were in Hades: of God Who appeared in the flesh, Who takes away the sins of the world and grants great mercy unto us.

*Theotokion:*

Having become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Theotokos and Ever-Virgin, for thou didst bear God incarnate unto us, we all therefore worthily magnify thee with piety.

*Tone 3. Kontakion:*

Today the barren woman gives birth to the Forerunner of Christ, and he is the fulfillment of all prophecy; for having laid his hand in the Jordan upon Him Whom the prophets foretold, he was shown forth (cont. on fol. 181v) *Daily Hymns*

as the prophet, preacher, and forerunner of God the Word.

*On Wednesday: To the Cross. Tone 1. Troparion:*[^523]

Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance...

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Having acquired thy protection, O Most Pure One, we are delivered from dangers by thy prayers; for being preserved everywhere and at all times by the Cross of thy Son, we all worthily magnify thee with piety.

*Tone 4. Kontakion:*

O Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross of Thine own will: (written with the Troparion, at Matins, see fol. 45).[^524]

*On Thursday: To the Apostles. Tone 3. Troparion:*[^525]

Holy Apostles, entreat the merciful God, that (cont. on fol. 182r) *Daily Hymns. [Fol. 182r]*

He grant remission of sins unto our souls.

*Theotokion:*

Knowing Christ our God, the Word of the Father, Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Theotokos, pray unto Him to save our souls.

*To Saint Nicholas. Tone 4. Troparion:*[^526]

A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of temperance did the reality of things show thee forth unto thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire by humility the lofty, and by poverty riches: O our father, Hierarch Nicholas, entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*To the Apostles. Tone 2. Kontakion:*

The steadfast and divinely-speaking preachers, the summit of Thy disciples, O Lord, Thou didst accept (cont. on fol. 182v) *Daily Hymns*

into the enjoyment of Thy good things and rest; for their labors and death didst Thou accept beyond every whole burnt offering, O Thou Who alone knowest the secrets of the heart.

*To Saint Nicholas. Tone 3. Kontakion:*

In Myra, O holy Nicholas, thou wast shown forth as a hierarch; for having fulfilled the Gospel of Christ, O venerable one, thou didst lay down thy life for thy people and didst save the innocent from death. Therefore thou wast sanctified as a great initiate of the mystery of the grace of God.

*On Friday: The Troparion and Kontakion are the same as on Wednesday: (written on fol. 181).*[^527]

*On Saturday: To All Saints. Tone 2. Troparion:*[^528]

Apostles, prophets, and martyrs, hierarchs, venerable and righteous: (written on fol. 107).

*Daily Hymns. [Fol. 183r]*

*For the Departed. Tone 2. Troparion:*

Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, Thy servants: (see fol. 23).

*Theotokion:*

O Holy Mother of the most pure Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we praise and magnify thee.

*For the Departed. Tone 8. Troparion:*

O Thou Who by the depth of Thy wisdom: (see fol. 23).

*Theotokion:*

We have thee as a wall and a haven, and an acceptable intercessor before God Who was born of thee, O unwedded Theotokos, salvation of the faithful.

*To All Saints. Tone 8. Kontakion:*

As the first-fruits of nature: (see fol. 107).

*For the Departed. Kontakion:*

With the Saints give rest, O Christ, to the souls of Thy servants: (written in the Midnight Office, see fol. 24).

## THEOTOKIA AND STAVROTHEOTOKIA OF THE EIGHT TONES[^529]

*Theotokia and Stavrotheotokia. [Fol. 183v]*

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*[^530]

Receive the prayers of thy servants, O Theotokos, and deliver us from every distress, as thou didst bear Christ the Savior, the Redeemer of our souls.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Having acquired thy protection: (written on Wednesday, see fol. 181).

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*

O Holy Mother: (written on Saturday, see fol. 183).

*Stavrotheotokion:*

We magnify thee, O Theotokos, crying aloud: Rejoice, O rod from which without seed thou didst sprout forth God, (cont. on fol. 184r) *Stavrotheotokia. [Fol. 184r]*

Who by the Tree destroyed death.

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*[^531]

Wheresoever each one is saved, thither he rightly flees; and what other haven is there like unto thine, O Theotokos, which covers our souls?

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Having acquired the rod of strength, the Cross of thy Son, O Theotokos, thereby we cast down the insolence of enemies, that we may unceasingly magnify thee.

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*

An unconquerable rampart unto us: (written in the Inter-Hour of the Third Hour, see fol. 85).

*Stavrotheotokion:*

O Virgin all-immaculate, Mother of Christ God: a sword pierced thy most pure soul when (cont. on fol. 184v) *Theotokia and Stavrotheotokia*

thou didst behold thy Son and God crucified of His own will; entreat Him unceasingly, O most blessed one, that He may grant remission of our transgressions.

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*[^532]

O Christ God, Who didst shine forth from the Virgin unto the whole world, and on her account didst show us forth as sons of the light: have mercy on us.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

By the Cross of thy Son, O woman full of the grace of God, the deceit of idols was abolished, and the power of demons was trampled down. Therefore we, the faithful, as is due, ever hymn and bless thee, and confessing thee to be in truth the Theotokos, we magnify thee.

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*[^533]

According to thy word, O true Mother: behold, all generations, blessing thee, humbly magnify thee.

*Stavrotheotokia. [Fol. 185r]*

*Stavrotheotokion:*

O Virgin Theotokos, entreat thy Son, Who was voluntarily nailed unto the Cross and freed the world from deceit, Christ our God, that He may have mercy on our souls.

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*[^534]

Though the King knew thee as Mother, yet the Creator showed thee forth as Virgin; for thou didst bear in the flesh Christ our God, Who saves our souls.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

O pure Ever-Virgin Theotokos, beholding thy Son hanging upon the Cross, weeping as a Mother, thou didst magnify His strange descent.

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*

Rejoice, thou who from the Angel didst receive the joy (cont. on fol. 185v) *Theotokia and Stavrotheotokia*

didst receive the joy for the world; rejoice, thou who didst bear the Creator of creation; rejoice, thou who wast vouchsafed to become the Mother of Christ God.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The Lamb, and Shepherd, and Savior of the world, upon the Cross: (written in the Ninth Hour, see fol. 113).

*End of the Theotokia and Stavrotheotokia, and of the Whole Horologion.*[^535]

## FEAST OF THE DORMITION OF THE MOST HOLY THEOTOKOS[^536]

*Month of August. [Fol. 186r]*

*On the 15th Day of the Month of August: The Dormition of our Most Holy Lady Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary. Tone 1. Troparion:*

In thy childbearing thou didst preserve virginity, and in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Theotokos; thou didst pass over unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thine intercessions deliverest our souls from death.

*Tone 2. Kontakion:*

Neither the tomb nor death could hold the Theotokos, who is sleepless in supplications and an unfailing hope in intercessions; for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

---

# TROPARIA AND KONTAKIA TO THE RUS' SAINTS [^537]

*Troparia and Kontakia to the Rus' Saints. [Fol. 186v]*

*On the 10th Day of the Month of July: Commemoration of our Venerable Father Anthony of the Caves. Tone 4. Troparion:*[^538]

Departing from the turmoil of the world, thou didst reach the quiet haven of the Holy Mountain of Athos; and renouncing the world, according to the Gospel thou didst follow Christ, having lived an angel-like life. And thence, by the blessing of the Fathers, coming unto this mountain, having laboriously in the Caves completed (cont. on fol. 187r) *Troparia and Kontakia to the Rus' Saints. [Fol. 187r]*

thy life, thou didst show forth the path unto the Heavenly Kingdom for a multitude of monastics, and didst bring them unto Christ; entreat Him, O venerable father, that He may save our souls.

*Tone 8. Kontakion:*

Committing thyself unto God from thy youth, O venerable father, loving Him above all things, and following Him with love from thy whole soul, counting corruptible earthly things as nothing, thou didst fashion for thyself a cave in the earth; wherein having striven well against the wiles of the invisible foe, thou didst shine forth like the radiant sun unto the ends of the earth. Whence rejoicing thou didst pass over unto the heavenly mansions, and now standing with the Angels before the eternal (cont. in cohort 22) *Kontakion continuation from previous cohort:*
remember us who honor thy memory, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Antony, our father!

*May 3:*
*Commemoration of our venerable father Theodosius of the Caves:*[^539]

*Tone 8. Troparion:*
Having ascended unto virtue from youth, thou didst love the monastic life, and having valiantly attained thy desire, thou didst settle in a cave; and having adorned thy life with fasting and radiance, abiding in prayers as though bodiless, thou didst shine forth like a radiant beacon in the land of Rus', O father Theodosius: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 3. Kontakion:*
*(Catchword: The star) Continuation of Kontakion:*
Today let us honor the star of Rus', which shone forth from the east and came unto the west; for he enriched all this land with wonders and goodness, and all of us by the diligent labor and grace of the monastic rule, the blessed Theodosius.

*July 11:*
*Commemoration of the venerable Olga, in Holy Baptism Helen, Princess of Rus':*[^540]

*Tone 1. Troparion:*
Having feathered thy mind with the wings of divine understanding, thou didst soar above visible creation, seeking after God and the Creator of all things; and having found Him, thou didst receive rebirth in baptism, delighting in the Tree of Life, *Continuation of Troparion:*
abiding incorrupt unto ages, O Helen ever-renowned.

*Tone 4. Kontakion:*
Today let us hymn the Benefactor of all, God, Who glorified in Rus' Helen the divinely wise; by her prayers, O Christ, grant remission of sins unto our souls.

*July 15:*
*Commemoration of the right-believing Prince Vladimir:*[^541]

*Tone 4. Troparion:*
Having likened thyself unto a merchant seeking goodly pearls, O gloriously ruling Vladimir, sitting upon the height of the throne of the Mother of Cities, God-saved Kyiv,[^542]
*(Catchword: Kyiv) July 24:*
*Commemoration of the Holy Passion-Bearers Roman and David (Boris and Gleb):*[^543]

*Tone 3. Kontakion:*
Today your glorious memory has shone forth, O noble passion-bearers of Christ, Roman and David, summoning us unto the praise of Christ our God; therefore, fleeing unto the shrine of your relics, we receive the gift of healing through your prayers, O holy ones; for ye are divine physicians.

*(Tailpiece: Medallion woodcut ornament)*  
*(Catchword: Table of Contents)*

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: Physical front cover: Contemporary early seventeenth-century brown calfskin binding over wooden boards, adorned with blind-tooled ornamental borders and a central floral rhombus medallion. At the top left of the upper cover is affixed the printed paper shelfmark label of the Main Library of the Holy Trinity–Saint Sergius Lavra (*Свято-Троицкой Сергіевой Лавры Главная Библіотека*), with nineteenth-century inventory numbers entered by hand.

[^2]: Inside front board pastedown: Handmade laid paper pastedown containing historical library shelfmarks in ink and Russian bibliographical dating notes in pencil (*Киев 1616 / 1617*), referencing the printing dates of the Pechersk Lavra Horologion.

[^3]: Provenance inscriptions on the preliminary flyleaf verso detail the bibliographic history of this copy: "Book very rare — firstling of the Kyiv Printing House, published in the year 1616 (see the end of the Epilogue of the Horologion). Lacks leaves 1 and 189." The note indicates that this copy lacks the title leaf (fol. [1]) and final leaf 189. Foliation starts on the facing recto with printed signature *а* as leaf 1.

[^4]: Archimandrite Eliseus (Yelysei) Pletenetsky (c. 1550–1624), Archimandrite of the Kyiv Caves Lavra (1599–1624), founder of the Kyiv Caves Lavra Printing House (1615), and a foremost champion of Orthodox cultural and theological renewal in Ukraine.

[^5]: Church Slavonic: *Кї́евскїй* (Kyivan / of Kyiv). In Early Modern Ruthenian publications, the Caves Lavra is consistently titled *Лавра Печерская Кіевская*.

[^6]: Church Slavonic: *Правосла́вному Ро́дꙋ Рѡссі́йскомꙋ* (unto the Orthodox Rus' / Russian / Ruthenian race/people). Here *Ро́дъ Рѡссі́йскїй* denotes the Orthodox Ruthenian/East Slavic people of the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth under the Kyivan metropolitanate.

[^7]: Greek loanword: *ѠРОЛОГІ́ОНЪ* (*Horologion*, the Book of Hours). Archimandrite Eliseus Pletenetsky characterizes the 1616/1617 Horologion as the firstling and forerunner (*предитеча*) of the monumental publishing program of the Kyiv Caves printing press.

[^8]: 2 Chronicles (2 Paralipomenon) 32:20–21.

[^9]: 2 Chronicles (2 Paralipomenon) 33:12–13; cf. the Prayer of Manasseh.

[^10]: Sextus Julius Africanus (c. 160 – c. 240 AD), early Christian chronicler, whose *Chronographiae* preserve traditions regarding the repentance and miraculous release of King Manasseh from his bronze and iron fetters in Babylon.

[^11]: Greek loanwords: *Маке́лла* (*macellum*, meat market / shambles); *Ѻ҆́рганъ* (*organon*, instrument / tool).

[^12]: *Apophthegmata Patrum* (Sayings of the Desert Fathers), Agathon 9: Abba Agathon taught that of all spiritual struggles, prayer requires warfare and labor unto the very last breath.

[^13]: A reference to the Kyiv Epiphany Brotherhood School (*Братська школа*), founded around 1615 on land donated by Halshka Hulevychivna, for which Archimandrite Eliseus Pletenetsky and the Kyiv Caves Lavra printed this Horologion as an essential primer and liturgical text.

[^14]: *Предосло́віе Часосло́ва*: The theological and ecclesiological Foreword written by the scholar, theologian, and future archimandrite Zacharias Kopystensky (d. 1627). It sets forth the Ruthenian Orthodox understanding of Jerusalem as the Mother Church and liturgical archetype, defended against both Roman Catholic and Protestant claims.

[^15]: Septuagint citation of Isaiah 66:23: *Прїи́детъ всѧ́ка пло́ть, поклони́тисѧ предомно́ю в̾ І҆ерл҃имѣ́, речѐ Гд҃ь* ("All flesh shall come to worship before Me in Jerusalem, says the Lord"). The printed margin marks *И҆са́. ѯ҃ѕ*.

[^16]: Honorific title: *Кѵ́ръ* (Greek *Kyr*, Lord/Master), standard liturgical and diplomatic styling for hierarchs and archimandrites.

[^17]: Reference to the Jerusalem Typikon of the Lavra of Saint Sabas the Sanctified (Mar Saba), which governed the liturgical cycles of the Ruthenian Church.

[^18]: Genesis 2:8.

[^19]: Numbers 2:3: The camp of Judah was commanded to pitch toward the sunrise on the east side of the Tabernacle.

[^20]: Baruch 3:38 (3:37 in Greek/LXX): "Afterward did He show Himself upon earth, and conversed with men."

[^21]: Isaiah 2:3.

[^22]: Luke 24:47.

[^23]: Hermias Sozomen, *Historia Ecclesiastica*, Book 3, Chapter 20 (as cited in the printed marginal rubric *Кни: г. за: к.*), recording that the orthodox Christians of Jerusalem and their bishop remained untainted by Arianism.

[^24]: Psalm 86:5 (LXX): *Ма́ти Сїѡ́нъ рече́тъ чл҃къ, и҆ чл҃къ роди́ся в̾не́й, и҆ то́й ѡ҆снова̀ ю̀ вы́шнїи* (MT Psalm 87:5).

[^25]: Isaiah 1:26.

[^26]: St. John of Damascus, Sunday Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 4 (Octoechos), as cited in the margin (*Ѡ҆кт: гл҃: д҃. Стї: въскр҃снꙋ*).

[^27]: The Synodical Letter of the Second Ecumenical Council of Constantinople (381 AD), preserved in Theodoret of Cyrus, *Historia Ecclesiastica*, Book 5, Chapter 9 (*Ѳеѡдо́ кни: є҃ з: ѳ҃*), which addresses the Church of Jerusalem as "the Mother of all Churches" (*τῆς γε μητρὸς ἁπασῶν τῶν ἐκκλησιῶν*).

[^28]: The Synodical Letter of Constantinople (381 AD) confirms Cyril of Jerusalem (*Кѵрі́лла є҆ппа*) as bishop of Jerusalem, declaring Jerusalem to be the Mother of all Churches.

[^29]: Church Slavonic: *Кї́евски бре́гѡ доспѣ́лъ* (reached the Kyivan banks/hills). This recounts the foundational Kyivan transmission narrative from the *Primary Chronicle* (*Tale of Bygone Years*), wherein the Holy Apostle Andrew the First-Called erects a Cross on the hills of Kyiv and prophesies that great churches and divine grace shall shine forth there. The marginal note cites Symeon Metaphrastes (*Сѵмеѡ́: Мета: Фев: к҃д*).

[^30]: Allusion to Ephesians 2:2: "the prince of the power of the air" (*въз̾доу́шны непрїѧ́зни*, the aerial adverse spirits / demons).

[^31]: Saint Vladimir the Great, Equal-to-the-Apostles, baptizer of Rus' (988 AD), analogized to Saint Constantine the Great. The marginal note cites 1 John 2:8 (*І҆оан: в҃*, "because the darkness is past, and the true light now shines").

[^121]: Pastoral valediction to the Orthodox Christian reader.
